UNIVERSAL SPORTS DECISION-SUPPORT CORE - ADDED 2026-05-27 FROM COMPREHENSIVE UPDATE PLAN

This core sits above every sport-specific section in this file. Do not remove the existing AFL, MLB, cricket, basketball, NFL, NHL, NRL, soccer, or other sport modules. Treat those modules as sport annexes that inherit this universal core. If a sport module conflicts with this core, follow the stricter rule or explicitly state the conflict before analysis.

PURPOSE:
- Turn the sports document from a long instruction manual into a controlled decision-support system.
- Preserve the existing sport-specific expertise while adding common governance, source, metric, validation, provenance, privacy, model-risk, and maintenance controls.
- Make every prediction, retrospective, model output, dashboard, and research note reproducible, auditable, and clearly tied to the data cutoff.

SCOPE:
- Covers pregame research, live reassessment, post-event settlement, retrospective learning, sport-specific analysis, source quality, metrics, dashboards, model governance, privacy/ethics, and maintenance.
- Does not authorize blind betting, automated adverse athlete decisions, medical diagnosis, integrity accusations, or use of private player health/workload data without a lawful basis and proper access controls.
- Default framework mode allows betting lines/odds as supporting context only. If the user explicitly requests no odds or no market data for a specific query, odds, sportsbooks, market movement, and betting-preview pages must be omitted for that query.
- HARD RULE - BETTING/PREVIEW SITE NON-CONTROL (reinforced by user directive 2026-07-09): final analysis and rankings must never be based primarily on betting-preview pages, sportsbook writeups, Covers-style pages, tipster pages, odds screens, or market commentary. Those sources may be used only as secondary context, sanity checks, or discovery leads. The actual decision must be built from the workflow inputs in this document: official/current event state, ruleset and settlement convention, confirmed availability and lineups, team/player/unit stats and L5/L10/L15/L20 form versus baselines, matchup mechanics, venue/surface/weather, travel/rest/motivation, press/news signals, sport-specific checkpoints, model output, and retrospective lessons. If a betting or preview source is opened, the card must state which non-betting evidence actually controlled the pick.

CANONICAL HARD DATA RULES - CONSOLIDATED 2026-07-11 (controls all later duplicate wording):
- HARD RULE A - STATMUSE FIRST FOR AMERICAN LEAGUES: for every U.S./North American league StatMuse covers, including NBA, WNBA, MLB, NFL, NHL, MLS, CFB, CBB, PGA and comparable leagues, StatMuse is the mandatory first statistical reference for every supported decision-driving team, player, unit, phase, H2H and home/away split. Validate the answer sentence, competition/season, qualifier and newest table date. If blocked, stale, unsupported, incomplete or misparsed, write the exact StatMuse status/query and the fallback used. Official league/team sources still control live state, availability, lineups, starters, conditions, rules and settlement.
- HARD RULE B - FOUR WINDOWS FOR EVERY AVAILABLE ASPECT: for every decision-driving statistic in every sport, collect L5, L10, L15 and L20 for both teams and every available player or unit that can materially affect the listed markets, as far as reliable observations exist. Cover scoring and allowed scoring, phases, pace/opportunity, shooting/conversion, defence, venue/home-away, opponent tier, H2H and prop-specific production where applicable. Compare with the individual L5 results, season/year and historical/career or same-level baselines, and name the form anchor.
- If fewer than 20 observations exist, use all available observations and label the actual sample N. Every unavailable aspect is `MISSING` with a reason, never silently estimated. `PARTIAL`/`MISSING` decision-driving coverage caps confidence and forbids the word `comprehensive` unless the missing field is irrelevant to every ranked market.
- Canonical compact matrix: `STAT | L5 | L10 | L15 | L20 | SEASON/YEAR | HIST/CAREER | HOME/AWAY/H2H | VERDICT`. Section 13.10 contains the operational detail; later StatMuse/rolling-window paragraphs are archived reminders, not separate rules.

ACTIVE NON-NEGOTIABLE CHECKLIST - CLEANED 2026-06-27:
Use this checklist before every serious card, regardless of sport. The sport-specific module then translates each item into that sport's mechanics.
1. Read this Universal Core, the active upgrades, the relevant sport module, and the latest same-sport log/ledger lessons before ranking.
2. Verify fixture identity, competition, ruleset, settlement convention, venue, local start time, Australia/Sydney conversion, and current game state.
3. Use official/current sources first, record source freshness and access state, and mark unavailable facts as UNKNOWN rather than guessing.
4. Apply Canonical Hard Data Rules A-B and complete the coverage matrix before ranking.
5. Run the press-conference/team-representative playstyle gate: current pre-match coach/player/official comments, classified as tactical, availability, motivation, PR-noise, or UNKNOWN.
6. Confirm availability, lineups/team sheets/Playing XI/final 17/inactives/goalies/starters at the sport's release window; classify projected or limited roles explicitly.
7. Check venue, weather/roof/surface/pitch/ground, officials/referees/umpires, rest/travel, motivation, and similar-condition form.
8. Write the projected score/total/margin/phase band before ranking, then count exact-market hit rates where possible.
9. Produce an independent probability table, run #1 stress test, #4 swap/failure-path test, confidence caps, volatility check, and PASS/LOW-EDGE decision when no genuine edge exists.
10. For live questions, discard stale pregame comfort and recompute from score, clock/overs/period, required remaining pace, and current game script.
11. Log the final ranking verbatim, settle only from reliable final/phase sources, and update retrospectives with what was overweighted, missed, or correctly handled.

LESSON PLACEMENT AND PRECEDENCE:
- Universal lessons apply first across all sports: source freshness, data cutoff, final-score/phase verification, availability, press-conference evidence, live-state arithmetic, fielding/defence, weather-window verification, and bottom-slot calibration.
- Sport-specific annexes then translate the universal lesson into that sport's mechanics. For example, "phase verification" means first-5/first-10 overs in cricket, Q1/1H in basketball, innings/bullpen entry in baseball, quarter splits in AFL/NRL, and 1H/corners in soccer.
- New retrospectives should not duplicate an existing universal rule. First cite or reinforce the relevant universal rule, then add only the sport-specific detail that is new.
- If a sport-specific rule conflicts with a universal rule, use the stricter or more current rule and write the conflict explicitly before ranking.

ACTIVE OPERATING INDEX - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM CUSTOM DOCUMENT REVIEW:
- This is the current entry point for every future card. Older repeated "MASTER SPORTS PREDICTION MODEL INSTRUCTIONS", "MANDATORY PRE-GAME GATES", "ANALYSIS OUTPUT STRUCTURE", and "REQUIRED OUTPUT FORMAT" blocks remain as archived context only unless they contain a sport-specific detail not repeated here or in the 2026-06-10 Comprehensive Accuracy Upgrade.
- The active order is:
  0. Run the 2026-06-25 COMPREHENSIVE SYSTEM UPGRADE first: GATE -1 mandatory reading order (all-sports governance in full, THEN the sport section in its entirety, THEN the log/ledger same-sport sweep) before opening any source; GATE -0.5 web-access/browser-like rendering ladder; GATE -0.25 StatMuse mandatory American-sports gate; GATE -0.1 recurring-mistake registry M1-M9.
  1. Read this Universal Core and Active Operating Index.
  2. Run the 2026-06-10 Comprehensive Accuracy Upgrade, especially Gates 0-10.
  3. Run the 2026-06-12 Comprehensive Blindspot Upgrade (Sections 13-18, end of file): Form Delta table + Form Anchor (Sec 13), Press-Signal line with S1-S6 classification (Sec 14), Officials line with release timings (Sec 15), no-API access chains incl. the X/Reddit/ESPN directive reconciliation (Sec 16), cross-sport stat additions (Sec 17), and the ledger/KPI schema v2 (Sec 18).
  4. Run the 2026-06-13 Comprehensive Sport Data Model and Deep Feature Upgrade (Sections 19-24, end of file): sport data architecture for soccer, cricket, AFL, baseball, and American leagues; pregame-only leakage controls; similar-condition form; role/replacement/bench modelling; style-weather/venue fit; volatility/stability; public/market/news context; data-source disagreement; and "what would change the prediction" trigger fields.
  5. Run `POISSON_DISTRIBUTION_AND_SIMULATION_FRAMEWORK.md`: complete its mandatory comprehensive framework/log/ledger sweep before every new prediction-log entry; run Poisson as the transparent count baseline where applicable; compare the required challenger and sport-native models; use at least 100,000 production simulations with a logged seed; reject raw Poisson when dispersion, dependence, tail or calibration diagnostics fail.
  6. Apply any sport-specific annex only as a translation layer.
  7. Use the current card template in PREDICTION_RESULTS_LOG_v2.md (v2 template includes the Form Delta, Press Signal, Officials, Deep Context, Source Disagreement, Trigger Conditions, and distribution/simulation fields).
  8. Update the calibration tracker after settlement (ledger v2 columns plus any deep-context and model-diagnostic verdicts).
- Canonical active gate sequence: Gate -1 mandatory reading order (all-sports governance, then sport section in full, then log/ledger sweep); Gate -0.5 web-access/browser-like rendering ladder; Gate -0.25 StatMuse mandatory for American sports; Gate -0.1 recurring-mistake registry M1-M9; Gate 0 pending sweep; Gate 1 fixture identity, format, ruleset lock, and competition variant; Gate 2 game-state/timezone; Gate 3 source freshness and official source order; Gate 4 availability plus eligibility/classification packet; Gate 5 conditions; Gate 6 recency stack plus opponent adjustment; Gate 7 projection first; Gate 8 separate side from total and settlement matrix; Gate 9 probability table plus slot tests; Gate 10 consistency and output.
- Required card fields: request/cutoff time; game state; fixture identity; ruleset lock; settlement convention/matrix; opened sources with source/timestamp/access/status tag; official source order; availability and eligibility/classification verdict; conditions/hidden factors; stat coverage matrix; recency/baseline deltas; similar-condition form; role/replacement/bench verdict; style-weather/venue fit; source-disagreement/confidence score; prediction trigger conditions; projection band by market; framework sweep; Poisson baseline where applicable; distribution diagnostics; challenger/selected model; simulations/seed; distribution percentiles and over/under/push/void probabilities; model disagreement/calibration status; probability table; #1 stress test; #4 failure-path and swap test; pass/no-edge decision; final ranking; confidence cap; log-ready row; retrospective audit cause after settlement.
- PASS / NO-EDGE STANDARD: if no option clears the #1 requirement (at least about 60% probability, fresh mechanism, stress-test pass) or the #1 vs #4 separation is under about 20 points, do not force a confident ranked answer. Output "PASS / NO GENUINE EDGE" or "LOW-EDGE FORCED RANKING" and keep #4 labelled forced when its probability is above about 30-35%.
- Calibration must be measured, not estimated. After every settlement sweep, update exact counts where possible: settled cards, listed-item W-L-P, top-pick penalties, bottom-pick penalties, top-half sweep exemptions, raw slot hit rates, rolling last-20 top hit rate, rolling last-20 bottom win rate, and probability-bucket calibration (50-59, 60-69, 70-79, 80+). Approximate counts are allowed only as temporary placeholders and must be labelled approximate.
- Source registry maintenance: every source used as a repeated dependency needs source name, sport, purpose, access state, last tested date/time, freshness limit, fallback, and next retest trigger. A source access claim older than 30 days is stale unless it is an official static rules/fixture source.
- Rule retirement and cleanup policy: every new learning must name the existing active gate it modifies. If it only repeats an existing universal rule, do not add a new paragraph. If it replaces an older rule, mark the older rule as archived/superseded rather than leaving two active instructions. Avoid adding another sport-specific rule unless the mechanism is genuinely new.
- Encoding/readability maintenance: keep this file UTF-8 readable. Replace corrupted punctuation and dead citation artifacts during cleanup passes. Headings should be readable ASCII or valid punctuation, because corrupted headings make search and review unreliable.
- Redundancy cleanup rule: do not delete settled historical retrospectives unless they are exact duplicates. Instead, move active authority into this index and the 2026-06-10 upgrade, then treat duplicated older workflow text as archived reference. This preserves evidence while stopping obsolete process text from controlling future cards.

=== 2026-06-25 COMPREHENSIVE SYSTEM UPGRADE (read at the start of EVERY card, before any research) ===
This block consolidates the recurring-mistake registry, the mandatory reading order, the browser-like web-access ladder, and the StatMuse hard gate into one enforced pre-research and pre-ranking protocol. It does not replace the gates below; it sequences them and closes the leaks the settled logs keep exposing. If any step cannot be completed, write the missing dependency on the card and cap confidence.

GATE -1: MANDATORY READING ORDER BEFORE RESEARCH (do not open a single stats source until done):
1. Read ALL-SPORTS GOVERNANCE IN FULL first: this Universal Core, the Active Operating Index, this 2026-06-25 upgrade, the RULESET-LOCKED governance, the 2026-06-10 Accuracy Upgrade (Gates 0-10), and the 2026-06-12/13 blindspot/data-model upgrades. These apply to every sport and must be loaded before sport detail.
2. Then read the SPORT-SPECIFIC SECTION IN ITS ENTIRETY: the sport's RULESET LOCK, its IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES line, its module/annex, AND every dated sport learning for that sport in the LEARNINGS sections and VALIDATED RULES REGISTRY. Do not skim - the leak is usually a sport-specific lesson already written and ignored.
3. Then sweep PREDICTION_RESULTS_LOG_v3.md and _v2.md for the most recent same-sport and same-competition retrospectives, plus the SPORTS_CALIBRATION_LEDGER. Carry forward the closest prior lesson by name.
4. Only after 1-3 may research/sourcing begin. The card must state "GATE -1 reading complete" implicitly by citing at least the sport rule lock and the nearest prior same-sport lesson.

GATE -0.5: WEB ACCESS / BROWSER-LIKE RENDERING LADDER (use to extract stats the way a browser would; never report a stat "unavailable" until the ladder is walked):
- Rung 1 - KEYLESS STRUCTURED APIs FIRST (most reliable, do not skip): ESPN site API (site.api.espn.com/.../scoreboard?dates=YYYYMMDD and /summary?event=ID for finals, line scores, corners wonCorners, injuries), MLB Stats API (statsapi.mlb.com) for game state/lineups/weather/box, and equivalent league APIs. These return clean JSON and settled the entire 2026-06-24/25 sweep.
- Rung 2 - STATSMUSE for American-sports statistical form (MANDATORY GATE, see GATE -0.25 below).
- Rung 3 - DIRECT WebFetch (markdown) for static/server-rendered pages (official league sites, nznbl.basketball, recaps, Guardian/ESPN articles).
- Rung 4 - BROWSER-LIKE RENDERING when a page is JavaScript-gated and Rung 3 returns an empty shell/roster-only/"cannot find" (Flashscore, Sofascore, 365scores, many live tables):
  a. r.jina.ai reader proxy: WebFetch https://r.jina.ai/https://<target-url> - renders the JS page to text. VALIDATED 2026-06-25: raw WebFetch of the Flashscore Otago results page returned only roster/nav, but r.jina.ai returned actual game-by-game results. CAVEAT: the proxy can serve a CACHED/older render - the 2026-06-25 test returned 2025-dated rows - so VERIFY THE DATE of every rendered row before use and re-pull from an API/official source if the dates are stale.
  b. Claude-in-Chrome browser MCP (mcp__Claude_in_Chrome__navigate / get_page_text / read_page / find): load via ToolSearch when an interactive, current render is needed and the reader proxy is stale or blocked. Requires the connected extension.
  c. computer-use MCP as the last resort for a true desktop-browser render.
- Rung 5 - DOCUMENTED FALLBACK CHAINS already in the source registry (Bluesky public API for social; official club/federation pages; aggregator cross-checks). Tag every fetched fact with source + timestamp + access state (OPENED / SNIPPET / RENDERED-PROXY / CACHED / ASSUMED). A RENDERED-PROXY or CACHED fact cannot control a #1 or #4 pick until its date is verified against an official/API source.

GATE -0.25: STATSMUSE MANDATORY FOR AMERICAN SPORTS (hard gate, reinforces the AMERICAN SPORTS STATS SOURCE PRIORITY block):
- For NBA, WNBA, MLB, NFL, NHL, MLS/FC, college FB/BB, PGA and similar U.S./North American leagues, StatMuse (statmuse.com/{league}/ask/{hyphenated-question}) is the REQUIRED FIRST statistical access point. Query L5, L10, L15 and L20 for BOTH teams and every decision-relevant available player, plus season and historical/career baselines and the market-relevant phase split (Q1/1H for basketball totals, first-5/bullpen for MLB, period splits for NHL).
- Validate every StatMuse answer: read the answer sentence, the restated timeframe, the table dates (must include the latest completed game), and whether a playoff/regular-season/venue qualifier was silently dropped. Beware the documented opponent-phase misparse (it can echo the requested team's own phase). If unsupported, stale, or misparsed, write "StatMuse MISSING/misparsed" and use the fallback chain, disclosing the replacement source. Official sources still control volatile state (injuries, lineups, starters, live score, settlement).

GATE -0.1: CONSOLIDATED RECURRING-MISTAKE REGISTRY (the "do not repeat" list - check the card against EVERY item before ranking; each is grounded in settled losses):
M1. EXACT-WINNER OVER-TRUST. The single biggest leak across the 2026-06 logs: side/winner and total are independent axes, yet exact-winner leans keep losing (Detroit over Yankees, Atlanta over Padres, Czechia, West Indies-as-winner-was-right-but-total-wrong, South Korea non-loss). FIX: cap exact-winner confidence; never rank an exact winner #1 unless the talent+availability+form edge is clean AND the win path is not coin-flip. Prefer cushions (+1.5 / +handicap) and totals over exact winners when the band is tight.
M2. NON-LOSS / DOUBLE-CHANCE OVER-RANKED. "Non-loss above exact winner" only holds when the projected band genuinely centres on the draw. A must-win side out-creating a can-draw side beats the non-loss safety (South Africa 1-0 South Korea). FIX: drop the can-draw non-loss toward a coin flip when the must-win team has the chance-creation edge.
M3. TOTALS-DIRECTION STACKING OFF ONE FACTOR. Stacking all overs or all unders off a single narrative repeatedly fails (Aces-Storm all-unders off Storm's bad offense; Golden State-Atlanta all-overs off Atlanta's L5 surge). FIX: write the projected number first (Gate 7); anchor to the COMBINED environment; when one side owns an elite/slow top-league defense, anchor the total to its opponent-PPG, not the visitor's scoring spike; keep over/under adjacent within ~1 scoring unit of the line.
M4. FULL-TOTAL BLOWOUT/OT TAIL vs PHASE UNDERS. Phase unders (Q1/1H) can win while the full total clears via a second-half blowout (Chicago 101-78 over Portland) or overtime (Southland-Whai OT 198). FIX: treat phase totals and the full total as separate axes; never rank a full under above the full over on low early-phase scoring alone when a one-sided-favourite or close-game-OT path is live.
M5. COLD-OFFENSE CUSHION. A +1.5 / +handicap cushion is not safe when the cushioned side is cold AND missing its primary scorer/bat (Braves +1.5 lost, Atlanta swept without Acuna). FIX: separate "starter/talent edge" from "can this lineup actually score"; downgrade the cushion of a star-thinned, slumping side.
M6. REPUTATION/RETURNING-ACE & PARK-SUPPRESSION NARRATIVES. A returning/reputation ace can still be taken deep; HRs beat a wind-in note (Skubal 3 HRs, Yankees 4-2). FIX: keep the opponent live when they have shown power/ceiling; do not let a park/weather suppression note override demonstrated over-the-fence paths.
M7. ELITE-CEILING vs VENUE/VARIANCE UNDER. Elite batting/scoring units clear "high" lines that venue history says are tough (Australia 199/219 WT20I, England 186 at Lord's). FIX: against elite ceilings, require same-day swing control / early wickets / weakened top order before topping an under; do not bottom a ceiling over off venue average alone.
M8. BOTTOM-SLOT HONESTY / FORCED COMPLEMENTS. Forced exact-complement bottoms keep winning when mislabelled impossible. FIX: run the #4 test (<=30%, most failure paths, swap test); if forced, label "forced bottom slot, not a dead fade" and keep its true probability.
M9. SOURCE RECENCY / RENDERED-DATA DATE. Cached/proxy/aggregator data can be old (r.jina.ai returned 2025 rows). FIX: verify the date of every rendered/aggregated row against an API/official source before it controls a pick.
- Each new retrospective must say which registry item (M1-M9) or which active gate it reinforces, and add only genuinely new sport-specific detail rather than a duplicate paragraph.

=== LEARNINGS UPDATE 2026-07-09 (pending sweep: World Cup knockout soccer) ===
- Soccer knockout corners, from Argentina 3 vs Egypt 2: a favourite can dominate possession and still fail a corners Over 7.5 when the underdog contributes only one corner. Do not rank a high-ish corner over as a strong positive leg from favourite territory alone; require either (a) the favourite to have a credible 8+ corner solo path from recent shots/crosses/blocked shots, or (b) both teams to have live corner contribution. Egypt's transition/scoring path raised goals without raising corners.
- Soccer knockout totals, from Argentina 3 vs Egypt 2 and Switzerland 0 vs Colombia 0: separate the favourite-score/winner path from the total ceiling. Under 3.5 is fragile when the written stress test already admits 3-1, 4-0, 3-2 or chase-state goals. Conversely, low-total/non-loss structures can be correct in even knockout ties while the advance/winner lean fails in extra time or penalties. Regulation non-loss, low total, corners and advance markets must be modelled as separate settlement events.

=== LEARNINGS UPDATE 2026-06-27 (pending-log cleanup: NZNBL, AFL, MLB, MLC, WNBA) ===
- NZNBL / FIBA domestic totals, from Manawatu 104 vs Otago 97: missing one major scorer and a nominal strong opposing defence are not enough to top-rank full/half unders when the direct matchup, weak defence, and H2H pace already sit in the 190-200 range. Manawatu-Otago reached 46 Q1, 102 at halftime and 201 full despite Kaden Sand being out. Before anchoring an NZNBL under, verify the current H2H phase band and whether the weaker defence can drag even a modest offence into a shootout.
- AFL low-spread side calibration, from Brisbane 126 vs Sydney 83: a +1.5 cushion is effectively a winner call, not a safety blanket. Do not protect the away/season-profile underdog above the home favourite cover when the home side has a concrete explosive scoring path, dry conditions, and a strong venue-response profile. Low-spread underdog cushions still need a clear current matchup edge, not just superior ladder/H2H reputation.
- MLB live-state run-line calibration, from Tampa Bay 13 vs Kansas City 2: in live/start-window cards, once the home favourite already leads by multiple runs and the road starter is not stabilising, demote the road +1.5 cushion below the favourite ML and over paths. A pregame starter/opener tension can be overtaken by live scoreboard pressure, traffic, and bullpen exposure; do not keep the road cushion top just because it was the broadest pregame market.
- MLB postponed-game settlement, from Arizona-St. Louis rainout: postponed/delayed MLB rows must carry a separate settlement branch. Do not grade side or total markets from a postponed original date unless the specific book/action rules roll action to the rescheduled game; mark POSTPONED/NO GRADE or PENDING BY HOUSE RULE and revisit only if the user supplies settlement rules.
- MLC/new-venue ceiling calibration, from Seattle 227/6 vs Washington at Oakland: one low-scoring first game at a new venue cannot anchor a high-180s under when another credible source gives a 190-200 venue band and the batting side has proven 220+ ceiling plus multiple middle/death hitters. Without a same-day official pitch report, treat source disagreement as a confidence cap, not an under signal; keep full over adjacent or above the under when batting depth can add 110+ after a merely decent powerplay.
- WNBA phase/full split, from Las Vegas 99 vs Dallas 84: direct H2H first-half pace can correctly support the 1H over while the full total still needs a separate injury/matchup test. Do not rank a full-game under above its over solely because of a recent under streak if direct H2Hs are hot and an injury creates a superstar efficiency route, such as Dallas missing Alanna Smith against A'ja Wilson. Full totals near the projection band need the same close-complement honesty as Q1/1H markets.

RULESET-LOCKED COMPETITION GOVERNANCE - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM DEEP RULES REVIEW:
- This block upgrades every sport template from analytics-only to ruleset-locked decision support. Before any ranking, identify the exact competition law environment. A true rule from the wrong competition is still a wrong input.
- RULESET LOCK: record competition, governing body, rulebook or playing-conditions edition, competition variant, season phase, and settlement convention. If any field is UNKNOWN, state the missing dependency and cap confidence.
- OFFICIAL SOURCE ORDER: use the governing-body rulebook or playing conditions first, competition regulations second, official match centre/gamebook/scorecard third, then verified statistical databases or credentialed reporting. Tag every source as OFFICIAL, PROJECTED, UNOFFICIAL, DELAYED, BLOCKED, ARCHIVAL, or SNIPPET.
- SETTLEMENT MATRIX: before analysis and again before grading, record whether the market includes or excludes overtime, extra time, penalties/shootout, Super Over, DLS adjustment, reserve day, automatic extra-inning runner, suspended-game resumption, official-stat correction, and protest-review correction.
- ELIGIBILITY / CLASSIFICATION CHECK: confirm roster or team-sheet deadline, named starters/XI/active list, suspension status, age/category restrictions, import or registration clearance, concussion/HIA return clearance, and tournament classification or tiebreak method when standings context matters. If unresolved, mark the affected player/team as ELIGIBILITY UNKNOWN.
- DISPUTE / OFFICIAL-CORRECTION RULE: do not finalize settlement if the competition permits result continuation, protest procedure, replay correction, official scoring correction, or suspended-game resumption and the official final record is not yet published.
- STATUS CERTAINTY TAXONOMY: CONFIRMED means final official competition or club source. PROJECTED means official or trusted projection but not final. EXPECTED means strong reporting without official final status. UNKNOWN means no reliable source opened. Do not promote PROJECTED/EXPECTED to CONFIRMED in the card.
- RETROSPECTIVE AUDIT TEST: every serious miss must state whether the root cause was wrong ruleset, wrong availability/eligibility state, wrong settlement convention, source-status error, projection error, or rank-slot calibration error. Update the results log and calibration ledger with the same classification.

SPORT-SPECIFIC RULESET LOCKS:
- AFL / AFLW / Australian rules: identify AFL men, AFLW, state league, under-age, or local competition before using timing/player-count/interchange assumptions. Check team-sheet deadline, interchange/rotation structure, blood-rule handling, substitute/late-change status, and any age or local variation. Do not apply AFL men's player-count or timing assumptions to AFLW.
- Baseball / MLB / NPB / KBO: record season phase and competition rule family. For MLB, explicitly mark regular season vs postseason because automatic-runner extra innings and other administration rules differ. Confirm pitch timer, batter alert timing, disengagement limits, three-batter minimum, ABS/challenge status if active, suspended-game treatment, roster/lineup eligibility, and park/roof/weather state.
- Basketball / NBA / WNBA / FIBA-style domestic: identify NBA/WNBA administration vs FIBA-style competition before analysis. Record quarter length, overtime length, injury-report deadline, starter submission/team-sheet timing, import/registration status, protest procedure, and classification/tiebreak method where standings context matters.
- HARD RULE - NBA SUMMER LEAGUE IS NOT NBA REGULAR SEASON (added 2026-07-12): identify Las Vegas, Salt Lake City or California Summer League before using any NBA-branded statistics. Do not import the parent franchise's regular-season StatMuse L5/L10/L15/L20, H2H, pace, lineup, injury report or player roles into a Summer League card. StatMuse must still be attempted first under the American-sports rule, but if it returns the NBA roster/regular season or cannot isolate Summer League, label it `UNSUPPORTED/MISPARSED - NOT USED`. Use official NBA Summer League schedules, rules, rosters, live pages and box scores first, then a transparent box-score source such as RealGM for possessions and phase splits. Run L5/L10/L15/L20 only to the extent the same current Summer League squad has that many games; early in the event, write the unavailable windows explicitly and cap confidence. Historical Summer League teams are roster-discontinuous context only. Four 10-minute periods, the one-free-throw rule, 10-foul player limit and Summer League overtime rules must replace ordinary 12-minute NBA assumptions.
- Cricket: apply rule hierarchy in this order: MCC Laws edition, ICC playing conditions, tournament supplemental conditions. Record format, innings length, toss/XI status, DLS/rain-shortened state, reserve day, no-result rules, Super Over eligibility, concussion replacement status, and any local playing-condition override.
- Cricket surface-evidence hard gate: before ranking any cricket innings, powerplay, first-5/first-6, or chase-total market, write three separate labels: ACTUAL PITCH REPORT, CAPTAIN/TOSS COMMENTS, and LIVE SURFACE BEHAVIOUR. Do not merge them. If the actual pitch report is not opened, write ACTUAL PITCH REPORT: UNKNOWN and cap confidence. Captain comments such as "we bowled because of rain/unknown conditions" are not pitch reports, but they are still usable soft evidence for intent, uncertainty, dew/rain concern, expected new-ball movement, batting order/chase preference, or dressing-room read of conditions. Weight them only after the actual pitch report, weather, venue, boundary size, and toss/XI are stated; upgrade their impact when live surface behaviour confirms them, and downgrade them when the actual pitch report or scoring pattern contradicts them. Live scoring can override the pregame model only after play begins, and must be labelled LIVE SURFACE BEHAVIOUR, not retrofitted as the pre-match pitch report.
- HARD RULE - MLC / FRANCHISE-T20 SAME-DAY PITCH SOURCE LADDER (added 2026-07-12): the controlling actual pitch report is the host-broadcast pre-match pitch walkaround, normally 15-30 minutes before the toss/first ball. For MLC, check Willow by Cricbuzz first; use Seven/7plus in Australia or JioHotstar/other confirmed rights-holder carrying the same host feed where available. Record the reporter, exact wording, boundary measurements, grass/hardness/cracks/moisture, used/new strip status and timestamp when shown. Next use Cricbuzz live commentary only when it explicitly quotes or attributes the broadcast report. Official MLC/team match centres control fixture, toss, XI and score but must not be assumed to contain a pitch inspection. CREX, fantasy/prediction pages and generic venue articles are VENUE PRIORS only, even when headed "Pitch Report"; phrases such as hard surface, true bounce, batting-friendly or short boundaries are not an ACTUAL PITCH REPORT unless attributed to the same-day inspection. Accept a user-provided broadcast screenshot/transcript as primary evidence only after the identity checks below pass.
- HARD RULE - NO VERIFIED PITCH REPORT, NO FINAL CRICKET CARD (added 2026-07-12): every pregame cricket card must obtain a match-specific, same-day actual pitch report before issuing or ranking the final four picks. Verify all available identity fields: exact teams and competition, fixture date/start window, venue, reporter/source and timestamp, and whether the strip is new/used or a particular numbered/adjacent strip when stated. Cross-check the report against the official fixture and toss; reject a report from the wrong match, venue, date, earlier game at the ground, or generic venue preview. If the actual report cannot be opened or verified, write `ACTUAL PITCH REPORT: UNKNOWN - FINAL CARD WITHHELD` and provide only a provisional research/watchlist until the host report or a verifiable broadcast screenshot/transcript is obtained. Do not lower this gate merely by capping confidence. If play has already begun and the pre-match report cannot be recovered, say so explicitly: live analysis may continue from the verified score and ball-by-ball evidence, but it must be labelled LIVE SURFACE BEHAVIOUR and must never be presented as the missing pitch report.
- HARD RULE - CRICKET LIVE PHASE-CUSHION OVERRIDE (added 2026-07-12 from TSK-Washington): once play starts, recompute the exact remaining requirement before repeating any pregame phase lean. Example: at 48/0 after 4.4 overs on a first-six line of 54.5, the Over needs only seven runs from eight legal balls and the pregame Under must be abandoned regardless of venue averages, bowling reputation or the unavailable pitch report. More generally, when at least 75% of a phase threshold has already been consumed with one-third or more of the phase remaining and no wicket/collapse evidence, treat live pace and wickets in hand as controlling. Log the score/overs/wickets trigger as LIVE SURFACE BEHAVIOUR and rerank the linked full-innings ceiling separately; a hot powerplay raises but does not automatically settle the 20-over over.
- Cricket banned surface shorthand: do not use "road", "road-like", "flat deck", "green top", "two-paced", "sticky", "seaming", "turner", or equivalent surface labels unless the actual pitch report/source says it, or live ball-by-ball evidence has already proved it. When live evidence proves it, state the score/time trigger and keep the source class as LIVE, e.g. "road-like live inference after 82/0 in 8 overs", not "pitch report said road."
- Soccer / Football: record match-outcome mode before ranking: regulation only, extra time, penalties, aggregate/two-leg tie, or tournament group ranking. Confirm substitution allowance, extra-time substitute allowance, added-time expectations, named-player eligibility, and penalty shoot-out eligibility. Treat shoot-outs as separate settlement events, not just more goals.
- NFL / College football: record active rulebook year and regular-season vs postseason overtime convention. Do not import prior-year overtime, kickoff, replay, timing, or injury-report assumptions without confirming the current rulebook year and competition level.
- NRL / Rugby league: record competition manual/source state, team-list bulletin status, cutdown/final-team timing, HIA/concussion clearance, judiciary/suspension status, interchange rules, captain's challenge scope, and golden-point settlement. If official NRL pages are access-restricted, name the alternate official club/media chain and cap confidence on administration questions.
- NHL / IIHF / hockey: separate NHL from IIHF/NCAA/other rule families. Mark regular season vs playoff overtime/shootout settlement. Starting goalies and line combinations remain PROJECTED until confirmed by official team/warmup/game-sheet evidence.

RULESET VALIDATION TESTS:
- AFLW variant test: an AFLW fixture must trigger AFLW player-count, quarter-length, and rotation assumptions instead of AFL men's defaults.
- MLB season-phase test: an MLB regular-season tied game must invoke the automatic runner; a postseason tied game must not unless the current rulebook says otherwise.
- Basketball jurisdiction test: NBA/WNBA cards must use NBA/WNBA injury and starter timing; FIBA-style cards must surface FIBA quarter length, protest, and classification rules.
- Cricket weather-state test: rain-affected cards must label full-length, DLS-adjusted, no-result, reserve-day, and Super Over states before ranking.
- Cricket surface-source test: every cricket card must show ACTUAL PITCH REPORT vs CAPTAIN/TOSS COMMENTS vs LIVE SURFACE BEHAVIOUR. Captain/toss comments should not be dismissed; they should be prefaced by the actual pitch report, weather, boundary/venue context, and current live evidence state, then used as a secondary adjustment. If any answer uses a surface shorthand without this classification, the card fails Gate 5 and must be rewritten before ranking.
- Soccer shoot-out test: knockout cards must separate regulation, extra time, and penalties; penalty shoot-outs must use eligible-on-field player logic.
- NFL overtime test: regular-season and postseason overtime must be written separately from the current rulebook, not assumed from prior years.
- NHL status test: a projected goalie or lineup remains PROJECTED until official team/warmup/game-sheet confirmation.
- Retrospective audit test: every graded card that misses badly must update the log and ledger with ruleset, availability, and settlement-cause flags.

ACTIVE PRIORITY OVERRIDE - ADDED 2026-06-04 FROM COMPREHENSIVE LOG REVIEW; UPGRADED 2026-06-10:
- 2026-06-10 UPGRADE POINTER (read first): a comprehensive accuracy upgrade now governs every card - the MANDATORY PRE-CARD GATE SEQUENCE (Gates 0-10), the TIMEZONE AND GAME-STATE RESOLUTION PROTOCOL (request time is AEST; convert the venue-local start to AEST and classify PREGAME/LIVE/LIKELY COMPLETE before saying anything about whether a game has happened), the DATA FRESHNESS AND ANTI-STALENESS PROTOCOL (every decisive fact tagged source + timestamp + OPENED/SNIPPET/ASSUMED), the MASTER SOURCE DIRECTORY (current lineup/injury/news/weather sources and fetchable APIs per sport), the EVIDENCE-CITED REASONING REQUIREMENT, the TOP/BOTTOM PENALTY ELIMINATION PROTOCOL (written probability table; #1 needs >=60% + fresh mechanism + stress test; #4 needs <=30% + most failure paths; swap test; forbidden-#4 list), the LIVE ACCESS TEST MATRIX, the X/SOCIAL VERIFICATION GATE, the SOCIAL-ACCESS PLAYBOOK with the Bluesky public-API rung and the fetch-truncation rule (Section 10), the RANK-SLOT META-CALIBRATION with per-sport leak gates - raw slot rates #1 ~65%/#4 ~49%, cricket T20 under-bias, KBO inversion, MLB/AFL bottom-slot leaks, NBA phase coinflips (Section 11), and the REPRODUCIBLE PROCEDURES for access probes and log recounts (Section 12). All live in the dated block "2026-06-10 COMPREHENSIVE ACCURACY UPGRADE" near the end of this file. If short on time, run Gates 1, 2, 7 and 9 - they block the four dominant penalty classes.
- UNIVERSAL 5/10/15/20 TREND LADDER: for every sport and ranked card, collect the past 5, 10, 15, and 20 relevant matches/games/innings/rounds where the data exists. Cover all decision-relevant stat families, not only scoring: scoring for/against, phase splits tied to the market, attacking/creation metrics, defensive prevention metrics, possession/territory or field-position equivalents, efficiency/conversion, turnovers/errors/discipline, set pieces/special teams/bullpen/bench/relief units, venue/home-away splits, and player/unit availability where available. Use sport-specific equivalents and state unavailable fields as UNKNOWN rather than guessing. If a team or competition has fewer current-season matches than a window, say whether the wider sample includes prior-season data or stop at the largest reliable current-context sample.
- MANDATORY BASELINE COMPARISON - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM USER RULE: when researching past stats, do not stop at a last-N number. For the team as a whole AND the players/units materially involved in the card, compare the last 5/10/15/20 split against (1) current-season average, (2) historical/career or multi-season average, and (3) the relevant venue/home-away/opponent-quality split when available. Write the direction of the delta plainly: heating up, cooling down, stable, role-changed, opponent-inflated, schedule-inflated, injury-distorted, or sample too small. For players, include role/minutes/usage or lineup spot/batting order/bowling spell/ice time/snap share where relevant; a player last-N stat is not meaningful if the role changed.
- LAST-N DECISION RULE: a recent split controls only when it is both fresh and explanatory. If last 5 differs sharply from last 20, season, or historical average, identify the cause before ranking: lineup change, injury return/scratch, opponent strength, venue, weather, tactical role, fatigue, official/referee/umpire profile, or variance. If no cause is found, regress the last-5 number toward season and historical baselines rather than treating it as the new truth.
- UNIVERSAL STAT COVERAGE MATRIX - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM USER RULE: every serious research card must run a "stat coverage matrix" before ranking. For each sport, list each relevant stat family as CHECKED / NOT AVAILABLE / NOT RELEVANT TO THIS MARKET. For checked families, record last 5/10/15/20 where available, season average, historical/career or multi-season baseline, source/access tag, and the direction of the delta. Do not claim a stat was accounted for unless a source was opened or the local log/doc already contains a fresh verified value. If a source cannot be opened, write UNKNOWN and cap confidence when that stat family matters to the provided options.
- STAT WEIGHTING MECHANISM: convert the stat matrix into a short decision table before ranking: (1) market relevance, (2) sample quality, (3) recency vs season/historical direction, (4) injury/lineup/role adjustment, (5) venue/weather/officials adjustment, (6) final probability band. A stat only moves a pick when it has both relevance and a plausible mechanism. Raw averages without a mechanism are context only.
- BASEBALL / MLB / NPB / KBO IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: team runs scored/allowed, inning splits, first-5 vs bullpen scoring, home/away/park splits, handedness/platoon splits, lineup order and confirmed starters, OBP/SLG/OPS/wOBA/wRC+ where available, strikeout and walk rates, ISO/power, stolen-base and baserunning value, starter ERA/FIP/xFIP/SIERA where available, WHIP, K-BB%, ground-ball/fly-ball profile, hard-hit/barrel/exit velocity/xwOBA/xBA/xSLG where available, pitch velocity/mix/recent command, times-through-order penalty, catcher framing/throwing, bullpen ERA/FIP/WHIP/K-BB/leverage arms/rest/pitch counts/back-to-backs, defensive outs above average/DRS/errors, umpire/strike-zone tendency if posted and sampled, park factors, roof/wind/temperature/humidity, travel/rest, and recent injury/scratch status.
- BASKETBALL / NBA / WNBA / DOMESTIC IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: points for/against, pace/possessions, offensive rating, defensive rating, net rating, Q1/1H/quarter splits, eFG%, true shooting, 2P/3P/FT rates and accuracy, shot profile at rim/midrange/three, free-throw rate, turnover rate, offensive/defensive rebounding rate, assist rate, paint points, fast-break points, second-chance points, bench points, lineup/on-off/net rating where available, usage rate, minutes/load/rest, foul rate, opponent rim protection and three-point allowance, clutch/late-game creation, home/away and back-to-back splits, injuries/suspensions/import eligibility, referee foul/free-throw tendency when posted and sampled.
- CRICKET IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: format-specific team runs scored/conceded, innings and phase splits (powerplay, middle overs, death overs), run rate, required/chase run rate, wickets lost by phase, batting average/strike rate/boundary rate/dot-ball rate, batter role and batting position, left-right matchups, bowling economy/strike rate/average, dot-ball rate, powerplay/death bowling roles, wickets by phase, recent overs workload, spin vs pace splits, venue average by innings, boundary size, pitch type/age, toss and bat/bowl choice, dew, weather/interruption/DLS risk, fielding/catching quality, keeper impact, team XI/availability, umpire/match-referee/pitch-rating context, and comparable-opponent quality.
- SOCCER / FOOTBALL IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: goals for/against, xG/xGA/non-penalty xG where available, shots and shots on target, big chances for/against, box entries/touches in box, possession and field tilt/territory, pass completion/progression, key passes/chances created, pressing/PPDA or pressure metrics where available, set pieces, corners for/against, crosses, offsides, fouls, yellow/red cards, penalties for/against, first-half/second-half goals, clean sheets, goalkeeper saves/save percentage/post-shot xG where available, formations and tactical matchup, home/away/neutral split, travel/rest, squad rotation, injuries/suspensions, referee cards/fouls/penalty/added-time tendency, venue surface/weather, and match context/table motivation.
- AMERICAN FOOTBALL / NFL / NCAA IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: points for/against, EPA/play, success rate, yards/play, explosive-play rate, early-down efficiency, third/fourth-down conversion, red-zone TD rate, goal-to-go efficiency, pass/rush rate and pace/seconds per play, QB efficiency/CPOE/ANYA/QBR where available, pressure/sack rate, offensive-line injuries and pass-block/run-block profile, rushing success and stuffed rate, receiver/TE/RB availability and target share, defensive EPA/success rate, pressure/blitz rate, coverage injuries, run-stop rate, missed tackles, turnovers and turnover-worthy plays, special teams field position/kicking, penalties by crew/team, weather/wind/field surface, travel/rest/short week, and game-script sensitivity.
- AFL IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: points for/against, quarter splits, inside-50 differential, scoring shots, goals/behinds conversion, expected score where available, clearances, centre clearances, stoppage profile, contested possession, uncontested possession, disposal efficiency, turnovers, intercepts, marks inside 50, contested marks, tackles/pressure, rebound 50s, time in forward half/territory where available, ruck hitouts to advantage, key forward/key defender availability, midfield availability, substitutions/late changes, home/away/interstate/travel, venue dimensions/weather/wind, umpire/free-kick profile where available, and motivation/ladder context.
- NRL / RUGBY LEAGUE IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: points for/against, half splits, tries/conversions/goal-kicking, run metres, post-contact metres, line breaks, tackle breaks, offloads, sets and completion rate, errors, penalties/six-agains, possession, territory, play-the-ball speed/ruck control where available, kick metres and forced dropouts, red-zone/tackle-zone efficiency, missed tackles, tackle efficiency, spine availability (1/6/7/9), forward-pack/middle rotation, edge defence, interchange/HIAs/suspensions, short turnaround/travel, weather/surface, referee penalty/six-again/high-contact tendency where available, and ladder/motivation context.
- NHL IMPORTANT STAT FAMILIES: goals for/against, shots for/against, shot attempts/Corsi/Fenwick, expected goals/xG share, high-danger chances, scoring chances, shooting percentage, save percentage, PDO/regression, power play and penalty kill, penalties drawn/taken, faceoffs by zone where relevant, zone starts, rush chances, forecheck/turnover pressure where available, goalie confirmed starter, goalie recent save percentage/GSAx/quality-start rate, backup/rest workload, line combinations, defensive pairs, injuries/scratches, travel/rest/back-to-back, home/away, period splits, empty-net risk, referee penalty tendency, and arena/ice/travel context.
- Read this before every ranked card. The biggest leak is not the top pick; it is the fourth slot. Current log audit shows top picks are usually strong, but the declared least-likely pick has been winning too often. Spend the most time proving slot 4 is genuinely unlikely.
- Slot 4 is allowed only when the outcome has the most failure paths and a clear probability below about 30%. If the four provided options force a near-coinflip into slot 4, explicitly say "forced bottom slot; no genuine least-likely outcome" and cap confidence.
- For every total or phase-total card, state current-season scoring/pace plus last 2-3 actual games before old H2H. Multi-season H2H and preview adjectives are context only, never the anchor, unless a concrete current mechanism supports them.
- Opponent-adjust recent scoring before using it. Low scoring against strong opponents can understate a team's ceiling against a weak opponent; high scoring against minnows can overstate the ceiling against a peer.
- Do not bury ordinary overs when the expected band sits on or near the line. If the expected total is within about one scoring unit of the threshold, rank over/under adjacent and keep both out of the confident fade slot.
- OVERRIDE to the above for cricket powerplay markets: when an inserted batting side faces an elite pace new-ball attack (Australia-tier: Garth/Hamilton-class) under final/knockout pressure, rank the POWERPLAY UNDER at or above the powerplay over even if the batting side's recent PP outputs cleared the line against weaker attacks. Those PP figures do not transfer against elite new-ball quality. Evidence: 2026-07-06 WT20I final, England inserted at Lord's slumped to 39/2 in the PP (line 45.5) and made 150/4 despite recent 46/51/57 powerplays vs weaker sides; the full-innings under and Australia winner were correct, but PP Over was wrongly ranked above PP Under. Opponent new-ball quality outweighs the batting side's own recent powerplay form.
- Margin modelling must be mechanism-based: efficiency edge, current form, comparable-opponent margins, home/away, injuries, fatigue, and game state. A favorite cover and an over can be positively correlated when the opponent's weakest dimension is defense.
- Live questions override pregame models. Pull the live score/state, settle resolved legs immediately, and rerank the remaining legs from exact arithmetic: required points/runs/goals/corners, time left, current pace, foul/penalty/bench state, and game script.
- Settlement hygiene is part of accuracy. Before a new analysis, clear stale pending labels with official/primary sources where available. Do not leave "pending" or "needs confirmation" wording after a reliable final score or phase split is found.
- Availability is a primary input, not a footnote. Before every ranked card, re-check injuries and last-minute lineup exclusions at the latest possible cutoff and weight each by PLAYER PROFILE and replacement drop-off, not by headcount. A confirmed scratch of an elite/MVP-tier player must collapse any side/winner/total edge that rested on that player; a "day-to-day"/"questionable" tag is not confirmation. Net both teams' exclusions by profile and write an "availability verdict" before ranking. See the dedicated module "INJURY AND LAST-MINUTE LINEUP EXCLUSION IMPACT MODEL".

AMERICAN SPORTS STATS SOURCE PRIORITY - ARCHIVED DUPLICATE:
- The active authority is Canonical Hard Data Rule A at the top of this file and Section 13.10. This pointer replaces four repeated policy bullets formerly located here.
- Re-evaluation lesson from Sparks-Storm: StatMuse season and last-5 splits can materially change phase-total ranking. Sparks 88.7 PPG/91.09 opponent PPG and Storm 76.2 PPG/83.31 opponent PPG supported a full-game under lean, but the decisive correction was last-5 and phase form: Sparks 85.4 PPG, Storm 71.0 PPG, combined last-5 first-quarter scoring 37.6 and combined last-5 first-half scoring 76.8. That moved 1H Under above 1H Over and made full Over 169.5 the weakest option.

FIFA WORLD CUP SOCCER SOURCE PRIORITY - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM USER SOURCE REQUEST:
- Scope: applies to FIFA men's World Cup, Women's World Cup, World Cup qualifiers, and World Cup warm-up/friendly cards unless a more specific competition source exists. If the user means FIFA Club World Cup, keep FIFA official pages first for official state, but use club-form sources (FotMob, FBref, domestic/continental official pages) for last-5/10/15/20 club context.
- OFFICIAL FACTS / SOURCE OF RECORD: FIFA official tournament pages and match centres control fixtures, kickoff time, venue, official squads, team sheets when posted, substitutions, disciplinary status, final score, official match events, and tournament standings. If FIFA is dynamic/thin or blocked, cross-check official federation/team pages and major broadcaster match centres, but do not let an aggregator override FIFA for official state.
- BEST PRACTICAL FREE STATS HUB: FotMob is the first stop for current World Cup team/player stats, fixtures, tables, lineups, match stats, player ratings, goals/assists, shots/on-target, xG-style views where shown, form, and H2H. FotMob is human-facing and discourages automated scraping, so use it as a manual/current stats hub and cross-check decisive facts.
- DEEPER POST-MATCH / ADVANCED STATS: FBref/StatsBomb public pages are the preferred free advanced-stat layer when available: standard, shooting, passing, pass types, goal/shot creation, possession, defensive actions, goalkeeping, misc stats, and xG. FBref can be delayed or return 403, so mark access state and fall back to FotMob/FIFA/other public match centres when blocked.
- BROAD CROSS-CHECKS: ESPN soccer, BBC/Guardian, FootMercato, Soccerway, WorldFootball.net, StatBunker, SofaScore, Flashscore, LiveScore, and WhoScored are secondary/tertiary depending on access. Use them to cross-check live state, corners/cards/fouls, formations, and injuries, but label them by access quality and never settle a disputed final or official lineup from a single aggregator.
- AUTOMATION-GRADE / PREMIUM: for the most reliable up-to-date all-stat feed across World Cup matches, use a licensed provider such as Opta/Stats Perform, Sportradar, or Sportmonks. These are better than consumer pages for automated event feeds, lineups, player stats, xG/event data, and historical querying, but access depends on paid licensing. If no licensed feed is available, use FIFA + FotMob + FBref/ESPN/Guardian cross-checks.
- LAST 5/10/15/20 WORKFLOW FOR WORLD CUP CARDS: collect each national team's last 5, 10, 15, and 20 senior matches from FIFA/FotMob/Soccerway/WorldFootball/ESPN, separating World Cup finals, qualifiers, continental tournaments, Nations League, and friendlies. Record goals for/against, xG if available, shots, shots on target, big chances, corners, cards, fouls, offsides, possession, pass volume/accuracy, clean sheets, first-half goals, and home/away/neutral venue.
- SAMPLE-QUALITY TAGS: because national-team samples are sparse and uneven, tag every last-5/10/15/20 match by opponent tier, competition type, venue, travel/rest, squad strength, and whether the coach/lineup resembles the current tournament XI. Do not treat a friendly-heavy last-10 the same as a qualifier/tournament last-10.
- PLAYER FORM: for World Cup player projections, combine national-team minutes/role with club last 5/10/15/20 appearances, because national-team samples alone are often too small. Track minutes, starts, position/role, goals/assists, shots, key passes/chances, defensive actions, set-piece role, injury return, and workload since the club release date.
- IN-TOURNAMENT OVERRIDE: once the World Cup begins, the team's current tournament matches override older form for tactical shape, role, set-piece takers, pressing intensity, cards/suspensions, and goalkeeper/defensive cohesion. Use the tournament sample first, then the 5/10/15/20 ladder for priors and regression.

NON-OBVIOUS FACTORS + OFFICIALS ASSIGNMENT RULE - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM USER SOURCE REQUEST:
- Before every serious card, run a "hidden factors" pass in addition to obvious form/injury/venue checks. Include referee/umpire/match-official assignments, rule-enforcement tendencies, disciplinary context, travel/rest/time-zone burden, altitude, surface/roof/wind, schedule congestion, motivation/table state, rivalry/derby tension, tactical comments, set-piece roles, late lineup role changes, ballpark/ground dimensions, and weather in the actual match window. If a factor is unavailable, write UNKNOWN rather than ignoring it silently.
- Officials can be decision-driving for specific markets: soccer cards/fouls/penalties/corners/added time; basketball fouls/free throws/technical fouls/pace interruption; baseball strike zone/walks/contact count/over-under and catcher framing; cricket wides/no-balls/DRS/discipline/pitch ratings; AFL/NRL penalties/free kicks/six-agains/high-contact interpretation; NHL penalties/power plays; NFL penalties/pass interference/holding/roughing and game pace.
- OFFICIALS SOURCE HIERARCHY: use official competition/league match centres, game books, appointments pages, media guides, scorecards, or match reports first. If not posted there, use official federation/team communications, accredited reporter posts, broadcast notes, and reliable specialist databases. Aggregators can discover names but must be cross-checked before they control a ranking.
- SOCCER / FIFA WORLD CUP: FIFA official match pages, referee appointment releases, match reports, and competition media guides control referee, assistant referees, fourth official, VAR, AVAR, and other video officials. For domestic/continental soccer, use league/confederation referee appointment pages first, then ESPN/BBC/Guardian/FotMob/SofaScore/WhoScored only as cross-checks. For World Cup, the pre-tournament list of officials is not enough; the match-specific appointment matters.
- MLB / BASEBALL: official MLB Gameday/boxscore/live feed controls umpires once posted. If pregame official pages do not show the crew, use MLB game notes, Baseball Press, Umpire Scorecards, Close Call Sports, Retrosheet historical umpire data, and broadcast notes as secondary sources. For totals, note home-plate umpire strike-zone/walk tendency only if supported by a recent/multi-season sample; do not overrate a small umpire sample over starter/bullpen/weather.
- NBA/WNBA/NCAA BASKETBALL: official NBA/WNBA/NCAA game notes, official box scores/game books, league referee assignment pages where available, and team PR/broadcast notes control officials. Track referee crew foul/free-throw/technical profile when available, especially for totals, player fouls, and free-throw-heavy teams, but lineup/pace/shot quality remain primary.
- NFL/NHL: use official gamebooks, league operations/officiating assignment pages, official scoresheets, and broadcast/game notes. For NFL, crew penalty tendency matters most for totals, pass-heavy offenses, offensive line mismatch, and defensive holding/PI. For NHL, referee/linesperson profile matters most for penalty volume, power plays, and physical rivalry games.
- AFL/NRL/Rugby: use official AFL/NRL/league match centres, team sheets, judiciary/tribunal pages, and league umpire/referee appointment releases where available. If assignments are not public pregame, use postgame official reports for settlement learning and keep pregame officials UNKNOWN. Track umpire/free-kick or referee/penalty/six-again tendencies only with a real sample.
- CRICKET: official ICC/board scorecards and match previews control on-field umpires, TV umpire, fourth umpire, and match referee. ESPNcricinfo/Cricbuzz scorecards are strong cross-checks when official pages are thin. Match referee/pitch-rating context can matter for over rates, no-ball/wide strictness, DRS flow, over-rate penalties, and player discipline, but pitch/toss/XI remain stronger pregame inputs.
- WEIGHTING RULE: officials are modifiers, not standalone anchors, unless the market is directly officiating-sensitive and the official's tendency is well-sampled and current. Never put a pick at #1 or #4 solely because of an official assignment without at least one independent support signal from teams, venue/weather, lineup, tactical matchup, or recent form.
- RECORDING RULE: if officials are available before the card, name them and state the expected effect in one line. If unavailable, write "OFFICIALS: not posted/UNKNOWN at cutoff" and cap confidence for officiating-sensitive markets. In retrospectives, add official names when final gamebook/scorecard exposes them and record whether their whistle/zone/discipline profile mattered.

SETTLEMENT LEARNINGS - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM LKL NEPTUNAS-LIETKABELIS AND MLB ARI-MIA:
- Domestic basketball playoff/third-place series: immediate series phase splits can crush season PPG. Neptunas-Lietkabelis Game 3 finished 66-63 with Q1 39, halftime 77, final 129 after prior series totals of 159 and 148. When the current playoff H2H ladder is already below the user thresholds, rank the under ladder high even if season averages imply 170+. Also lower the exact projected score band; do not merely put the under first while still projecting a mid/high-150s or 160s game.
- Phase separation still matters: Game 1 had a 45-point Q1 but only 83 by halftime and 159 final; Game 3 had a 39-point Q1 and 77 half. In basketball playoff rematches, Q1, 1H, and full-game totals need separate last 5/10/15/20 and same-series counts. A hot first quarter can be an outlier inside an otherwise under series.
- MLB roof/park suppression: ARI-MIA finished Marlins 2, Diamondbacks 0. Roof-closed loanDepot, Arizona's cold offense, and Miami run prevention were strong enough to make Under 9.5 and Marlins ML correct, but the projected 5-3/5-4 score was too high. In roof/low-run parks, compare last 5/10/15/20 team and key-hitter form against season and historical baselines, then let cold-offense/park signals lower the exact run band, not just the rank.
- Top-half exemption validated: both settled cards had every top-half pick win. Do not apply a bottom-pick penalty when the top half is swept; the model selected the right priority cluster even if the bottom half contains live-but-lower-probability outcomes.

SETTLEMENT LEARNINGS - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM TEX-KC MLB AND MEXICO-SOUTH AFRICA WORLD CUP:
- MLB wind-out context is a ceiling raiser, not an automatic over. Rangers-Royals finished 4-2 despite 88F weather and 20 mph wind out to CF because both starters/team run-prevention paths were competent and the 12+ run path needed multiple things to break wrong. Keep weather as a modifier behind starter current form, bullpen availability, lineup damage paths, and recent 5/10/15/20 run bands.
- Soccer favorite-control shape: Mexico-South Africa finished 2-0 with a 1-0 halftime. This validated ranking 1H Over 0.5 and Under 2.5 together when the modal script is a controlled 1-0/2-0 favorite win. On a separate 1.5-goal card, Under 1.5 is a fragile bottom when the clean 2-0 path is live. Do not force all "opening match caution" into a blanket under; split 1H goal probability from full-game ceiling.
- Soccer favourite-control draw-risk addendum, added 2026-06-14 from Qatar-Switzerland: a favourite can control territory, shot volume, and the first-half lead while still failing to win if finishing is inefficient and the opponent has a late cross/set-piece path. Qatar-Switzerland finished 1-1 after Switzerland led 1-0 at halftime, so Under 2.5 and 1H Over 0.5 were right but the Switzerland winner lean failed. Keep side/winner confidence capped when the projected score is 1-0/2-0 but the weaker team has credible late delivery or aerial routes.
- Soccer high-chance finishing correction, added 2026-06-19 from Switzerland-Bosnia-Herzegovina: do not make Under 4.5 the #1 anchor when a favourite has just produced elite chance volume and faces a weaker/deeper side that can both concede late and still score once by set piece, counter, penalty, or consolation. Switzerland won 4-1, validating the Swiss scoring/winner and Bosnia-fade logic, but the fifth goal exposed that "group-stage control" is not enough to suppress the 4-1/3-2 tail. In similar World Cup group matches, separate "favourite scores/wins" from "hard ceiling under"; keep Under 4.5 as a lean only if lineups, finishing, game state, and underdog scoring routes all suppress 5+.
- Soccer draw-protection reinforcement, added 2026-06-19 from Czechia-South Africa: Czechia-South Africa finished 1-1 while all four listed safety picks won: South Africa team total Under 2.5, full Under 4.5, Czechia team total Over 0.5, and Czechia/draw. South Africa still generated 61.7% possession, 17 shots, and the equaliser despite suspension context. In expanded group formats where one point has value, keep double-chance/non-loss and team-total/ceiling markets above exact winner calls when the projection band already includes 1-1.
- Soccer 1H-goal/full-over split, added 2026-06-20 from Turkiye-Paraguay: a strong 1H Over 0.5 profile does not automatically make full Over 2.5 a strong leg. Paraguay scored in the 2nd minute and 1H Over 0.5 won, but the match still finished 1-0 after Paraguay protected a lead with 10 men and Turkiye generated pressure without finishing quality. In group-stage matches, split early opener probability from 3+ goal probability; keep Over 2.5 adjacent or below the complement when the leading side can defend deep and the chasing side's chance quality is uncertain.
- Soccer attacking-lineup exception, added 2026-06-21 from Netherlands 5, Sweden 1: the Turkiye-Paraguay split is a caution, not a blanket downgrade of full overs. Netherlands-Sweden had confirmed attacking lineups, defensive-concession evidence from both openers, and a Netherlands urgency script; 1H Over 0.5 and full Over 2.5 both won after a 2-0 halftime. When both XI shape and recent match states support multiple goals, keep the full over above its complement instead of over-protecting group-stage caution.
- MLB environment weighting update, added 2026-06-19 from Toronto-Boston and Minnesota-Texas: Toronto beat Boston 4-3 at Fenway despite wind out to left, and Minnesota beat Texas 9-3 under a closed Globe Life Field roof. Weather, wind, roof, and park state are modifiers, not standalone total anchors. For high totals such as 9.5, cold offence plus starter command can still keep the under live even with hitter-friendly wind; for modest totals such as 7.5, a weak starter's walk/HR profile and opponent scoring form can drive an over even with roof suppression. Protect broad run-line cushions above exact winner calls when the projected margin cluster is narrow or the favourite's offence is cold.
- MLB volatile-starter Wrigley correction, added 2026-06-21 from Toronto 8, Chicago Cubs 6: crosswind is not automatic run suppression when both starters allow traffic, both lineups have HR routes, and late bullpen leverage can break open. Protect the broad road +1.5 cushion above a narrow home-winner lean when the favourite edge is mostly starter/contact matchup rather than a large team-quality gap; if the total line is 9.5, keep the over live unless wind/umpire/starter-command evidence all point lower.
- WT20I high-threshold innings correction, added 2026-06-19 from West Indies Women-Scotland Women: West Indies won the match but made only 153/6 with a 33-run powerplay, so Over 164.5 and PP Over 45.5 lost while the exact unders won. At 165+ first-innings thresholds, good-pitch comments, short boundaries, batting depth, and recent chase form are not enough unless the powerplay plus wickets-in-hand path clearly supports 46+ early and a death-overs surge. Third-use surfaces, early cloud/new-ball movement, and good-length seam evidence should cap over confidence and keep exact complements labelled low-edge.
- WT20I batting-friendly-pitch caveat, added 2026-06-28 from West Indies Women 128/7 vs Ireland Women at Bristol: an actual pitch report can include "excellent batting track" or short-boundary language and still produce a sub-46 powerplay and sub-150 innings if same-day scoring is slow, the pitch has dry/green/overhead/carry caveats, and the bowling side has a credible new-ball/seam route. Same-day phase evidence such as Pakistan 34/1, Netherlands 39/2 and West Indies 40/2 should outweigh a generic good-surface phrase for 150s WT20I lines unless the batting side's top order and wickets-in-hand path clearly supports acceleration.
- WT20I powerplay/full-innings split, added 2026-06-21 from England Women 200/5 vs Scotland Women: a high powerplay under and a high full-innings over can both win. England scored exactly 51 in the powerplay, under 51.5 by half a run, but reached 200/5 through middle/death hitting from Dunkley, Capsey, Kemp and Gibson. If an early wicket, key-player absence, or disciplined new-ball spell caps the first six, do not automatically downgrade the full-innings over when elite batting depth and death-overs acceleration remain intact.
- Men's T20 actual-pitch ceiling override, added 2026-06-19 from Texas Super Kings-Seattle Orcas: when the actual broadcast pitch report is emphatically batting-friendly and gives concrete boundary/weather support, do not leave 200+ as the true bottom merely because historical first-innings averages are lower. Seattle made 220/2 after the report described 60m/71m side boundaries, very hot conditions, a batting wicket and a belter; Texas still chased 221/4. In men's T20, an actual "belter"/short-boundary report can make high-180s and 200+ ordinary live paths. Let the actual pitch report override pre-report average bands and move ceiling outcomes above forced-complement bottoms unless there is confirmed elite bowling, new-ball movement, or batting weakness strong enough to counter it.
- Soccer mismatch over-tail correction, added 2026-06-16 from Sweden 5-1 Tunisia: in a genuine World Cup group-stage quality gap with an elite striker/forward pairing, opponent defensive instability, new selection/coach uncertainty, or a vulnerable goalkeeper, do not rank Under 2.5 above Over 2.5 merely because the fixture is a group opener or the underdog profile looks compact. Sweden-Tunisia validated the top 1H/Over 1.5 and bottom Under 1.5, but the middle was wrong: the game was already 2-1 at halftime and finished 5-1 through errors and second-half breakage. For similar mismatches, classify the 3+ and 4+ goal tails as ordinary live paths, especially when the favourite has bench attackers who can keep scoring after the match state opens.
- Soccer expanded-format draw inflation rule, added 2026-06-17 from Euro 24-team and World Cup 48-team review: when a tournament advances best third-placed teams, an opening or second group-stage draw becomes more strategically valuable. Euro 2016 expanded from 16 to 24 teams and sent four of six third-placed teams through; World Cup 2026 uses 12 groups of four with top two plus eight best third-placed teams advancing. In these formats, do not price Matchday 1 favourites as if only a top-two race matters: underdogs and mid-tier teams can rationally protect 0-0/1-1, favourites may accept controlled non-loss rather than overextend, and double-chance/draw/underdog-cushion plus under combinations become more live. Expansion is not the only cause of draws - sample variance, modern compact defending, tournament chemistry, and first-game caution still matter - so do not blindly rank every favourite/draw low; apply this only when group maths, team incentives, and tactical matchup make one point useful.
- Soccer elite-favourite late-ceiling correction, added 2026-06-17 from France 3-1 Senegal: a conservative Under 4.5 can be correct while stricter low-score assumptions are fragile. France-Senegal was 0-0 at halftime, then finished 3-1 through second-half role adjustment, elite finishing, bench flexibility, and late consolation/chasing chaos. For strong favourites against credible transition underdogs, separate "avoid 5+ goals" from "low scoring": Under 4.5 and opponent team-total unders can remain strong, but do not casually promote Under 2.5 or 1H over/under extremes unless the lineup and tactical evidence specifically support that phase.
- Top-half exemption reinforced again: both settled rows had the priority cluster right and the bottom pick lost. Retrospectives should reward the correct top-half ordering and avoid creating a bottom-pick penalty when the user's new exemption applies.

DOMESTIC BASKETBALL FINALS RESPONSE AND SIDE-VS-TOTAL UPDATE - ADDED 2026-06-16 FROM TURKISH BSL FENERBAHCE-BESIKTAS AND PLK ZASTAL-LEGIA:
- Favourite-response blowout consistency check: if the written #1 stress test says the favourite can produce a 12-18 point control win through home response, depth, tactical adjustment, or opponent structural absences, do not simultaneously top-rank the underdog cushion and bury the favourite spread last without a stronger reason. Fenerbahce-Besiktas Game 2 finished Fenerbahce 93-68 after Besiktas won Game 1, with the exact pregame kill path - Fenerbahce depth plus Besiktas frontcourt/rotation absences - showing up as a 25-point response. Recent H2H and Game 1 upset evidence can protect the dog, but it cannot override a realistic blowout mechanism that the card itself already identified.
- Same-series totals can be more stable than side reads in domestic finals. Zastal-Legia Game 4 finished Zastal 85-73: the full Under 162.5 and bottom Over 162.5 were correctly ranked from the repeated 142-159/low-160s series band, but the Legia side/winner missed because the home underdog had already shown a winning path and had urgent series-equalising context. In FIBA-style domestic finals, separate total confidence from side confidence: if the total band is repeated but the side projection is only a few points, protect the home underdog cushion more than a road-favourite lay.
- Half-point phase/total landings are calibration notes, not automatic major-process failures. Fener full total landed 161 against 161.5 and Zastal Q1 landed 40 against 39.5. For 40-43 Q1 lines and 160-163 full totals, keep complements adjacent unless pace/shot-quality/rotation evidence gives at least the normal basketball edge threshold.

SETTLEMENT LEARNINGS - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM WNBA CHI-IND, WNBA PHX-DAL, AND SOUTH KOREA-CZECHIA:
- WNBA full-game unders need an overtime/regulation stress test. Chicago-Indiana finished 114-106 after OT, but regulation was already 98-98, so the top-ranked Under 177.5 was wrong before overtime. Before ranking a WNBA full under #1, ask whether either team can reach 95-100 and whether the opponent has any credible 80-90 response path through pace, free throws, offensive rebounds, bench scoring, or late fouling. If yes, do not let injuries or last-5 cold offense alone make the full under a top anchor.
- WNBA phase-corridor rule: PHX-DAL Q1 finished 44, so both Q1 Under 45.5 and Q1 Over 41.5 won; CHI-IND Q1 finished 41, so both Q1 Under 44.5 and Q1 Over 40.5 won. When a card offers a low over and high under that create a 3-5 point middle corridor, and recent/season Q1 averages sit near that corridor, neither side is a genuine bottom slot. Label the bottom forced or rank a more structural side/full-game option below it.
- WNBA side-vs-total separation: Dallas beat Phoenix by 15 in a 155-point game. The total-under stack was right, but Mercury +10 was too high because Dallas' current form, home creation, and Phoenix's scoring/rebounding weakness created a normal double-digit win path. Low totals make large spreads harder, but they do not protect an underdog whose offense cannot keep the game within 10.
- WNBA repeat-upset stress-test discipline, added 2026-06-16 from Dallas 96-66 Las Vegas: when a previous head-to-head upset path is current, the underdog's star creator is returning or active, and the written stress test names several concrete dog-cover mechanisms, do not top-rank the favourite cover or bury the underdog cushion. A favourite can have the better season record, Cup motivation, and star power while still being structurally vulnerable to the exact matchup that already beat it. If the dog-cover path is specific rather than generic, keep the side pair adjacent or protect the dog cushion above volatile total complements.
- Soccer opener/altitude split: South Korea-Czechia finished 2-1 with goals at 59, 67, and 80 after a 0-0 halftime. In near-equal World Cup group openers at altitude/heat, a full-game Over 1.5 can still be correct through second-half substitutions, fatigue, set pieces, and chasing state, while 1H Under 0.5 is the better first-half lean. Do not rank 1H Over 0.5 above 1H Under 0.5 unless the match is a clear mismatch or there is specific early-press/lineup evidence.

NBA FINALS PHASE PARTIAL-SETTLEMENT UPDATE - ADDED 2026-06-11 FROM SPURS-KNICKS GAME 4 Q1/1H:
- Reinforces the live-state and phase-settlement rules above: a full NBA card can remain pending while a completed Q1 leg is already settled. Settle Q1 immediately from the official scoreboard/line score, then keep 1H/full/side legs pending until their market state is complete.
- Spurs-Knicks Game 4 opened Spurs 41, Knicks 22 (Q1 total 63), then reached halftime at Spurs 76, Knicks 49 (1H total 125), so Q1 Under 55.5 and 1H Under 110.5 lost while Q1 Over 55.5 and 1H Over 110.5 won. The miss came from ranking 1H Under first and Q1 Under above the side legs despite visible same-series slow-start evidence for New York and repeated fast San Antonio starts.
- For NBA Finals/rematch phase cards, do not rank a Q1 under high merely because the full-game or first-half projection leans under. Q1 is its own market: use the last 2-3 same-series Q1 splits, current starting-lineup matchup, early foul/turnover risk, and whether one team has a repeated opening burst pattern. If the Q1 count straddles the line, keep Q1 over/under adjacent and away from confident slots.
- After a high Q1, calculate the exact points needed for the 1H over before defending a pregame 1H under. In Spurs-Knicks, a 63-point Q1 left only 48 points needed for Over 110.5, and the same Spurs shot-quality/Knicks defensive-disorganization signal carried into Q2. A full-game under lean is not a reason to keep 1H Under on top once the live phase math has flipped.

WNBA SPREAD/PHASE GATE - ADDED 2026-06-11 FROM SPARKS-STORM PREGAME:
- For WNBA exact-complement cards, separate full-game total, Q1, 1H, and side. A poor offensive team can make full-game under attractive while the opponent's healthier creator group and the weak defense can still make first-half over live.
- When a team is missing a core rim protector/rebounder, split the effect: it may hurt that team's own scoring and cover probability, but it can raise the opponent's early paint, free-throw, and offensive-rebound scoring. Do not treat the absence as a pure under input.
