- For low Q1 thresholds around 41.5, do not bury the over if the stronger offense has recent 45+ and 50+ Q1 environments, even when the weaker team's own recent Q1s are slow. Keep Q1 complements adjacent unless both teams' current starts and availability point the same way.
- WNBA fast-Q1 / full-over caution, added 2026-06-14 from Indiana Fever at Connecticut Sun: a massive Q1 does not validate a full-game over if the 1H model and matchup still point to suppression. Indiana-Connecticut opened with 59 points in Q1, but Q2 produced only 26 combined, halftime was 85, and the final was Indiana 85, Connecticut 75 (160 total). The top-ranked Full Over 170.5 lost while Fever -8.5, 1H Under 88.5, and Q1 Over 44.5 all won. When the 1H projection is below the line and the full-game/H2H band is near the threshold, do not top-rank the full over solely from season PPG or recent high-total games; model Q2/Q4 drought paths and favourite-cover plus under correlation.
- WNBA 1H-over/full-game-regression split, added 2026-06-18 from Las Vegas Aces 86, Phoenix Mercury 76: a strong first-half over can be the cleanest expression even when the full-game over is only a lean. Aces-Mercury reached 94 by halftime, so 1H Over 83.5 won easily, but the second half was only 68 and the full total stayed at 162. When season/last-5 1H averages clear the 1H line but the full-game total sits near the projection, rank the phase edge above the full-game over and explicitly test second-half regression, margin control, and post-Cup-intensity cooling.
- WNBA recent-pace and opponent-star correction, added 2026-06-19 from Atlanta 108, Indiana 101: do not let one slow H2H or a single frontcourt absence suppress a full-game total when both teams' current scoring environment and star creation can reach 95-105. Atlanta-Indiana finished 209 with Atlanta winning by 7 even though the pregame card leaned Fever +1.5 and Under 173.5. Atlanta's all-starter scoring, Angel Reese interior production, and guard/wing creation outweighed the Brionna Jones absence, while Indiana still scored 101. If the projection admits a 175+ path and recent Indiana totals include 200+ environments, keep Over adjacent or above the under and do not bury the road favourite/dog cover solely from home form and prior H2H.
- WNBA same-matchup correction confirmation, added 2026-06-21 from Atlanta 113, Indiana 96: the Jun 19 Atlanta-Indiana lesson repeated immediately. Dream +5.5, full Over 175.5 and 1H Over 89.5 all won; Indiana opened hot but lost cover control through 19 turnovers, Atlanta steals/transition, and five-creator scoring. For Fever-Dream style cards, do not treat Caitlin Clark/Indiana early shot-making as cover protection unless Indiana also projects to control turnovers, defensive glass, and transition defence. Atlanta can cover and the game can fly over even without Brionna Jones.
- WNBA live-start verification reinforcement, added 2026-06-22 from Washington-Minnesota live-state check: before calling a WNBA game "upcoming", open the official/ESPN live feed and record period, clock, score, and starters. If the game has already started, label the card LIVE and let the current score/pace adjust phase and spread probabilities without pretending those possessions were unknown pregame.
- WNBA missing-star phase correction, added 2026-06-22 from Washington 84, Minnesota 79: do not over-weight the first few live possessions or a favourite's recent high 1H totals when the favourite is missing its MVP-tier scorer/frontcourt hub. Minnesota without Napheesa Collier and Dorka Juhasz produced only a 77-point first half and lost outright; live-start cards must net availability, half-court shot creation, rebounding/foul profile, and underdog creator health before ranking 1H overs or big favourite covers.
- Men's T20 live-start collapse rule, added 2026-06-22 from Bangladesh 109/8 vs Australia: if a cricket innings is already live and early wickets/swing movement are confirmed, the live score replaces pregame good-wicket/captain-comment priors for phase and full-innings markets. Bangladesh were 22/3 in the powerplay and 42/5 at 10.0, so the full under and PP under were the real live anchors; do not leave pregame overs on top after a 7/2 or 22/3 start.
- Men's T20 high-venue ceiling discipline, added 2026-06-22 from LAKR 196 and Seattle 115: even at Grand Prairie, 200+ is live but not automatic. A very high PP line such as 63.5 can still be an under when the bowling side chooses to field and has wicket-taking pace/change-up routes; a full-innings 203.5 over needs wickets in hand beyond the opening stand, not just a hot venue label.
- KBO favourite-vs-cushion update, added 2026-06-22 from KIA 11, KT 5: in KBO games with recent 10+ run H2H, active contact/power, and uncertain starters, the over and underdog +1.5 can both outrank a home favourite ML. Do not let home record/H2H leader status push the favourite ML above the cushion when the opponent has a credible multi-run win path and late-inning offense.
- MLB whole-number/pitcher-gap settlement note, added 2026-06-22 from Phillies 6, Mets 2: an ace-vs-traffic-starter gap can make the favourite ML the cleanest listed outcome while the total lands exactly on a whole-number threshold. At 8.0, treat the push band as a major projection outcome; warm wind and recent overs can lift the total to the threshold without making the over clearly stronger than an ace-led favourite side.
- NZNBL elite-opponent pace/blowout correction, added 2026-06-22 from Southland 117, Hawke's Bay 93: do not top-rank high 1H/full unders from one team's recent slow phase history when the opponent is an elite offence with a strong H2H/scoring template and the spread is supported by current class gap. Southland led 62-40 at halftime and won by 24; the Hawks' recent sub-93 first halves did not survive the Sharks' pace/shot-making. For NZNBL, recent phase unders still matter, but require opponent-adjusted confirmation before fading both a high total and a strong favourite cover.
- NZNBL overtime-tail correction, added 2026-06-25 from Southland 102, Whai 96 after OT: full-game totals that include overtime need a close-game tail even when regulation scoring leans under. Southland-Whai was only 81-81 after regulation, but OT lifted the full total to 198 and turned the bottom Over 180.5 into a winner. In FIBA-style domestic basketball, do not treat OT risk as a generic note when the side model already projects underdog resistance or a narrow favourite win.
- Liga Endesa finals phase-variance correction, added 2026-06-25 from Valencia 108, Barcelona 84: same-series slow Q1 evidence is powerful but not absolute. Valencia-Barcelona Game 4 opened at 49 points, finished with 192, and broke the Q1/full under stack because Valencia shot 51% FG and 48% from three while Barcelona's defensive/turnover issues widened in an elimination/title game. In domestic finals, keep Q1 complements closer when the favourite or stronger side has a credible early shot-making/transition burst and the prior series sample is only three games.
- WT20I England elite-ceiling correction, added 2026-06-25 from England 186/7 vs West Indies: a powerplay under and full-innings over can coexist. England were only 46/2 after six, so PP Under 51.5 won, but Wyatt-Hodge/Knight plus West Indies drops and hot, dry Lord's conditions pushed the innings over 175.5. For elite WT20I batting, historical venue averages and one missing star should not top-rank a high full-innings under if the opponent's fielding leakage, boundary pockets and middle/death batting depth support 176+.
- World Cup final-group phase split, added 2026-06-25 from Switzerland 2, Canada 1 and Bosnia-Herzegovina 3, Qatar 1: first-half goal probability and full Over 2.5 probability can diverge sharply on Matchday 3. Switzerland-Canada was 0-0 at halftime but went over after Swiss halftime adjustments and Canada's chase; Bosnia-Qatar had urgency on both sides and hit 2-1 by halftime. Before ranking 1H Over above full-over/under, separate draw/top-group incentives, halftime adjustment paths, and true must-win urgency.
- MLB low-total cushion reinforcement, added 2026-06-25 from Marlins 4, Rangers 2: in low-run roof/park games, a home underdog +1.5 and under can both be stronger than an ace-led road winner. Texas-Miami stayed under 7.5, but Miami's contact form, home/June momentum and Eury Perez return beat the narrow deGrom/Texas winner lean. Protect the broad cushion above exact winner calls when the projected margin cluster is 1-2 runs and the home lineup has a live late-scoring route.
- AFL top-three underdog cushion / favourite-winner split, added 2026-06-19 from Fremantle 99, Geelong 90: a strong home favourite can win and still fail a large cover when the opponent is a top-three side with credible territory/clearance indicators. Geelong +19.5 ranked first and won, Fremantle won by only 9, Over 176.5 won at 189, and Fremantle -19.5 correctly lost from the bottom slot. In AFL side-total cards, treat "favourite winner", "favourite cover", and "over" as three separate mechanisms; a high-total game does not automatically mean the favourite clears 20+ if the opponent can score into the 80s or 90s.
- AFL top-side underdog cushion failure, added 2026-07-10 from Fremantle 111, Sydney 73: the Geelong-style top-opponent cushion rule needs a four-quarter resistance check. Sydney +14.5 looked live at halftime, leading 4.9 to Fremantle's 0.11, but Fremantle's 65-47 inside-50 edge, 39-31 clearance edge and 100-point second-half response turned the game into a 38-point home-favourite cover and 184-point over. In top-four AFL clashes, do not rank a quality underdog cushion above the favourite cover solely from ladder rank, old H2H or public-model margins near 8-15 when the favourite's current home sample contains repeated 30+ margins and a clear territory/clearance surge path. Keep the favourite cover adjacent or above unless the underdog has a sourced four-quarter pressure, territory and scoring-resistance mechanism.
- AFL low-spread favourite-cover alignment, added 2026-06-20 from Hawthorn 113, Gold Coast 97: do not over-apply the large-cover caution to low single-digit lines. When the favourite is already the cleaner winner, has the stronger midfield/territory profile, and the line is only around a goal, favourite winner and favourite cover can be the same best mechanism. Hawthorn -3.5 and Over 175.5 both won; Suns +3.5 was live only through an upset or near-draw, not through the broader "good opponent can lose close" path that protected Geelong +19.5.
- AFL top-half structure versus winner lean, added 2026-06-21 from Carlton 88, GWS 65: when the best two listed legs are underdog cushion plus under, the written winner lean should be capped unless there is a strong favourite scoring/blowout trigger. Carlton +21.5 and Under 184.5 both won while Carlton won outright; the GWS winner lean over-weighted home-field/key-defender absence and under-weighted Carlton current form. If the card's own market structure says "competitive and lower scoring", do not describe the favourite winner as more than a modest lean.
- NZNBL close-spread total fragility, added 2026-06-21 from Otago 91, Taranaki 87: Airs +4.5 won, but 1H Under 86.5 and full Under 173.5 lost after an 89-point first half and 178 final. In NZNBL/FIBA domestic cards, a close-spread underdog cushion can be cleaner than the total when recent phase totals straddle the line. Do not rank a phase under confidently unless the recent 5/10 phase band, roster pace, and shot-quality context all sit clearly below the threshold.

NBA/WNBA SETTLEMENT UPDATE - ADDED 2026-06-11 FROM SPURS-KNICKS AND SPARKS-STORM FINALS:
- Spurs-Knicks Game 4 finished Knicks 107, Spurs 106 after Spurs led 76-49 at halftime. This confirms that phase and full-game totals can diverge sharply: the Q1/1H overs won, but the full-game Under 216.5 still won because San Antonio scored only 30 second-half points. Do not use a full-game under lean to rank 1H Under high when the opening matchup has a credible burst path; model Q1, 1H, and full game as separate markets.
- Sparks-Storm finished Sparks 88, Storm 83: Q1 39, halftime 80, final 171. The StatMuse-first live correction fixed Q1 Under, 1H Under, Storm +7.5, and Sparks narrow winner, but Full Under 169.5 as top and Full Over 169.5 as bottom was still a near-line extreme-slot failure. When the 5/10/15/20 ladder produces an opponent-adjusted full-game band within 1-3 points of the listed threshold, the full over/under pair must stay adjacent and out of top/bottom slots.
- The 5/10/15/20 ladder must be converted into a probability band, not treated as a single average. For Sparks-Storm, last-5 pointed lower, but last-10/15/20 plus opponent defensive adjustment put the full total close to 169.5; that made the full Over a live complement, not a true fade. Phase edges were cleaner than the full-game edge.

PENDING-LOG SETTLEMENT LEARNING UPDATE - ADDED 2026-06-04 FROM ADELAIDE-GEELONG, NC-SAMSUNG, AND PAKISTAN-AUSTRALIA:
- Updated calibration after the 2026-06-08 comprehensive pending-log sweep: approximately 150 settled/closed cards, approximately 349-292-4 listed items, 26 top-pick penalties, 70 bottom-pick penalties, and about 349-388-4 adjusted. Top picks remain strong overall, but the declared bottom pick is still the dominant leak; the latest Antwerp-Akcel and Liechtenstein-Cyprus settlements both punished live bottom outcomes.
- Settlement source discipline: Adelaide-Geelong was confirmed through ABC's live/final report and AFL match context; NC-Samsung was confirmed through the MyKBO final line score and pitcher decision listing. Pakistan-Australia 3rd ODI remained live, but its completed first-five-over markets were settled independently from reliable later scores.
- Void/canceled fixture settlement: Equatorial Guinea-Burundi was listed as canceled at its scheduled kickoff, so the card is void/ungraded. Do not grade canceled fixtures from a 0-0 placeholder, a prediction page, or an abandoned live-score shell. Remove the row from pending and tag it VOID/CANCELED.
- Stalled live-score integrity: Dubai-Partizan initially remained stuck at Q2/halftime on accessible live-score pages. Do not settle a basketball game from a "Final" competition label or from an incomplete table showing only Q1/Q2 columns; wait for a true final source or a full quarter breakdown.
- Basketball 90+ PPG finals correction: Dubai defeated Partizan 99-93 after a 97-point first half. When both teams average around 90+ PPG and recent playoff games show high output, do not make the full-game under top or the over bottom solely because direct H2H totals were lower. Require confirmed lineup suppression, pace suppression, or a current phase trend that survives recent playoff scoring form.
- Basketball domestic-final Game 2 rule: after a Game 1 blowout, separate winner, spread, Q1, 1H, and full total. Immediate Game 1 phase splits and official/credentialed comments about defensive physicality or a blocked early offense should control phase modelling, but a venue switch and large road-favorite handicap can still make the underdog cushion live even when the favorite remains the clear winner.
- Basketball half-point spread caution: Dubai won by 6, so Dubai -6.5 lost and Partizan +6.5 won. If the projected margin is 5-8, rank both spread sides adjacent and cap confidence because the cover can flip on one possession, late free throws, or a backdoor sequence.

PENDING-LOG SETTLEMENT LEARNING UPDATE - ADDED 2026-06-06 FROM BILBAO-VALENCIA, PANATHINAIKOS-OLYMPIACOS, AND GIANTS-CUBS:
- Settlement source discipline: use final sources with period/inning detail, not just a result headline. Bilbao-Valencia required Q1 and halftime markers (Q1 38, halftime 84, final 159). Panathinaikos-Olympiacos required period markers (Q1 24, halftime 59, final 126). Giants-Cubs required the official MLB line/box score because the inning shape and pitcher damage explained the miss.

PENDING-LOG SETTLEMENT LEARNING UPDATE - ADDED 2026-06-06 FROM WINGS-SPARKS, WHAI-GIANTS, AND NORTH-FREMANTLE:
- WNBA exact-complement totals: if the same research stack ranks the first-half Over high because both guard groups/creators are available and efficient, do not put the full-game Under ahead of the full-game Over unless there is a separate late-game suppression reason. Dallas-LA reached a 109-point half and 200 final; the winner/side and early-over read was right, but the narrow full-game under lean contradicted the offensive-availability thesis.
- NZNBL source identity: when NZNBL has Rapid League and main NBL games with the same teams on the same date, settle only after confirming competition label and tip time. Sofascore's nearby Whai-Giants listing was Rapid League; the main-NBL settlement used the separate completed main-game result and quarter table. Keep Rapid/main separation as a hard source-control gate.
- NZNBL phase calibration: high-scoring league baselines do not erase repeated current main-game phase unders. If the freshest same-team main-game Q1/1H splits sit below a high threshold and no confirmed lineup/pace catalyst appears, rank Q1/1H unders first even in NZNBL. Whai-Giants confirmed this with Q1 29, halftime 70, final 127.
- AFL quasi-home mismatch rule: a sold/interstate home game can function as an opponent advantage when venue, crowd/travel, and regional geography favour the listed away side. North-Fremantle in Bunbury showed that Fremantle's WA environment, 57-32 inside-50 edge, ruck/territory edge, and forward quality made the favourite cover and Over positively correlated through one-sided scoring. Do not protect an underdog cushion from stale close H2H when the underdog's recent high totals came mostly from conceding 100+.
- KBO live-total correction: after a home favourite leads by 5+ with seven early runs already scored, do not price the remaining over as a clean 70% edge unless both teams can still add runs. Kiwoom-Doosan finished 9-1 after a 1-6 live state because Kiwoom added nothing after the first inning, Jamsil suppressed the late ceiling, Doosan did not bat in the 9th, and big-lead urgency/lineup substitution reduced the run chase. Missing bottom-9th path and trailing-team offensive weakness must be explicit live-total deductions.
- Basketball playoff phase threshold rule: do not put a half-point first-half under in the bottom slot when the projected half is only a few points above the line and the full-game thesis is under. Bilbao-Valencia landed exactly 84 at halftime, making 1H Under 84.5 an ordinary path, not a true fade.
- Same-series home-response side rule: if the winner lean is the home team in Game 2 after a Game 1 loss and the total projection is very low, do not top-rank the road small-spread cushion unless the road team has a clear late-creation or foul/free-throw edge. Panathinaikos won by 10 in a 126-total game because Olympiacos stalled for an 8-point Q3.
- Basketball under validation rule: a low final total can still coexist with a clear favorite cover. Do not assume "derby under" means "small margin." One-quarter offensive failure can produce both the under and the favorite spread.
- Basketball Game 5 / OT-inclusive finals note, added 2026-06-14 from Olympiacos-Panathinaikos research setup: separate regulation phase evidence from full-game settlement. A double-overtime Game 4 can flip the full-game total over while Q1, 1H, and regulation remain under-shaped. For decisive domestic finals, rank Q1/1H totals from the same-series phase ladder first, then apply an explicit OT/foul-risk adjustment to full-game totals and spread covers.
- Basketball domestic-final spread addendum, added 2026-06-14 from Zalgiris-Juventus settlement: a dominant title-game favorite can win and control the result without clearing an extreme 20+ spread. Zalgiris beat Juventus 95-77, so the winner and full under were right but Zalgiris -23 failed. In domestic finals, keep huge favorite spreads below cleaner winner/full-total reads unless the projected margin is clearly above the line and the opponent has no backdoor path.
- Basketball rivalry-decider full-total addendum, added 2026-06-14 from Olympiacos-Panathinaikos settlement: do not over-anchor a full-game under from same-series phase unders when elite rivalry Game 5 conditions add late-foul, shot-making, and volatility risk. Olympiacos won 89-85, so the winner and Panathinaikos +6.5 were right but full Under 162.5 and Olympiacos -6.5 failed. Treat underdog cushion and full over as live in one-possession rivalry-decider scripts even when Q1/1H evidence remains under-shaped.
- Basketball rivalry-decider PHASE-LADDER INVERSION rule, added 2026-06-14 from Olympiacos-Panathinaikos Game 5 phase settlement (refines the 2026-06-14 OT-inclusive finals note above): the over-tail of a heated winner-take-all decider extends to the EARLY phases, not just the OT-driven full total. The card led with #1 1H Under 80.5 and #2 Q1 Under 40.5 off the same-series under ladder (Q1 Under had hit 3/4, 1H Under 3/4 in the series), but Game 5 opened 26-17 (Q1 43, OVER) and 47-35 (1H 82, OVER), so BOTH top picks lost AND the structural bottom #8 1H Over 80.5 WON. Do not anchor #1/#2 on Q1/1H unders pulled from a series phase ladder in a rivalry/title Game 5; treat early-phase totals as closer to coin flips, lean them toward the series high with the decider, and never park the matching 1H/Q1 over in the structural-bottom slot when rivalry intensity, a fast home start, and foul/shot-making volatility are all live. The full-total/spread version of this lesson is in the rivalry-decider full-total addendum above; this adds that the EARLY phases invert too.
- Cricket OVER-STACK / NO-SAME-DAY-PITCH-REPORT correction, added 2026-06-14 and corrected 2026-06-16 from New Zealand Women vs West Indies Women (WT20 World Cup, Rose Bowl): when no same-day broadcast pitch report opens and the only surface read is a preview label ("good bounce/carry, stroke-makers play freely") while there is new-ball pace assistance under cloud cover against a quality seam/all-round attack, do NOT make the high full-innings over the automatic #1. The original settlement mistakenly imported West Indies' chase powerplay of 35 into New Zealand's innings; the correct Cricbuzz scorecard shows New Zealand's first-innings powerplay was 49/1, so NZ first-6 Over 45.5 WON while NZ innings Over 165.5 LOST at 162/6. Correct lesson: in women's T20, a batting-friendly powerplay and a capped full innings can split. Do not stack full-innings over with powerplay over unless wickets-in-hand/death batting support both; also do not bury the powerplay over when the actual batting side has already shown a 49-run powerplay on the same surface.
- WT20I same-surface regression and bowling-adjustment rule, added 2026-06-17 from New Zealand Women vs Sri Lanka Women at the Rose Bowl: one same-surface powerplay success is not enough to top-rank the repeat PP over when the pitch is on its second outing, the captain has already warned it may get slower, and the opponent's bowling weakness is specifically line/length rather than absence of wicket-taking options. Sri Lanka corrected enough to hold New Zealand to 38 in the first six and 150/6, then chased 153/5. In women's T20, a prior 49-run powerplay on the same ground is a positive input, but repeat PP overs still need current opening-batter stability, no early-wicket risk, and proof the opponent cannot adjust. If those are missing, keep PP Over/Under adjacent or put the full-innings under above the full over.
- WT20I low-threshold innings/first-6 corridor rule, added 2026-06-15 from Bangladesh Women vs Netherlands Women at Edgbaston: when the full-innings threshold sits around 140.5 and the projection is only modestly over, do not bury the innings under if the stated collapse path already lands inside 118-138 or if two powerplay wickets are realistic. Netherlands finished 139/8 and their powerplay was exactly 40/2, so #1 innings Over 140.5 lost by 1.5 runs and #2 first-6 Over 40.5 lost by 0.5 runs while both under legs won. Treat 140.5-style WT20I innings and 40.5-style first-six lines as coinflip corridors unless fresh live surface evidence plus wickets-in-hand clearly separates them; cap #1 confidence and avoid a structural bottom on the complementary under.
- WT20I Bangladesh low-threshold under reinforcement, added 2026-06-21 from Bangladesh Women 123/6 vs Pakistan Women at Southampton: a 130s innings line is not automatically low enough to favour the over when Bangladesh's top order has recent collapse evidence and the opponent has a credible spin/seam wicket mix. Bangladesh fell to 13/3, made only 23 in the powerplay, and still reached 123/6 through Nigar 36 and Shorna 39*; the 133.5 full under and 38.5 PP under both won. For Bangladesh-style batting profiles, rank under first when the success path depends on avoiding a 2-3 wicket powerplay and a middle-over stall, even if late-order recovery can drag the final into the 120s.
- T20 good-wicket powerplay/full-innings split rule, added 2026-06-17 from Bangladesh men vs Australia at Chattogram: a batting-positive toss/captain read can clear a sharp first-six under even when the full innings still collapses well under the total. Bangladesh reached 52 in the powerplay but were all out for 131 as spin/all-rounder wickets controlled overs 7-19. For best-leg parlays, prefer safer powerplay thresholds or wickets/innings-under legs when the wicket looks good but the batting side has availability/depth concerns; do not pair a sharp PP under with a full-innings under as if they are the same risk.
- WT20I elite-opener powerplay caution, added 2026-06-18 from India Women vs Netherlands Women at Headingley: formal seam/green-tinge language is not enough to make a 55-run powerplay under the lean when the batting side has elite openers, dry weather, fast outfield, and a clear class gap against an associate/debutant attack. India reached 59 in the first six, then 209/5. For India/Australia/England-level WT20I batting units, require live swing control, an early wicket, or a clearly weakened top order before preferring a sharp first-six under near 55.
- WT20I elite-batting full-innings ceiling correction, added 2026-06-18 from India Women 209/5 vs Netherlands Women 114: do not convert a preview seam/green-tinge note, long boundaries, or a slower-scoring venue history into a confident innings under when the batting side has elite openers, dry/fast conditions, and the opponent has an associate/debutant attack with catching/extras risk. Postmatch comments described the wicket as flat enough for Netherlands to have a chance, and India still reached 209/5. If the actual pitch report is not opened, cap the full-innings under and keep 185+ tails live for India/Australia/England-level WT20I units unless early live swing or wickets prove suppression.
- WT20I elite-vs-associate 190s ceiling correction, added 2026-06-20 from Australia Women 219/6 vs Netherlands Women at Southampton: do not top-rank a 190s full-innings under from venue scoring dip, cloudy/swing labels, or one missing batter when the same associate attack has already conceded 200+ to an elite side in the tournament and the current elite side still has multiple middle/death hitters. Australia reached 61 in the powerplay despite losing two wickets by 5.1, then Mooney 74 retired hurt, Gardner 58, Wareham 41 off 18, and 58 from the last five overs took the innings to 219/6. For India/Australia/England-level WT20I units versus Netherlands/associate attacks, require live swing control, repeated early wickets that slow the run rate, or a genuinely weakened top/middle order before making Under 190-198 the anchor; otherwise keep PP over and full-innings over live together, with the full under capped rather than promoted.
- MLB starter-gap vs runline-cushion rule, added 2026-06-18 from Mets 9, Reds 1: do not top-rank a home underdog cushion from sweep momentum when the written projection already leans the road team and the road starter has a concrete WHIP/K/current-form edge over a traffic/HR-risk opposing starter. Great American Ball Park can turn that starter gap into a 3+ run road win even when the total projection clusters around 8-10. Model the blowout kill path explicitly before making the dog +1.5/+2.5 the top parlay leg.
- MLB positive-parlay bottom-slot caution, added 2026-06-18 from Mets-Reds and Dodgers-Rays: a weakest positive leg is still the declared least likely leg. If the top half does not sweep and the #4 leg wins easily, record the bottom-slot miss; if the top half does sweep, use the top-half exemption. This keeps best-leg parlay cards honest without pretending every #4 is a true fade.
- MLB injury-managed starter workload rule, added 2026-06-18 from Dodgers 5, Rays 4: a soreness or workload-management tag should lower pitcher-prop confidence only to the extent a sourced innings/pitch cap exists. Ohtani was banged up and not batting, but still threw 6.0 innings, 91 pitches, and struck out 5. For strikeout props, separate role limitation from hitting-lineup limitation and require a formal cap before burying 4+ K style legs.
- WT20I favourite-winner humility addendum, added 2026-06-14 from New Zealand Women vs West Indies Women: do not treat defending-champion status, H2H dominance, or favourite batting depth as insulation for the match-winner lean when the same model already projects only a chaseable 160s first innings. West Indies chased 162 with 7 wickets in hand and 7 balls left through Shemaine Campbelle 90* and Qiana Joseph 53. If the underdog has a credible top-order chase core and the favourite innings model is not clearly 175+, keep the winner lean separate from the innings-over thesis and cap confidence.
- Domestic basketball finals high-offense/top-half rule, added 2026-06-15 from Paris Basketball vs Monaco Game 1: Paris won 95-91 after Q1 50, halftime 103, and final total 186. When current-season scoring, recent H2H, and confirmed lineups all sit above the full-game/1H/Q1 thresholds, do not overcorrect to an under just because it is a final. Overs can stay in the top cluster across Q1/1H/full. Keep side/margin separate: if the projected favourite win is narrow, protect the underdog cushion (+4.5 type) above the favourite spread even when the favourite is the correct winner.
- NZNBL large-favourite cover caution, added 2026-06-20 from Auckland Tuatara 85, Hawke's Bay Hawks 74: a clear favourite winner does not automatically justify a large spread when the line sits above season margin and a current-season H2H has already shown a close backdoor path. Auckland won and the full/1H unders were right, but -15.5 lost while Hawks +15.5 won. For NZNBL/FIBA-style domestic games, rank phase/full unders above a large favourite cover when current scoring form supports suppression and the favourite can win by 8-14 without needing a blowout.
- MLB ace-vs-ace expression rule, added 2026-06-15 from Marlins 4, Pirates 2: Max Meyer and Paul Skenes both delivered run suppression, so Marlins +1.5 and Under 7.5 were the right top two while Pirates ML failed. In ace-vs-ace games, do not let the more famous/home ace drive the winner lean when the opposing starter is equally strong in current form and the opponent is hotter. The cleaner expressions are often the under and the underdog run cushion; keep the home ML below those unless lineup/bullpen/support evidence clearly separates the sides.
- Cricket source-conflict settlement hygiene, added 2026-06-15 from West Indies vs Sri Lanka 3rd T20I live sweep: do not settle from an official-looking or board-hosted scorecard if core identity fields conflict with the verified live match. A West Indies match-centre page surfaced with a final-like score but listed a wrong venue/header, wrong toss, and an unrelated older player pool, while Cricbuzz's live page matched the actual toss, XI, venue, and ball-by-ball state. In cricket settlement, check venue, toss, XI/player pool, innings order, and phase score before accepting a final. If those do not cohere, carry the row open and grade only mathematically settled phases.
- Settled basketball update from Neptunas-Lietkabelis, Nanterre-Monaco, Real Madrid-Tenerife, Bamberg-ALBA Berlin, and Storm-Lynx, added 2026-06-07: settlement discipline matters as much as prediction quality. Never grade from model scores, stale H2H rows, or incomplete live-state fragments - wait for a verified final/phase split. Cantho-Hanoi (initially held pending while sources showed only fixture/prediction shells) was settled in the later 2026-06-07 sweep as Hanoi 87-80 (total 167, Q1 38, 1H 88), adjusted 4-6; see the 2026-06-07 VBA quick-rematch LEARNINGS UPDATE for the anchor-failure lesson.
- Basketball Q1-vs-1H separation: do not automatically bundle Q1 under with 1H/full under. Neptunas-Lietkabelis produced a 45-point Q1 from a one-sided 31-14 start, but the half still landed 83 and the full game 159. A favorite opening burst can clear Q1 over while the longer windows slow down.
- Basketball same-series total regression: Nanterre-Monaco is a major correction. Games 1-2 were high-scoring, but Game 3 fell to 144 after Monaco's defense and Nanterre's shooting regression took over. Same-series overs are not self-validating; require a current mechanism that survives tactical adjustment, venue switch, and verified player participation.
- Basketball availability timing correction: do not let preview absences or stale rotation assumptions drive a top-ranked total/side unless actual game participation is checked close to tip. If the box score or live evidence shows the rotation is different, rerank from actual available creators, defenders, rebounders, and minutes burden.
- Basketball decider side caution: Real Madrid-Tenerife validated the Q1/1H/full over stack, but punished the large-favorite side. When Game 1 was a one-point underdog win and the opponent has elite shot-making, free-throw accuracy, low turnover rate, and late creation, do not let a Game 2 blowout or home-decider narrative push a large favorite cover above the underdog cushion.
- BBL same-venue slowdown rule validated: Bamberg-ALBA confirmed that recent same-venue phase data plus quick-turnaround fatigue can outweigh a high-scoring opener. If Games 2-4 show a slowdown and the same arena produced low Q1/1H totals, rank full-game and first-half unders ahead of the old shootout unless a fresh pace/lineup catalyst appears.
- WNBA favorite-cover/full-under rule partly validated: Storm-Lynx confirmed that a double-digit favorite cover and full-game under can be positively correlated when the underdog offense is fragile and the favorite can create turnovers. But Q1 under can still pair with 1H over if the favorite responds with a pressure/transition Q2 run, so do not rank 1H under too high when that response path is clear.
- MLB rain-delay correction: a delay is not a generic under input after the official first-pitch environment is known. Re-check official post-delay temperature, wind direction, pitcher health/readiness, and recent lineup power. Giants-Cubs had a one-hour delay, but official conditions were 78 degrees with wind out to LF, and San Francisco hit seven homers.
- MLB pitcher-readiness gate: a starter returning from a blister/health interruption cannot be treated as a normal current-form edge without a hard command/readiness discount. Edward Cabrera allowed 8 ER in 3.2 IP; that risk was visible before the card and should have kept Over 9.5 out of the bottom slot.
- MLB top/bottom total gate: if either starter has health/command uncertainty and either lineup has a recent 12+ run or multi-homer path, do not rank Under 11.0 first or Over 9.5 last unless the post-first-pitch weather/park state strongly suppresses carry.
- Fixture identity correction from Tajikistan-India: never leave a row pending after matching only one future fixture listing if the same teams have multiple friendlies in the window. Confirm the exact requested date, local kickoff, venue, and final status. Tajikistan-India was a completed June 5, 2026 match, while a later June 9 listing caused the pending error.
- Soccer goals-vs-corners split: low corner volume does not prove low-goal conditions. Tajikistan beat India 3-1 with only three total corners. Stronger teams can score through penalties, central entries, transition, and defensive mistakes without producing wing pressure or corner accumulation.
- Soccer bottom-total rule: do not bottom-rank Over 2.5 when 2-0 is already a main script and the weaker side has a live late-consolation path. In stronger-home-side friendlies, 3-0 and 2-1 are ordinary extensions of a 1-0/2-0 projection, not extreme tails.

AFL LOW-TOTAL SIDE-AXIS CORRECTION:
- Adelaide 11.9 (75) defeated Geelong 10.14 (74), total 149. The Under 166.5 thesis was correct, but Cats -12.5 lost and Crows +12.5 won. Halftime was only 72 combined, three-quarter time was 106, and the final quarter added 43; this was a compressed game throughout, not a late scoring accident.
- West Coast-Port reinforced the same side-axis rule at a smaller line: Port Adelaide won 11.11 (77) to 10.11 (71), total 148. Under 169.5 won, Port won outright, but Port -6.5 lost by the half-point and West Coast +6.5 won. In sub-170 AFL projections, even a correct favorite/winner lean does not automatically justify laying nearly a goal away from home.
- A low expected total increases the value of a home underdog cushion because each scoring shot is a larger share of the margin. Do not treat "depleted underdog attack" as automatic support for a double-digit favorite cover when the underdog also has an elite defense and home/post-bye resistance.
- Missing forwards can strengthen the TOTAL UNDER without strengthening the favorite SIDE. Separate those axes. The favorite still needs territory dominance plus reliable conversion to cover a large line in a low-total game.
- Direct-matchup conversion is a real side warning. Geelong kicked 9.14 in the Round 3 win and 10.14 in the Round 13 loss. Repeated scoring-shot inaccuracy against the same defense means raw scoring differential and H2H wins may overstate cover probability.
- AFL double-digit away-favorite cover gate: before ranking the favorite line above the home underdog cushion in a projected sub-170 game, require (1) a clear inside-50/territory edge, (2) a current conversion-quality edge, and (3) evidence the home defense cannot keep the contest within two scores. If any gate fails, rank the cushion above the cover or cap confidence.
- Replacement/debutant uncertainty is two-sided. A debutant is not automatically a downgrade; Hugo Hall-Kahan's decisive late tackle showed that role energy and pressure can preserve a depleted team's side value even when its scoring ceiling is lower.

KBO STARTER, OVERLAP-TOTAL, AND LIVE-STATE CORRECTION:
- NC defeated Samsung 6-3, total 9. NC won the hit count 13-5, Koo Chang-mo earned the win, Chun Sa-min the save, and Won Tae-in took the loss. Lions ML lost, Dinos +1.5 won, Over 9.5 lost, and the buried Under 11.0 won.
- Do not let season H2H, team record, hitter-friendly park reputation, or prior-series totals automatically outrank a credible starter's current form. Those are upper-tail inputs; they do not erase an ordinary 8-10 run path.
- On KBO overlapping-total cards, a broad high under such as Under 11.0 should rank above a modest over such as Over 9.5 when at least one credible starter is in strong current form and the low/middle band remains common. If the pregame write-up can honestly say "6-4 or 5-4 is very live," the high under cannot be the genuine least-likely outcome.
- For completed cricket phase markets, an exact milestone display is not required when a reliable later score mathematically proves the threshold. For example, if a team is 23 after six overs, its first-five score was necessarily 23 or lower, so a first-five Under 26.5 can be settled.
- Timezone/date rule for user-facing sports dates: this workspace/request context is Australia/Sydney, and June is AEST (UTC+10). Treat user-facing request dates/times as AEST unless the user explicitly states otherwise. Always preserve the source timezone and convert the fixture kickoff to AEST before deciding whether a game is "today," "tomorrow," already live, complete, or tied to a previous-day local fixture. A prediction request may be for a game in a different timezone whose official local start date is the previous day; do not treat that date difference as a schedule conflict without comparing exact kickoff times.
- Player availability protocol: never convert "expected to miss," "questionable," a preview report, or a generic roster listing into a binary availability fact. Use three distinct states: confirmed inactive, active but role/minutes unconfirmed, and confirmed playing. Confirmed playing requires an official lineup, official box score, or live evidence that the player entered the game. A third-party substitute list is not enough.
- Player availability timing rule: when a request arrives within 60 minutes of tip-off, kickoff, first pitch, or first ball, perform a final-lineup and official-status search before ranking. If the status remains unconfirmed, model both scenarios, cap confidence, and do not make a top-ranked pick depend primarily on that player's absence or return.
- Player absence impact rule: test whether the predicted outcome is robust with and without the player. If an absent player can be replaced without materially changing pace, shot creation, defensive coverage, bullpen usage, or batting depth, do not give the absence major weight. Dubai-Partizan Game 1 showed that Partizan could still score efficiently in Q1 even if Tonye Jekiri was absent.
- Same-series bullpen usage is a risk branch, not a guaranteed late surge. NC-Samsung stayed scoreless on Samsung's side over the final four innings despite the prior day's bullpen workload. Require current bullpen availability, actual traffic, and contact quality before making fatigue the dominant total driver.
- Live contact-state override: when an underdog leads by the middle innings and holds a large hit advantage, discard the pregame favorite narrative and rerank from the live state. NC led 4-3 with an 11-5 hit edge at the logged follow-up and finished 6-3 with a 13-5 hit edge; that was a strong live signal for Dinos +1.5, against Lions ML, and against the aggressive over.
- KBO side hierarchy in close/middle run bands: a credible underdog starter plus +1.5 cushion can be safer than a no-cushion favorite ML even when the favorite has the better record and lineup. Team reputation must not outrank the starter-and-cushion path.
- KBO starter-edge and bullpen-volatility side correction, added 2026-06-16 from Lotte 10-6 SSG: when one side has the clearer listed starter edge and the opponent has both a high-risk listed starter and volatile bullpen/team-ERA profile, do not make the home +1.5 cushion the top pick merely because the game projects close. SSG led early but conceded a 5-run fifth and 4-run eighth; the correct stronger expressions were Lotte non-loss and Over 9.5. Home power can keep the dog live, but it does not protect +1.5 if the pitching staff has multiple multi-run-inning failure paths.
- KBO/NPB starter-edge vs cushion calibration, added 2026-06-20 from LG 3-2 Doosan and Seibu 4-0 Orix: separate "safe cushion in a close low-total game" from "starter edge strong enough to beat the cushion." LG +1.5 and Under 8.5 were the correct top cluster because LG's stronger lineup could flip a one-run game at Jamsil even against Benjamin. But Seibu's Sumida-over-Perdomo edge plus Orix's shutout path meant Seibu +0.5/non-loss should have ranked above Orix +1.5. When the cushioned side can plausibly score 0-1 and the opponent has a major current-starter edge, do not let home/H2H context make +1.5 the automatic #1.

ODI PHASE-MARKET SETTLEMENT CORRECTION:
- A live innings does not keep completed phase markets pending. Settle a first-five, powerplay, first-ten, or other completed phase as soon as a reliable score proves its result, while leaving the full-innings and match legs pending.
- Exact milestone figures are preferable, but they are not always necessary for threshold settlement. Cricket scoring is monotonic: if Australia were only 23/1 after six overs, their score after five overs could not have exceeded 23, which conclusively settles First-5 Under 26.5 as a win and Over 26.5 as a loss.
- Keep the evidence chain explicit. Record the later score, explain why it proves the earlier threshold, and do not invent an exact phase score that the source did not publish.
- The Pakistan-Australia first-five under also validates separating phase drivers from full-innings drivers. Wet-outfield boundary suppression, Shaheen Afridi's wicket at 0.2, and a measured recovery correctly supported the early under even though the full innings remained unresolved.
- Full-innings follow-through: Pakistan-Australia confirmed that the same surface/attack read can carry from phase to full innings when the batting side has already underperformed twice in the series and the confirmed XI lacks first-choice stability. Australia were all out for 157, so the full-innings Under 254.5 won comfortably and the innings Over 254.5 correctly lost from the bottom slot.

MLB MODEST-OVER BOTTOM-SLOT CORRECTION - ADDED 2026-06-05 FROM PADRES-PHILLIES:
- Philadelphia defeated San Diego 6-4, total 10. Padres +2.5 and Phillies ML were both correct, but the bottom-ranked Over 8.5 also won and triggered a bottom-pick penalty.
- A low-scoring recent H2H series can support a total under lean without making a modest over the least-likely outcome. If the favorite has a realistic 5-6 run path through starter command problems, lineup power, park home-run traits, or late relief volatility, Over 8.5 is ordinary rather than a true fade.
- Broad run cushions can coexist with overs. Padres +2.5 won in a 10-run game because the margin still stayed inside two runs. Do not assume "over" means the favorite cover must win or the underdog cushion must fail.
- Starter excellence does not protect the full-game under by itself. Zack Wheeler delivered 7 IP, 2 ER, and 8 K, but Philadelphia still reached 6 and a two-run ninth against Jose Alvarado pushed the total over. Model the favorite offense and late bullpen innings separately from the ace's first 6-7 innings.
- MLB bottom-slot gate: before ranking a modest over last, ask whether either team can score 5-6 by itself. If yes, the over usually has too many ordinary paths to be a genuine slot-4 fade, even when recent H2H unders are strong.

MLB STARTER-SUPPRESSION AND HOME-DOG CUSHION CORRECTION - ADDED 2026-06-05 FROM DODGERS-DIAMONDBACKS:
- Arizona defeated Los Angeles 3-2 after trailing 2-0 into the 8th. The Under 9.0 won easily, while the top-ranked Over 7.5 lost, Dodgers ML lost, and the bottom-ranked Diamondbacks +1.5 won.
- What was right: Justin Wrobleski's current-form signal was real. He worked 6.0 IP, 0 ER, 6 H, 0 BB and 4 K, so starter suppression correctly supported a low-scoring band.
- What was wrong: the card treated Dodgers season offense, Ryne Nelson's season HR allowance, Arizona injuries, and the presumed LAD 5-6 run path as stronger than the actual current starter matchup. Nelson allowed only 2 ER in 7 IP, and Arizona's remaining high-end bats still had late leverage: Corbin Carroll homered, Geraldo Perdomo tied it, and Ketel Marte walked it off.
- Overlap-total rule: if both starters have plausible current-form suppression paths, do not rank a low over first or a broad under third just because one lineup has the better season stats. Put the under higher unless the weaker starter's current form, pitch mix, contact quality, and bullpen bridge all point to a clear scoring break.
- Home underdog cushion rule: in a projected 5-8 run band, a home underdog +1.5 is ordinary and should not be the bottom pick unless the favorite has a clear multi-run separation mechanism beyond "better team." Low totals increase the value of +1.5 because one late swing can flip the moneyline while still keeping the dog inside the cushion.
- Injury-list weighting rule: do not overcount absent depth if the opponent still has active top-end bats in leverage spots. A depleted lineup can still flip a low-scoring game through one or two elite hitters against the bullpen.

WNBA LIVE PHASE-SPLIT CORRECTION - ADDED 2026-06-05 FROM VALKYRIES-LYNX:
- Minnesota defeated Golden State 87-84. The live over/winner thesis worked: 1H Over 82.5 won at 86, Full-game Over 166.5 won at 171, Lynx -2.5 won by exactly the three-point margin, and Minnesota won outright.
- The Q1 model failed separately: Q1 ended Golden State 22, Minnesota 18, total 40, so Q1 Over 41.5 lost and the bottom-ranked Q1 Under 41.5 won, creating another bottom-pick penalty. A correct full-game or first-half over does not automatically imply a Q1 over once the quarter is already in progress.
- Live Q1 arithmetic gate: after the first 2-3 minutes, calculate remaining possessions, current shot quality, foul state, lineup/rotation timing, and points still needed. If the line is near 41-42 and the quarter can land 39-41 through one cold stretch, rank the Q1 over/under complements adjacent or mark the bottom slot forced/capped.
- Repeat what worked: live starters/inactives plus actual early state correctly identified the broader scoring and winner path. Do more of that for 1H/full-game markets, especially when the halftime requirement is modest and the favorite still has enough creation to recover.
- Fix what failed: do not let a broad "current scoring environment over" narrative spill into every phase. Q1, 1H, and full-game totals need separate bands; a 40-point Q1 can coexist with an 86-point half and 171-point final.
- Spread hook caution: Lynx -2.5 won because Minnesota won by exactly 3. When the projected margin is 2-4, treat both sides of a small spread as live and avoid burying the cushion side unless the late-game/free-throw script clearly supports the favorite.

CARD GRADING WEIGHT RULE - ADDED 2026-06-04 FROM USER DIRECTIVE:
- Grade outcomes against the CONFIDENCE they were ranked with, not as flat wins/losses. The four-option ranking is a probability statement (rank 1 = most likely ... rank 4 = least likely), so the feedback weight of each result depends on its rank.
- TOP-TWO FAILURE REVIEW - ADDED 2026-06-10 FROM USER DIRECTIVE: ranks 1 and 2 are the strongest cluster. If either one fails, run serious introspection with comprehensive source research before the next comparable card: verify final/phase result, re-check availability, lineups, weather/venue, tactical/press-conference context, form, matchup data, and source freshness; then add any concrete learning back into this sports doc. If both top-two picks fail, treat it as a major card-level process failure even if the raw W-L record is mixed. This creates mandatory review and learning; the separate top-pick penalty still applies only when rank 1 loses unless another grading rule says otherwise.
- BOTTOM-TWO LOSING IS EXPECTED AND HALF-WEIGHT: if either and/or both of the bottom two picks (ranks 3 and 4) LOSE, that is the predicted result - they were declared less likely - so it counts as only HALF a unit (a "half win" for correctly fading an unlikely outcome, or at most a "half loss"), never a full-weight event. A bottom-two pick LOSING must NOT trigger serious introspection; it confirms the ranking worked.
- SERIOUS-FAILURE TRIGGERS - FULL WEIGHT + MANDATORY INTROSPECTION: if the BOTTOM pick (rank 4) WINS, and/or the TOP pick (rank 1) LOSES, the ranking was wrong at its most confident point, except where the TOP-HALF SWEEP EXEMPTION applies. Treat non-exempt cases as a full failure, apply the existing top-pick and/or bottom-pick penalty loss, and write a SERIOUS retrospective naming what was overweighted, what warning signs were missed, and the one concrete rule change for next time.
- TOP-HALF SWEEP EXEMPTION - ADDED 2026-06-12 FROM USER DIRECTIVE: if every pick in the top half of the ranked card wins, do NOT apply an extra bottom-pick penalty even if the bottom pick also wins. For 4-option cards, "top half" means ranks 1-2; for 8-option cards, it means ranks 1-4. Still record the bottom pick as a normal item win/loss, but no extra bottom-pick penalty or mandatory serious bottom-slot retrospective is needed because the card correctly identified the strongest cluster.
- This sits ON TOP of the existing top-pick-penalty and bottom-pick-penalty bookkeeping (do not remove those). Listed-item W-L is still recorded exactly as before; this rule governs how heavily a result feeds back into process/confidence changes - bottom-two losses are low-stakes confirmation, while a bottom-pick win or top-pick loss is the high-stakes signal that forces a model change.
- Complementary-pair caveat: when the four options are forced complementary pairs (e.g. Over/Under on the same Q1 and 1H lines), the card grades 2-2 by construction and the bottom slot can be a forced near-coinflip. A bottom-pick win there is softer evidence of error than in a genuine four-distinct-outcome card - still introspect on the directional lean and the order, but weight the penalty against the forced structure noted pregame. If the top-half sweep exemption applies, record the bottom pick's normal result but do not add the extra bottom-pick penalty.
- ONE-GOAL-MARGIN CAVEAT (soccer/low-scoring totals, ADDED 2026-06-09 FROM USER DIRECTIVE): when a TOTAL GOALS market wins or loses by a SINGLE goal (a one-goal near-miss on either side of the line - e.g. a 2.5 line landing on exactly 2 or exactly 3), do NOT apply any double/penalty weighting to that result. Grade it as an ordinary single win or loss with no introspection trigger, because a one-goal swing in a low-scoring total is within normal variance and does not signal a confidence failure. The SOLE exception: if that one-goal total-goals pick was the declared BOTTOM pick AND it lost, the standard bottom-pick penalty still applies as normal. In every other slot a one-goal miss is just a normal result. (This caveat is specific to the close one-goal band; a total missing by 2+ goals is graded normally under the rules above.)

SINGLE-QUARTER / SINGLE-PERIOD AND LIVE PLAYER-PROP MODEL - CORRECTED 2026-06-04 FROM MERCURY-STORM Q4 AND BRIDGES PROP SETTLEMENTS:
- SINGLE-QUARTER (or single-period) SPREAD: do not treat a cushion as automatically safest or a lay side as automatically weakest. Period margins are volatile and can widen quickly even in low-scoring games. Verify the exact end-of-period score, current run, rotation/bench matchup, foul/bonus state, rest pattern, and which team must chase. If those inputs do not create a strong side edge, cap confidence. Mercury-Storm correction: Seattle led 55-52 entering Q4, Phoenix won Q4 20-13, Storm +2.5 lost, and Mercury -2.5 won, so the original cushion-first rule was invalid.
- SINGLE-QUARTER (or single-period) TOTAL: anchor to all completed periods and the exact current state, not the pregame full-game line divided by four. Model late-game intentional fouling and stop-clock free throws as a conditional branch, not an automatic over driver. Mercury-Storm Q4 still landed 33 under 39.5 even though the game was close, because Seattle scored only 13 and foul/free-throw inflation did not materialize.
- LIVE PERIOD-STATE INTEGRITY: before grading or ranking a live period, verify the official score at the actual period boundary and reconcile period totals to halftime and final totals. A stale or incorrect live score can invert the side ranking even when the total direction survives.
- LATE-GAME FOUL/FT INFLATION is a conditional, not automatic, over driver: it applies only when the trailing team is within ~one-to-two possessions in the final ~2 minutes and chooses to foul. Model it as a branch (close-late -> over-leaning; controlled/blown-open -> under-leaning), and state the margin threshold that flips it.
- LIVE PLAYER POINTS PROP FRAMEWORK: required-points math is necessary but not sufficient. Estimate remaining shot attempts and free-throw chances from current usage, touches, attempts, role, actual rotation, foul trouble, teammate takeover risk, and game script. Do not convert game clock into scoring rate as if full minutes and season usage are guaranteed. A non-blowout does not guarantee starter minutes or attempts, and a cold/low-usage game sample is meaningful when supported by low volume. Mikal Bridges correction: he had 7 late in Q3, needed only 4 more, and the Knicks rallied in a competitive game, but he finished with 9 points in 28 minutes and Over 10.5 lost.
- LIVE PLAYER-PROP SETTLEMENT INTEGRITY: verify the final player line against the correct date, opponent, and official/reliable game box before grading. The incorrect Bridges 25-point settlement imported his March 1 regular-season line against San Antonio into the June 3 NBA Finals game; the correct Finals Game 1 line was 9 points in 28 minutes.
- For any live in-game card, re-state the live state (score, period, clock, margin, foul/bonus state, who is resting) and rerank from exact remaining-time arithmetic before answering; settle any now-resolved legs (e.g. Q1/1H markets once halftime is reached) immediately for settlement hygiene.

CURRENT-FORM RECENCY OVERRIDE - ADDED 2026-06-04 FROM CROSS-SPORT RESEARCH AND LOG AUDIT:
- Before any ranked card, build a recency stack in this order: last 5, last 10, last 15, last 20, season-to-date, same-lineup/same-role sample, same-venue/home-away sample, comparable-opponent sample, and multi-season/historical baseline. If a window is unavailable, label it UNKNOWN instead of silently replacing it with season average.
- Use recency as a weighted signal, not a slogan. Current form should be treated like an exponentially weighted moving average: the newest games get the most weight, older games still matter, and the weight decays unless a stable long-term trait is being measured. Do not give every game in a multi-season H2H the same weight as last week.
- Last 5 games = alert window. It can flag role changes, injuries, tactical shifts, pitcher/goalie form, lineup chemistry, pace spikes, or bullpen/bench stress, but it is too noisy to override season quality by itself unless a concrete mechanism is confirmed.
- Last 10 games = directional window. If last 10 agrees with last 5 and is opponent-adjusted, it can move totals, spreads, and player expectations ahead of season average, especially in high-frequency sports such as MLB, NBA/WNBA, NHL, NPB/KBO, and domestic basketball.
- Last 15 games = override candidate. If last 5, last 10, and last 15 all point the same way, and the mechanism is visible, current form should usually override historical H2H and stale season averages.
- Last 20 games = new rolling baseline. If last 20 confirms the same direction, treat it as the current team/player state unless there is a strong regression reason, schedule distortion, or confirmed return of missing personnel.
- Recent form may override season/historical only after five gates pass: (1) opponent quality adjusted, (2) venue/home-away adjusted, (3) availability and role matched, (4) underlying efficiency/shot/contact/possession quality supports the raw score, and (5) the line/threshold is inside the recent-form projected band.
- Historical baseline may still win when recent form is unsupported by process: unsustainable shooting/finishing/BABIP/conversion, opponent run was very weak/strong, garbage time inflated scores, schedule was abnormal, sample is tiny, or the roster/role that created the streak is no longer active.
- If current and historical disagree, state the conflict explicitly. Default rule: current form wins for short-term markets when supported by mechanism; historical baseline wins only for stable traits and when current windows fail the five gates.
- Never anchor to historical H2H before current form. H2H is a tiebreaker only after current lineup, current role, current venue, current pace/pitch/weather, and current opponent-adjusted scoring are checked.
- For bottom-slot discipline, the #4 pick must fail the recency stack too. If recent form creates an ordinary path for the supposed least-likely outcome, rerank or call it a forced bottom slot.
- Required output habit: write one compact "recency verdict" before the final ranking: CURRENT OVERRIDES, HISTORICAL HOLDS, MIXED/LOW CONFIDENCE, or UNKNOWN/CAPPED. Include the one sentence reason.

SPORT-SPECIFIC RECENCY OVERRIDE GUIDE:
- MLB/NPB/KBO: last 5/10 starts for starters; last 5/10/15 appearances plus rest/back-to-back pitches for relievers; last 5/10/15/20 games for hitters and team offense; recent Statcast/contact-quality, K-BB%, pitch velocity/mix, lineup spot, catcher/defense, bullpen fatigue, and park/weather beat season ERA/OPS when they agree. A quality starter in current form can suppress totals more than warm weather or bullpen fear can lift them.
- Basketball NBA/WNBA/NBL/FIBA: last 5/10 games for pace, offensive/defensive rating, Q1/1H/2H splits, lineup availability, minutes, usage, and opponent pace. Current lineup/pace and recent phase scores beat old H2H. If recent points rose only through hot shooting while pace/shot quality did not, regress toward season.
- Soccer: last 5/10 matches for xG/xGA, shots, big chances, set pieces, first-half goal timing, lineup strength, squad rotation, and opponent quality. Current XI and opponent-adjusted chance creation beat old H2H and raw recent goals. Low scores vs strong opponents must be lifted against minnows; high scores vs minnows must be regressed against peers.
- Cricket: last 5/10 innings for batter role, balls faced, boundary rate, strike rate by phase, and dismissal pattern; last 5/10 spells for bowlers, economy, wickets, lengths, death-over role, and matchup. Toss, Playing XI, pitch report, dew, boundary size, and current phase form beat venue averages and old H2H.
- AFL/NRL/Rugby: last 5/10 games for scoring, territory/inside-50 or field-position volume, conversion, tackle/pressure, injury list, spine/midfield/key-position availability, and comparable-opponent margins. Current health and role structure beat historical close H2Hs.
- NFL/College football: last 4/6/8 games replace 5/10/15/20 because schedules are shorter. Weight current quarterback/offensive-line/defensive injuries, recent EPA/success rate, pace, red-zone, turnovers created by pressure, weather, and opponent strength above season averages and old H2H.
- NHL: last 5/10 games for team shot share, xG share, special teams, goalie starts, goalie GSAx/save form, rest, and travel. Confirmed goalie and recent shot-quality suppression beat season standings; raw win streaks without xG/goalie support are regression candidates.
- Tennis/Golf/combat/individual sports: use last 5/10 events or matches plus surface/course/opponent style. Recent injury, workload, surface-specific performance, serve/return or strokes-gained splits, and matchup style can override ranking/history; raw win streaks against weak fields cannot.

RESEARCH BASIS FOR RECENCY RULE:
- Exponentially weighted moving averages are useful because they put declining weight on older data, are simple to update, and need limited data, but too much recency weight creates noisy estimates. Practical rule: current data should decay older data, not erase it.
- Soccer Dixon-Coles style models use time-decay weighting because recent matches are more informative than old matches; decay rate should be cross-validated and can be faster in high-turnover leagues.
- Baseball sample-size research warns that stats do not suddenly "stabilize"; they become more reliable gradually. Practical rule: last-5 hot/cold batting lines are alerts, while contact quality, K/BB, pitch velocity/mix, and repeated last-10/15/20 confirmation are stronger.
- Baseball Savant rolling leaderboards and Statcast search support rolling-window checks for expected stats and contact quality. Practical rule: use rolling xwOBA/xBA/xSLG/barrels/hard-hit/velocity as process evidence, not just AVG/ERA/OPS.
- Basketball modeling research commonly blends one-season historical summaries with last-10 current-form features. Practical rule: recent net rating/pace/minutes can override season average only when the lineup and opponent context match.
- Elo-style sports models update after every game; higher update sensitivity gives more weight to recent results but can overreact. Practical rule: use current form to move the forecast, then stress-test whether the move is justified by mechanism.
- Hockey goalie and xG/GSAx work reinforces that raw save percentage or win streaks can mislead without shot-quality adjustment. Practical rule: recent goalie form matters most when supported by GSAx/high-danger shot quality and confirmed starter status.
- Source anchors: Holt exponential smoothing / EWMA (International Journal of Forecasting); Dixon-Coles/time-decay soccer model; FanGraphs sample-size and stabilization library; Baseball Savant rolling and Statcast expected-stat tools; FiveThirtyEight Elo methodology examples for MLB/NFL/NBA/NHL; Journal of Sports Analytics basketball prediction work using recent 10-game and longer-term features.

INJURY AND LAST-MINUTE LINEUP EXCLUSION IMPACT MODEL - ADDED 2026-06-04 FROM COMPREHENSIVE LOG RETROSPECTION AND RESEARCH:

WHY THIS MODULE EXISTS:
- A full-log retrospective shows availability misses are a recurring, distinct failure mode, separate from pace/total/H2H errors. The single highest-leverage variable that changes a game outcome late is who is actually on the field/court/mound, weighted by that player's profile and the quality of the player who replaces them.
- Settlement evidence forcing this module: (1) MLB Guardians 5 - Yankees 4 (2026-06-04): Aaron Judge, NY's MVP-tier bat, was a confirmed pregame scratch (rib-cage bone bruise) on top of Stanton and Dominguez already on the IL; the card had only logged Judge as "day-to-day" and still leaned Yankees as the narrow winner with the "stronger offense", and NY lost outright. (2) WNBA Liberty 97 - Tempo 82 (2026-06-04): the decisive exclusion was on Toronto's side - a thin frontcourt missing Fagbenle/Harrison was punished by Jonquel Jones (22/17) and Stewart, which outweighed New York missing the perimeter creator Ionescu.
- This module is a decision-support overlay, not a medical or workload-prediction tool. It does not authorize medical diagnosis, integrity claims, or use of private health data. It uses only published official injury reports, team sheets, inactives/lineups, toss/XI, and verified beat reporting.

CORE PRINCIPLE - PROFILE OVER HEADCOUNT:
- Never count bodies out; weight each exclusion by player profile and by the replacement-level drop-off (the gap between the missing player and whoever actually fills the role and minutes/innings/snaps). One elite, high-usage exclusion can outweigh three role-player outs.
- Re-price the four-option ranking from the CONFIRMED active lineup at the latest available cutoff. A "day-to-day", "questionable", "game-time decision", or "managing load" tag is NOT confirmation; do not carry the player into the model as if active. If status is unresolved at cutoff, label it UNKNOWN, cap confidence, and state the swing both ways.
- Net exclusions across BOTH teams before ranking. The question is not "who is hurt" but "which side's loss is larger by profile, and does the opponent have the personnel to exploit the specific hole."

PLAYER-PROFILE TIERS (weight an exclusion by tier, then by replacement drop-off):
- Tier A - franchise/MVP-tier or irreplaceable role: primary creator/scorer, ace starter, #1 goalie, franchise QB, lead ruck/midfielder, top-order anchor or strike bowler, talisman forward. Out = re-price side, winner, total, and phase markets; remove any edge that rested on this player; widen the projected band.
- Tier B - high-usage starter / important specialist: secondary scorer, set-up reliever/closer, key rim protector or elite rebounder, lockdown defender, primary playmaking guard, key set-piece taker, frontline bowler. Out = meaningful adjustment to the affected market(s); identify which market it touches most.
- Tier C - rotation/role player or depth: bench scorer, fifth starter, depth defender. Out = small adjustment unless several stack or it forces a cascade (e.g. a thin position group becomes exposed).
- Replacement drop-off modifier: a Tier A out with an in-form, like-for-like replacement is softer than a Tier B out with a rushed, out-of-position, or just-signed replacement. Rate the REPLACEMENT, not just the absentee (see PBA import-paperwork lesson below).

SIDE vs TOTAL vs PHASE - WHICH MARKET MOVES:
- A star scratch usually hits the SIDE/WINNER harder than the TOTAL. The team is worse, but the opponent still scores; the total can stay flat or even go OVER if the opponent supplies the runs/points (Guardians-Yankees: Judge out, NY lost, but total went OVER 9 on Cleveland homers). Re-price the winner/cover down on a star out; only drop the total if BOTH the missing player's scoring is large AND the opponent cannot replace that lost scoring environment.
- An exclusion can make a favourite-cover and a low total POSITIVELY correlated when the missing player was the depleted team's main source of scoring/transition and the opponent owns the matchup (WNBA Portland availability collapse to 66; AFL depleted transition suppressing totals). In that profile, do NOT bury the favourite line AND assume a low over is safe at the same time - one offense fully collapsing can sink both.
- Phase markets need profile-specific reads: losing a primary ball-handler/creator damages FIRST-QUARTER spacing and half-court initiation, not just late depth (NBA Jalen Williams/Mitchell out hurt OKC's Q1 spacing, not only Q2). Losing an interior anchor/elite rebounder swings the game most where the opponent attacks the paint and the boards (Liberty-Tempo frontcourt). Losing a closer/late reliever or a finisher batter most affects the death/late-game and the upper tail, not the early phase.
- WNBA COLD-OFFENSE + FRONTCOURT-DEPLETION UNDER STACK: when both teams' recent Q1/1H phase scores are below the threshold and the more depleted side also loses interior scoring/rebounding, phase unders can be stronger than season defensive weakness suggests. Mercury-Storm confirmed the stack with a 36-point Q1 and 69-point first half while Seattle lacked Magbegor/Malonga. Keep the winner axis separate: the healthier star core can still win narrowly even when the phase-total under is the stronger read.

SPORT-SPECIFIC EXCLUSION GUIDE:
- MLB/NPB/KBO: separate the STARTER scratch (re-rate the whole run-suppression and total read; an opener/bullpen-game replacing a normal starter changes the inning-by-inning model) from a HITTER scratch (re-rate the lineup's run-scoring and the side/winner). A top-of-order or middle-order bat out (e.g. Judge) lowers the team's run expectancy and win probability; the total may still clear if the park is hitter-friendly and the opponent has power. Verify the confirmed lineup card and the actual starter; do not model a listed starter who is really an opener.
- NBA/WNBA/NBL/FIBA: weight by usage and by role (creation vs interior vs perimeter D vs rebounding). A missing creator hurts shot quality and Q1 initiation; a missing interior anchor/rebounder hurts paint defense and the boards; net both teams' outs by profile. Returning starters/defensive identity returns (e.g. a defender coming back) can lower Q1/1H overs as much as an offensive star going out can raise unders.
- Soccer: weight by attacking output (xG/xA, goal involvement) and creation/set-pieces. A talisman striker/creator out lowers expected goals and can lower the first-half and full-game over; but opponent-adjust - a depleted favourite vs a minnow can still go over (Morocco 8-0). Confirm the actual XI, not the projected one; rotation/friendly squads change the profile materially.
- Cricket: weight by role and phase. A missing explosive opener/finisher caps the powerplay/death ceiling; a missing frontline strike bowler or spin controller raises the batting-over read. But a small boundary plus a capable replacement boundary-hitter can keep modest powerplay/innings overs live even after the named player is out (Ekana/Lucknow lesson). Check the toss and confirmed XI before applying.
- AFL/NRL/Rugby: weight by ball-use, key-position, ruck/midfield, and spine roles. A late scratch of an elite ball user or key defender downgrades transition quality, scoring, and cover (AFL Lever out + dry deck; Sydney-Richmond mismatch). A like-for-like ruck swap (Ladhams for Grundy) need not reduce output - rate the replacement. Crisis context (multiple core outs + coaching turmoil) means rank the healthy favourite's cover HIGH, not low.
- NFL/College football: a QB or key offensive-line/edge exclusion can re-shape the entire EPA/scoring picture; weight these far above season averages and stale H2H.
- NHL: a confirmed goalie change (and the backup's GSAx/recent form) can swing the total and side more than a skater out; confirm the actual starter.

CROSS-SPORT PLAYER AVAILABILITY, LINEUP, BENCH, AND MINUTES VERIFICATION PROTOCOL - ADDED 2026-06-05:

PURPOSE AND HONEST LIMIT:
- The objective is not to claim perfect injury knowledge. No public research process can guarantee hidden injuries, undisclosed minutes limits, illness after warmups, late scratches, or coach decisions that are not published. The objective is to prevent unverified availability from being treated as fact and to make every assessment robust to the information that is genuinely unavailable.
- Availability certainty and role certainty are separate. A player can be active but not start, start but be limited, dress but never enter, be named on a team sheet and then be replaced before kickoff, or be available only as an emergency. Never infer minutes, role, or participation from roster presence alone.

REQUIRED STATUS LABELS - USE THESE EXACTLY:
- CONFIRMED OUT: official inactive list, official final team sheet exclusion, official team/league announcement, official transaction/registration status, toss XI exclusion, or official gamebook/scorecard proof.
- CONFIRMED STARTER / CONFIRMED XI: official starting lineup, final team sheet, final 17/22/23, toss Playing XI, official lineup card, or official game center after lineups are posted.
- ACTIVE - ROLE UNKNOWN: listed active/dressed/available, but starting status, bench role, minutes, innings, snaps, TOI, or actual participation is not confirmed.
- LIMITED / ROLE RISK: a published coach/team/official report says minutes, workload, pitch count, snap count, substitute use, bench start, return-to-play, or role will be restricted or managed.
- PROJECTED: probable starter, likely XI, expected to play, preview lineup, morning-skate line, probable pitcher, or reporter expectation that has not reached official final status.
- UNKNOWN: conflicting, stale, inaccessible, untranslated, or absent evidence. UNKNOWN is not IN and is not OUT.
- CONFIRMED PLAYED: official box score, scorecard, play-by-play, substitution log, snap/TOI/minutes record, or live evidence proves the player entered the event.

MANDATORY LINEUP EVIDENCE PACKET BEFORE EVERY RANKED CARD:
- Record the exact research cutoff in Australia/Sydney time and the source's original timezone.
- Record event start time, current event state, and the sport-specific next official lineup/status publication window.
- For every Tier A and Tier B player, record: player, team, role, availability label, starting/bench label, limitation label, replacement, source URL/name, source tier, publication timestamp, and freshness.
- Record both teams' confirmed starters/XI/final team list, confirmed bench or active list where available, confirmed outs, suspensions, eligibility/registration issues, and unresolved statuses.
- Separate "not in starting lineup" from "not available." A bench player may still have a large impact; an omitted player may be injured, rested, suspended, ineligible, or simply a tactical decision.
- Separate "active" from "will play." A dressed basketball player, reserve soccer goalkeeper, emergency AFL player, bullpen arm, or hockey backup may never enter.
- Write an AVAILABILITY VERDICT before ranking: net impact across both teams, replacement drop-off, markets affected, unresolved swing scenarios, and confidence cap.
- If the card arrives before final lineups are normally published, state that the card is PRE-LINEUP. Do not describe the lineup as confirmed and do not let an unresolved player drive rank 1 or rank 4.

SOURCE HIERARCHY FOR VOLATILE AVAILABILITY FACTS:
- Tier A controlling source: official league/competition injury report, official game center, official team sheet, official lineup/inactive list, official toss/Playing XI, official gamebook, official registration/transaction list, or official club/team PR announcement.
- Tier B confirmation source: official team or league social account, official coach press conference, official game notes, official broadcast report, or a credentialed beat reporter directly reporting a coach/team statement, warmup observation, or late scratch.
- Tier C cross-check only: reputable local-language media, specialist lineup services, licensed data providers, or established injury/rotation reporters.
- Tier D discovery only: generic live-score pages, fantasy aggregators, fan accounts, reposts, forums, prediction pages, and unsourced social posts. These may trigger a search but cannot control the final status.
- One current Tier A source is enough for a formal status. Without Tier A, require two independent Tier B/C confirmations for a volatile fact and still label it non-official. If sources conflict, use UNKNOWN and cap confidence.

X/TWITTER AND SOCIAL-MEDIA RULES:
- Use X/Twitter, Instagram, Bluesky, and other social platforms as a speed layer, especially for team PR, official lineup graphics, warmup observations, coach quotes, late scratches, and international/local-language reporting.
- A blue X checkmark is not proof of authenticity; X states that it primarily indicates an active Premium subscription. Prefer a team/league account linked from its official website, a gold organization account, an organization-affiliation badge, or a reporter whose employer profile confirms the account.
- Prefer original posts over reposts, screenshots, or quote-post summaries. Preserve the post timestamp and convert it to Australia/Sydney time.
- Do not treat a player's own social post, travel photo, training photo, or warmup appearance as medical clearance or proof of participation.
- Do not treat a reporter's "expected," "likely," "should play," or "available barring setback" language as confirmed. Translate the wording into PROJECTED or LIMITED / ROLE RISK.
- For late social reports without official confirmation, seek a second independent source. If none exists, label UNKNOWN and describe the possible swing rather than forcing certainty.

SPORT-SPECIFIC CONTROLLING SOURCES AND LOCK WINDOWS:
- NBA: use the official NBA injury report, which is continually updated on game day, then the official game center/box score and team PR. NBA rules require starting lineups to be supplied to scorers at least 30 minutes before the scheduled start. Re-check inside 30 minutes and again at tip if a key player is questionable. A player being active does not confirm normal minutes.
- WNBA: use the official WNBA injury-report page, official team game notes/PR, team social accounts, and official game center.
