Treat probable starters in game notes as PROJECTED until the game center or team announcement confirms them. Re-check close to tip because public reporting is less standardized than the NBA.
- NFL: use official practice and game-status reports, team PR, and the official game-day inactive list. NFL inactives are normally required 90 minutes before kickoff. A player not inactive may still be limited, so check coach comments, snap expectations, and return-to-play context.
- NCAA/college sports: do not assume an association-wide injury-report system. Use official school/team communications and conference reports where they exist. The 2026 March Madness availability reports are a specific pilot with updates due two hours before tip; Big Ten football and other conference systems are competition-specific. If no formal report exists, availability certainty is lower by design.
- MLB: use MLB.com probable pitchers, MLB.com starting lineups, team transactions/injury pages, team PR, and Gameday. A published lineup can still change and is not formally official until delivered to the umpire; re-check for late scratches. Confirm the actual starter, opener/bulk structure, catcher, batting order, and bullpen availability.
- KBO: use the official KBO game center, official KBO registration status, official club communications, and reliable Korean-language local reporting. Confirm starters and the posted game-day lineup; do not infer availability only from first-team registration. Registration, lineup inclusion, and actual participation are different states.
- NPB: use NPB.jp schedule/game pages, official game roster/score pages, club communications, and reliable Japanese-language local reporting. NPB material notes that starting lineups are exchanged one hour before the scheduled start; re-check at that window. Registered roster and game roster do not prove starting status.
- NHL: NHL.com's daily lineup and goalie page explicitly describes lineups as projected. Use it for discovery, then seek coach/team confirmation, official team PR, warmups, and the official game sheet/game center. Do not make a strong side or total depend on an unconfirmed starting goalie.
- Soccer: use the official competition match center, federation/club team sheet, and official club social lineup graphic. IFAB Law 3 requires named substitutes to be given to the referee before the match, but named starters can still be replaced before kickoff. Re-check after team sheets post and at kickoff for warmup withdrawals. Squad call-ups and projected XIs are not final lineups.
- Cricket: use the official board/ICC match center and the captain's toss announcement. The Playing XI at the toss is the controlling pre-match source; an earlier squad or predicted XI is only PROJECTED. For workload-managed bowlers or returning players, use published captain/coach comments and recent overs, but do not invent a restriction.
- AFL: use AFL.com.au teams, official club communications, and the official match center for final teams, late changes, substitute, and emergencies. AFL official final team lists are submitted before the match, but illness and warmup withdrawals can still occur, so re-check close to the bounce.
- NRL: use NRL.com official team lists and club communications. The NRL process names a final 17 one hour before kickoff; confirm starters, bench, positional swaps, and late omissions at that window.
- Rugby union and other rugby leagues: use the official competition/team sheet and club/federation communications. Starting XV, replacements, and late changes are competition-specific; squad selection is not the final team.
- EuroLeague and European/international basketball: use the official competition game center and official injury report where the competition publishes one, then official club PR/social and local-language beat reporting. Many domestic leagues and regional competitions do not have NBA-style mandatory injury reports. A roster listing or substitute list is not proof of clearance, normal minutes, or entry into the game.
- Other sports: identify the competition's formal final-entry mechanism first: tennis draw/order of play and withdrawal notice, motorsport entry list and grid, combat-sport weigh-in/medical clearance and official bout status, or the relevant official event control. Do not import another sport's assumptions.

OFFICIAL AVAILABILITY SOURCE ANCHORS:
- NBA official injury report: https://official.nba.com/nba-injury-report-2025-26-season/
- NBA starting-lineup rule: https://official.nba.com/rule-no-3-players-substitutes-and-coaches/
- WNBA players/injury report: https://www.wnba.com/players
- NFL injury-report policy: https://operations.nfl.com/media/2683/2017-nfl-injury-report-policy.pdf
- MLB starting lineups: https://www.mlb.com/starting-lineups
- NHL projected lineups and starting goalies: https://www.nhl.com/news/topic/game-previews/nhl-projected-lineup-projections
- NCAA March Madness 2026 availability-report pilot: https://www.ncaa.org/news/2026/3/4/media-center-ncaa-releases-penalty-and-process-details-for-march-madness-player-availability-reports.aspx
- IFAB Law 3 team-list and substitute rules: https://theifab.com/laws/latest/the-players/
- ICC official site and match center: https://www.icc-cricket.com/
- AFL official teams and match center: https://www.afl.com.au/teams
- NRL official team-list process: https://www.nrl.com/news/2017/02/28/nrl-team-lists-announcement-changes/
- KBO official game center and registration status: https://www.koreabaseball.com/ and https://www.koreabaseball.com/player/register.aspx
- NPB official schedules/scores and game rosters: https://npb.jp/eng/ and https://npb.jp/scores/
- EuroLeague official game center: https://www.euroleaguebasketball.net/en/euroleague/game-center/
- X verification caution: https://help.x.com/articles/20174940 and organization-affiliation badges: https://help.x.com/en/using-x/verified-organizations/affiliate-benefits

MINUTES, BENCH, WORKLOAD, AND RETURN-TO-PLAY RULES:
- Starting status does not equal normal workload. Search specifically for "minutes restriction," "pitch count," "snap count," "managed," "bench role," "returning from injury," "conditioning," "will not play full game," and local-language equivalents.
- A limitation is CONFIRMED only when attributed to the team, coach, player, official report, or a credentialed reporter quoting them. Otherwise it is a risk scenario, not a fact.
- For basketball, compare last 3-5 games of minutes, starts, substitution timing, usage, and on/off role after the injury or return. For football/rugby, compare snaps or interchange role. For hockey, compare TOI, line/pair, and power-play role. For baseball, compare pitch counts, rest days, bullpen usage, and recent lineup position. For cricket, compare overs, batting position, and phase role.
- Bench depth must be modeled from likely usable rotation, not roster count. Identify who actually played meaningful minutes/snaps/TOI/innings recently and whether the replacement is like-for-like.
- If a key player is active but LIMITED / ROLE RISK, model at least two scenarios: normal role and reduced role. If the ranking flips between them, confidence cannot exceed Low-Medium.

FINAL DECISION GATES:
- Gate 1 - Source gate: no volatile availability fact enters the model without a source, timestamp, and status label.
- Gate 2 - Final-lineup gate: if final lineups should already be available, do not finalize the card until both teams' official lineup/team-sheet sources have been checked.
- Gate 3 - Robustness gate: rank 1 and rank 4 must remain defensible under every plausible unresolved Tier A/Tier B player scenario. If they do not, lower confidence or change the ranking.
- Gate 4 - Role gate: do not treat active, bench, or starter status as proof of normal minutes or usage.
- Gate 5 - Late-change gate: for a live follow-up or a request close to start, refresh the official game center and team PR/social feed before explaining what happened.
- Gate 6 - Audit gate: after settlement, compare predicted availability with the official box score/gamebook. Tag any miss as missing availability, wrong source tier, late scratch, role/minutes miss, eligibility/clearance miss, or replacement-quality miss.

TIMING AND CONFIRMATION RULES:
- Pull availability at the LATEST possible cutoff and re-check immediately before finalizing the ranking. Lineups, inactives, and scratches often post 30-90 minutes pre-event; a "day-to-day" star can be ruled out in that window (Judge).
- Verify clearance/eligibility, not just intent: a player can be "in" but ineligible. PBA import-paperwork lesson (Diouf not cleared in time, TNT played all-Filipino) - a rushed or uncleared replacement lowers continuity and early energy; downgrade accordingly and rate the replacement's readiness, not the absentee alone.
- Do not over-cap from missing personnel when the conditions offset it: missing openers plus tiny boundaries plus capable replacements can still clear modest cricket overs; a missing scorer plus a weak defensive opponent can still go over in soccer/basketball. Opponent-adjust and replacement-adjust every exclusion.

BOTTOM-SLOT AND PROCESS INTEGRATION:
- A late star exclusion can flip what the genuine least-likely outcome is. Re-run the slot-4 check against the CONFIRMED lineup: if the exclusion makes a previously-buried outcome ordinary (or makes the favourite blowout a primary path), rerank before committing.
- Add or refine the post-settlement error tag "late injury/scratch" and the new tag "replacement-quality miss" so availability misses are tracked distinctly from model/ranking, phase/tempo, and variance misses.
- Required output habit: before the final ranking, write one compact "availability verdict": list each team's confirmed OUT/IN by profile tier, the replacement and its drop-off, which market each exclusion moves most (side / total / phase), and the net cross-team effect. If anything is unconfirmed, mark UNKNOWN and cap confidence.

WORKFLOW IMPROVEMENT INTEGRATION - ADDED 2026-05-30 FROM "SPORTS PREDICTION WORKFLOW IMPROVEMENT REPORT.PDF":

INTEGRATION STATUS:
- This is an update to the existing combined sports doc, not a separate-document rewrite.
- The report's advice to split the system into multiple top-level documents is superseded by the current operating choice: keep one combined sports doc plus the existing results log unless the user explicitly asks for a restructure.
- Redundant advice already covered elsewhere in this doc, such as source tiers, data cutoff, phase splits, calibration, model risk, source freshness windows, and sport-specific feature checks, should be applied from the existing sections rather than repeated here.
- Account-dependent advice is not implemented: no sportsbook/exchange account setup, KYC, paid data feed, enterprise provider, account-managed API, stake-limit management, or paid historical-odds workflow should be added unless explicitly requested.

CURRENT CORRECTIONS AND NON-REDUNDANT RULES:
- Ranked four-option cards are a research and audit tool, not automatic betting instructions. If the evidence is weak, stale, conflicting, or too dependent on an unknown lineup/status item, state low confidence or no clear research edge while still ranking the user-provided items if requested.
- Do not rank any outcome lower because a similar pick recently lost. Every ranking must be rebuilt from the current game's inputs: player availability, starter/bench/bullpen/relief quality, team form, venue/park/pitch/weather, matchup context, and source freshness.
- Betting lines and odds may be referenced as market context, but they must not distract from the sports-doc research workflow: ruleset, game state, availability, injuries, lineups, player/unit quality, form, venue, weather, pitch/surface, phase splits, model output, stress tests and prior retrospectives. If the user explicitly requests no odds for a query, omit odds, sportsbook pages, market movement, offered price and fair price. CLV, P&L and stake advice still belong only in an explicit execution note or ledger, not in ordinary prediction research.
- Before final ranking, reduce the analysis to a compact decision object: event, market family, data cutoff, event status, source freshness, availability certainty, key quantitative inputs, projected band, confidence band, main invalidation triggers, ranked order, and settlement plan.
- Add a no-clear-edge state to analysis. A card can be useful for learning even when no item deserves strong confidence.
- Post-settlement error tags should distinguish the main failure mode: stale data, missing availability, wrong source tier, model/ranking error, phase/tempo miss, weather/venue miss, late injury/scratch, bullpen/bench/rotation miss, execution/settlement issue, or variance.
- KPI priority is updated: data quality/freshness first, probability quality/calibration second, price/CLV only when odds are explicitly allowed, and realized P&L/drawdown only when execution is tracked. Win rate alone must not drive process changes.
- Durable rules should be short, decision-linked, and non-duplicative. If a lesson merely repeats an existing rule, update the existing rule or cite it rather than adding another copy.

DOCUMENT ARCHITECTURE:
- Universal core: definitions, data cutoff, source tiers, status labels, metrics registry, methodology, validation, provenance, governance, model risk, privacy, dashboard standards, and maintenance cadence.
- Sport annexes: sport-specific source lists, metrics, lineup rules, phase logic, market rules, venue/weather/pitch rules, and edge cases.
- Results log: operational feedback loop for predictions, outcomes, penalties, settlements, and durable lessons.
- Source registry: list of approved official, commercial, specialist, secondary, local-language, weather, and integrity sources with access, reliability, licensing, and intended use.
- Metric registry: canonical definitions, formulas, units, refresh frequency, and sport substitutions.
- Provenance record: source, timestamp, cutoff, transformation version, model version, reviewer, QA status, and hash/record ID where practical.

DEFINE ONCE, INHERIT EVERYWHERE:
- Define these once in the core and apply to all sports: DATA CUTOFF, confirmed/projected/inferred/unavailable status, source tier, freshness, confidence band, uncertainty band, model version, transformation version, source conflict, validation failure, pending/live settlement status, and retrospective error tag.
- Sport annexes may add stricter sport-specific definitions, but should not weaken core definitions.

CANONICAL STATUS LABELS:
- CONFIRMED: verified by official source, official game centre, official injury report, official team sheet, official lineup/inactive/toss XI/final 17, or verified team/league/beat report close enough to the event.
- PROJECTED: expected based on preview, early team news, last lineup, probable starter, likely XI, or aggregator projection, but not confirmed.
- INFERRED: derived from available evidence, not directly reported. Must be labelled and confidence-capped.
- UNAVAILABLE: source not accessible or data not published.
- CONFLICTING: two or more credible sources disagree. Must trigger source-conflict protocol or scenario split.
- STALE: source lacks timestamp, is older than the relevant freshness window, or predates a material update.
- VOID / NO GAME / ABANDONED / DLS-SHORTENED / OVERTIME-INCLUDED / EXTRA-TIME-EXCLUDED / PUSH-ELIGIBLE: settlement statuses that must be recorded before grading.

MINIMUM OUTPUT METADATA FOR EVERY ANALYSIS:
- report_id or log row reference if available
- sport and competition
- event and market group
- data_cutoff_local and timezone
- data_cutoff_utc when practical
- current event status: pregame, live, final, postponed, void, abandoned, unknown
- source note: official status source, availability source, statistical source, context source
- availability labels: confirmed, projected, inferred, unavailable, or conflicting
- projected band for the relevant market
- confidence band and main uncertainty driver
- top-pick gate result and bottom-pick gate result
- main risk that would flip the ranking
- retrospective tag after settlement

METHODOLOGY LAYER:
- Separate descriptive, diagnostic, predictive, and prescriptive analytics.
- Descriptive analytics: what happened, such as score, pace, scoring split, lineup, injury, workload, venue, weather, or pitch state.
- Diagnostic analytics: why it happened, such as shot quality, powerplay wickets, bullpen exposure, turnover pressure, foul/bonus state, weather carry, or tactical adjustment.
- Predictive analytics: what is likely next, using historical and current inputs before the cutoff.
- Prescriptive analytics: what action or recommendation follows, such as rank order, pass/no clear edge, rerank live, or cap confidence.
- Do not use one metric as both descriptive fact and causal proof without supporting context.
- Always compare current evidence against historical evidence. Current confirmed status, current pitch/weather/venue, and current lineup usually beat older averages.
- Every advanced model must be benchmarked against a transparent baseline.
- Every forecast should carry uncertainty, source freshness, and a clear failure path.

FEATURE CONSTRUCTION RULES:
- Define the sample window before analysis: last 3, last 5, last 10, season, same venue, same matchup, same competition, same phase, same lineup/availability state, same weather/pitch/roof state, or same tactical context.
- Separate full-game, period/phase, side, spread/handicap, total, player prop, team total, live-state, and futures features.
- Adjust for opponent strength, venue, rest/travel, schedule density, lineup quality, injury/availability, role changes, weather/pitch/roof, referee/umpire/officials where relevant, and competition context.
- Do not mix regular season, playoffs, preseason, reserve/youth/women's competitions, friendlies, Rapid League/curtain-raiser formats, or cup matches unless explicitly modelled.
- Use rolling windows and long-term quality together. Recent form can be real, but sample size and opponent quality must be visible.

PLAYER RECENT-FORM VS HISTORICAL-BASELINE RULE - ADDED 2026-05-28:

- For any pick or team analysis materially driven by individual players, collect both current rolling-window stats and historical baseline stats before upgrading or downgrading that player.
- Required rolling windows when data exists: last 5, last 10, last 15, and last 20 games/starts/appearances, plus season-to-date and career or multi-season baseline.
- Do not blend rolling and historical stats into one generic "form" note. Label each key player as HOT CURRENT, COLD CURRENT, STABLE, POSITIVE REGRESSION, NEGATIVE REGRESSION, ROLE-CHANGED, SAMPLE TOO SMALL, or INJURY/WORKLOAD-AFFECTED.
- Recent stats may override historical reputation only when supported by a real mechanism: role or usage change, lineup/minutes/innings change, injury recovery or limitation, pitch mix or velocity change, shot profile/contact-quality change, opponent quality, venue effect, or confirmation across multiple rolling windows.
- Historical baseline may soften a bad short run when role, health, usage, and underlying quality remain stable and the recent decline looks variance-driven.
- Historical baseline must not excuse current decline when last 10/15/20 windows all confirm deterioration and there is a plausible cause such as injury, age, velocity loss, command loss, reduced minutes, lineup demotion, role loss, or weaker contact/shot quality.
- Always adjust rolling-window player stats for opponent strength, venue, home/away split, rest, role, and competition level. A hot streak against weak opponents or in an extreme venue must be discounted.
- If only season/career data is available and rolling data cannot be found, mark rolling player form UNKNOWN and cap confidence instead of assuming the player is still performing to reputation.

METRICS REGISTRY - PERFORMANCE:
- Win rate: wins / games played. Unit: percent. Refresh: post-match and rolling daily.
- Scoring differential: points/goals/runs scored minus conceded per game. Unit: sport-specific points/goals/runs. Refresh: post-match.
- Opponent-adjusted rating: team performance adjusted for opponent quality and venue. Unit: rating points. Refresh: daily/weekly.
- Offensive efficiency: scoring output divided by possessions, drives, innings faced, overs, plays, entries, or equivalent opportunities. Unit: points/runs/goals per opportunity. Refresh: match and rolling.
- Defensive efficiency: scoring conceded divided by opponent possessions or equivalent opportunities. Unit: points/runs/goals allowed per opportunity. Refresh: match and rolling.
- Net rating: offensive efficiency minus defensive efficiency. Unit: rating points. Refresh: match and rolling.
- Pace/tempo: possessions, plays, entries, balls faced, drives, or attacks per minute or match phase. Unit: events per minute or per phase. Refresh: match/live.
- Shot/contact/attempt quality index: expected scoring value of attempts divided by attempts. Unit: expected points/goals/runs per attempt. Refresh: match and rolling.
- Conversion over expectation: actual scoring minus expected scoring. Unit: raw and per match. Refresh: match and rolling.
- Clutch efficiency: high-leverage efficiency divided by baseline efficiency. Unit: index. Refresh: match/season.
- Phase split efficiency: quarter, half, innings phase, powerplay, middle overs, death overs, special teams, red zone, bullpen-entry, or other sport-specific phase efficiency. Unit: sport-specific. Refresh: live and post-match.
- Rest/travel disadvantage: function of days rest, time-zone change, travel distance, turnaround, venue familiarity, and schedule density. Unit: index. Refresh: pre-match.

METRICS REGISTRY - PLAYER AND ROLE:
- Usage share: touches, possessions used, target share, shot share, plate appearances, balls faced, or involvement divided by team total. Unit: percent. Refresh: match and rolling.
- Expected contribution: xG + xA, OBV, xwOBA-derived run value, expected value added, or equivalent event value. Unit: expected value. Refresh: match and rolling.
- On/off differential: team net rating or phase rating with player on field/court/ice minus off. Unit: rating points. Refresh: match/season.
- Availability-adjusted value: player value metric divided by availability share. Unit: value per available game/session. Refresh: weekly.
- Role versatility index: entropy across positions, roles, line combinations, batting positions, bowling phases, defensive roles, or deployment contexts. Unit: index. Refresh: monthly/season.
- Progression slope: slope of performance percentile over time. Unit: percentile points per 90 days or season. Refresh: monthly.
- Reliability index: minutes/innings/snaps/TOI/games played, injury burden, and role stability combined. Unit: index. Refresh: monthly.

METRICS REGISTRY - SEASON AND LONGITUDINAL:
- Strength-of-schedule-adjusted record: actual results normalized by opponent strength. Unit: wins, standings points, or rating. Refresh: weekly.
- Variability index: coefficient of variation in scoring differential or efficiency. Unit: coefficient. Refresh: weekly.
- Trend delta: current rolling window minus prior rolling window. Unit: raw or z-score. Refresh: weekly.
- Aging/development curve: estimated performance change by age and exposure. Unit: expected value change. Refresh: monthly/season.

METRICS REGISTRY - INJURY, FITNESS, WORKLOAD, AND SURVEILLANCE:
- Injury incidence: new injuries / exposure hours x 1,000. Unit: injuries per 1,000 hours. Refresh: weekly/monthly.
- Time-loss burden: days lost / exposure hours x 1,000. Unit: days lost per 1,000 hours. Refresh: weekly/monthly.
- Availability rate: eligible games or training sessions attended / scheduled. Unit: percent. Refresh: daily/weekly.
- Return-to-play latency: return date minus injury date. Unit: days. Refresh: case-based.
- Acute load: sum of external/internal load over last 7 days. Unit: AU, km, PlayerLoad, TRIMP, sRPE-min, or sport-specific. Refresh: daily.
- Chronic load: rolling 28-day or EWMA load. Unit: same as acute load. Refresh: daily.
- ACWR: acute load / chronic load. Unit: ratio. Refresh: daily, but interpret cautiously and never alone.
- Monotony: mean daily load over 7 days / standard deviation of daily load over 7 days. Unit: ratio. Refresh: weekly.
- Strain: weekly load x monotony. Unit: load units. Refresh: weekly.
- High-speed running load: distance above sport-specific velocity threshold. Unit: meters. Refresh: session/daily.
- Acceleration/deceleration exposure: count or total magnitude above threshold. Unit: count or load. Refresh: session/daily.
- Readiness composite: weighted z-score of wellness, HRV, sleep, soreness, neuromuscular test, and recent load. Unit: index. Refresh: daily.
- Reconditioning gap: current performance or load tolerance minus pre-injury baseline. Unit: percent or z-score. Refresh: daily during return-to-play.
- Exposure completeness: recorded exposure / expected exposure across squad and sessions. Unit: percent. Refresh: daily.
- Medical coding completeness: injuries with standard diagnosis code and severity recorded / total injuries. Unit: percent. Refresh: weekly.

WORKLOAD AND INJURY MODEL CAUTION:
- Workload metrics support surveillance, not deterministic injury prediction.
- ACWR and similar load ratios must not be used alone.
- Injury risk models need uncertainty, confounder control, exposure completeness, injury history, return-to-play stage, travel, age, and role context.
- Do not make automated medical, selection, contract, or availability decisions from workload models without human review.

METRICS REGISTRY - FINANCE, SCOUTING, AND INTEGRITY:
- Payroll efficiency: wins, standings points, or net rating divided by payroll. Unit: results per currency unit. Refresh: monthly/season.
- Wage-to-revenue ratio: player wages / operating revenue. Unit: percent. Refresh: quarterly/annual.
- Budget variance: actual spend minus planned spend. Unit: currency. Refresh: monthly.
- Valuation gap: internal model value minus external reference value. Unit: currency. Refresh: monthly/window.
- Transfer or acquisition ROI: on-field value or net proceeds generated minus acquisition and wage costs. Unit: currency or value index. Refresh: window/annual.
- Age-relative percentile: player percentile within age cohort and competition. Unit: percentile. Refresh: monthly.
- League translation factor: performance adjusted for league-strength differential. Unit: multiplier. Refresh: quarterly.
- Similarity score: distance to matched player archetypes across skill, physical, tactical, and role vectors. Unit: 0-100 similarity. Refresh: monthly.
- Suspicious alert rate: integrity alerts / events monitored x 1,000. Unit: alerts per 1,000 events. Refresh: monthly/quarterly.
- Odds-source disagreement: standard deviation of implied probabilities across regulated books. Unit: percentage points. Refresh: intraday where integrity monitoring is authorized.
- Unusual movement z-score: observed market movement minus baseline movement divided by historical standard deviation. Unit: z-score. Refresh: intraday where integrity monitoring is authorized.
- Escalation status: none, review, urgent review, regulator referral. Unit: category. Refresh: real time.

FINANCE, SCOUTING, AND INTEGRITY CAUTION:
- Public valuation sources such as Transfermarkt are benchmark signals, not official transaction truth.
- Use public valuations alongside official contracts, salary-cap constraints, club disclosures, and internal valuation models.
- Integrity monitoring must be segregated from predictive analysis. Suspicious betting alerts belong to risk/compliance, not ordinary match prediction.
- Do not use integrity alerts as betting handicapping evidence.

SOURCE REGISTRY AND TIERS:
- Tier 0 - current official event control: official league/team match centre, official injury report, official lineup/team sheet/final 17/inactives, toss/Playing XI, official scorecard, official gamebook, official venue/roof/weather status, official broadcast pitch report.
- Tier 1 - official or primary stat data: official league stats, MLB Statcast/Baseball Savant, NBA/WNBA official stats, NFL official injury reports and Next Gen Stats, NHL official stats, AFL/NRL official stats, official cricket scorecards, NPB.jp, KBO/Naver official-style pages, and official club/federation stat pages.
- Tier 2 - licensed commercial event/tracking/providers: Stats Perform Opta, Hudl StatsBomb, Sportradar, Catapult, STATSports, WIMU-type wearable systems, and other credentialed feeds where licensed.
- Tier 3 - specialist public analytics and databases: FanGraphs, Baseball Reference, Pro Football Reference, FBref/StatsBomb public data, Understat, Natural Stat Trick, MoneyPuck, Wheelo Ratings, Squiggle, AFL Tables, FootyWire, Statiz, SportsNavi/Yahoo Japan, RealGM, Proballers, FIBA LiveStats, and league-specific historical databases.
- Tier 4 - verified news and local reporting: official club accounts, team PR, verified beat reporters, reputable local-language outlets, press conferences, game notes, trusted local recaps.
- Tier 5 - aggregators and live-score pages: ESPN, CBS, FOX, Flashscore, SofaScore, Livesport, RotoWire, Lineups.com, FantasyPros, Covers score pages, and similar pages. Use for speed and cross-checking, not final volatile truth.
- Tier 6 - prediction, betting, fan, and forum content: betting previews, tipster pages, unsourced social posts, forum comments, model-pick pages. Do not use in research-only mode. Use only to discover a fact that must be verified from a higher tier.

SOURCE REGISTRY FIELDS:
- source_name
- source_tier
- sport/competition coverage
- best_use
- access_level: public, credentialed, licensed, internal, membership, unavailable
- reliability_level
- official_status: official, commercial, specialist, aggregator, local-reporting, integrity, internal
- refresh_cadence
- latency_expectation
- licensing_note
- privacy_class
- fallback_sources
- known_limitations
- last_audit_date

DATA QUALITY VALIDATION STACK:
- Identity: every event, team, player, competition, venue, season, source, and market must have a stable primary key. If missing, reject or quarantine the record.
- Time: store source timestamp and extraction timestamp in UTC; preserve local venue time as display field. If malformed, reject or normalize.
- Cutoff integrity: pregame analysis may only use data available at or before documented cutoff. If violated, hard fail the snapshot and tag leakage.
- Status labeling: every availability field must be confirmed, projected, inferred, unavailable, conflicting, or stale. Unlabeled status cannot enter output.
- Competition integrity: do not mix competitions, preseason, reserve leagues, women's leagues, youth levels, Rapid League/curtain-raiser formats, friendlies, or cup matches unless explicitly modelled. If mixed, hard fail or partition.
- Arithmetic consistency: team totals must reconcile to player totals within tolerance; phase splits must sum to final totals; innings/quarter/period/half lines must match final result. If not, reject and log exception.
- Impossible values: reject negative minutes, impossible scores, missing opponents, invalid lineups, player on two teams at once, impossible innings/overs, impossible period totals, or invalid settlement states.
- Missingness thresholds: if a critical field exceeds missingness threshold, suppress dependent metric and mark unavailable.
- Source conflict: if Tier 0/Tier 1 and lower source disagree on operational truth, higher tier wins unless a logged exception is approved.
- Wearable synchronization: device clock, unit assignment, sampling rate, and session boundaries must be synchronized before aggregation. Exclude affected sessions if failed.
- Leakage prevention: post-event facts must not appear in pre-event feature sets. If leakage is found, fail training/scoring pipeline.
- Version control: every transformation must carry a code version, ruleset version, or manual-rule version. If absent, fail data release or mark non-production.
- Schema drift: if source columns, labels, IDs, or field meanings change, quarantine until mapping is reviewed.
- Duplicate detection: event, player, team, and source records must be de-duplicated before aggregation.
- Unit consistency: convert and record units for distance, speed, load, currency, time, weather, and scoring before comparison.
- Settlement rule: record whether overtime, extra innings, super over, penalties, DLS, abandoned games, voids, pushes, and shortened games count before grading.

DATA CUTOFF AS STRUCTURED FIELD:
- DATA CUTOFF is not only a narrative reminder; it is a required field in every analysis and model snapshot.
- Store data_cutoff_local, timezone, data_cutoff_utc, extraction_timestamp_utc, and source_timestamp_utc when practical.
- Later facts may be used only for live correction, settlement, retrospective learning, and model validation, not original pregame reasoning.

PENDING/LIVE/INCOMPLETE SCHEMA:
- Every log row must carry status: pregame, live, final, pending, partially settled, void, abandoned, postponed, no game, unresolved source conflict, or unknown.
- Pending rows must carry next_check_trigger: next user request, final score available, official scorecard posted, phase split posted, settlement rule clarified, or source conflict resolved.
- Retrospectives must replace old live snapshots after final settlement.

MODEL AND ANALYTICS HIERARCHY:
- Baseline first: every advanced prediction should be compared with a transparent baseline such as Elo/Glicko, simple rating, season average, venue-adjusted average, or sport-appropriate score model.
- Match outcome forecasting: Elo/Glicko, hierarchical Bayesian models, Poisson/negative-binomial score models, and gradient boosting for nonlinear features. Required features: opponent strength, venue, rest, lineup, injuries, pace, recent form, officials/weather where relevant.
- Margin and total forecasting: regularized regression, Bayesian regression, gradient boosting, and quantile regression. Required features: pace, efficiency, lineups, rest, weather/park/venue, tactical context, phase splits.
- Live win probability: state-space, Markov, possession/drive/ball-by-ball models. Required features: score state, time remaining, possession/phase, fouls/wickets/bullpen/bench state. Must be separate from pregame model.
- Player valuation: gradient boosting, elastic net, Bayesian multilevel models, and SHAP-style explanations. External market values are features, not truth.
- Injury risk: survival models, recurrent-event models, Bayesian updating, and carefully calibrated gradient boosting. Must document uncertainty and avoid ACWR-only models.
- Lineup optimization: mixed-integer optimization, stochastic programming, and simulation. Must include coachable constraints, availability, minutes caps, role fit, opponent matchup, fatigue, substitution rules, and salary/squad constraints where relevant.
- Tactical pattern mining: clustering, sequence mining, graph methods, and spatio-temporal models. Must validate tracking accuracy and feed quality.
- Uncertainty estimation: conformal prediction, Bayesian posterior intervals, bootstrap ensembles, quantile intervals, and historical residual bands. Make uncertainty visible to users.

PRACTICAL MODEL BLEND FOR RANKED CARDS:
- Every serious ranked card should produce a transparent probability table from three layers before the final narrative ranking: (1) baseline, (2) sport-specific score distribution, and (3) machine-learning adjustment. If time/source coverage is thin, run layers 1-2 and label ML layer UNAVAILABLE rather than guessing.
- Baseline layer: build the simplest defensible projection from recent scoring for/against, opponent quality, venue/home-away, and phase split. This prevents the ML or narrative layer from drifting away from obvious arithmetic.
- Distribution layer: use the sport-appropriate scoring model to convert the projected mean into market probabilities. Soccer/hockey goals use Poisson or Dixon-Coles/bivariate Poisson; baseball/cricket runs often need negative-binomial or simulation because scoring is more overdispersed; basketball/AFL/NRL totals generally use normal/quantile or possession-based simulation unless a count model has been validated for that league. For phase markets, fit a separate phase mean instead of scaling the full-game mean blindly.
- ML adjustment layer: use regularized regression, gradient boosting, random forest, or calibrated ensemble only as an adjustment to the baseline/distribution, not as a black box. Useful features include lineup strength, injuries/role limits, rest/travel, venue/weather, referee/umpire, tactical style, shot/attempt quality, pace, recent form deltas, and live state. Explain the top positive and negative drivers in plain language.
- Probability calibration: after the raw model output, apply calibration from historical buckets and current log performance. If the model says 65% but similar prior 60-69% picks are underperforming, downgrade the displayed confidence and state the cap.
- Correlation and complement rules: for exact over/under pairs, probabilities must sum to about 100% after accounting for pushes/voids; do not rank both sides as if independent. For overlapping lines, explicitly model the middle band where both can win or both can lose/push.
- Live update rule: once a game is live, replace pregame distribution with conditional probability using current score, time/overs/possessions remaining, live pace, wickets/fouls/cards/bench state, and required rate. Pregame priors become modifiers only.
- Output rule: show the final probabilities or bands for each provided option, then rank. If no option clears the #1 threshold or the #1/#4 gap is small, call it LOW-EDGE/FORCED RANKING.

MODEL RISK CONTROLS:
- Document model purpose, intended use, excluded use, assumptions, feature list, training window, validation window, target variable, settlement rules, and known blind spots.
- Use walk-forward or time-aware validation for time-series sports predictions; do not random-shuffle future and past seasons for production validation.
- Track calibration and discrimination separately. Use Brier score, log loss, calibration curves/reliability tables, hit rate by probability bucket, and error tags.
- Monitor drift in source schema, feature distributions, missingness, calibration, residuals, confidence buckets, sport/market type, and source latency.
- Define retraining/recalibration triggers: sustained calibration miss, source schema change, major rule change, roster/competition format change, new season, new provider, or repeated top/bottom ranking penalties.
- Archive superseded model versions, ruleset versions, and source mappings.
- No fully automated adverse decision on athlete availability, contract, integrity escalation, or medical status without human review.
- Human override must record reason, reviewer, timestamp, evidence, and whether model/rules need updating.

CONFIDENCE AND UNCERTAINTY DISPLAY:
- Confidence must reflect evidence strength, source freshness, status labels, sample size, phase specificity, source conflict, and model calibration.
- Do not call a pick safe, guaranteed, dead, or impossible.
- Use confidence bands: Low, Low-Medium, Medium, Medium-High, High. High should be rare.
- If status is projected or conflicting on a key variable, maximum confidence is Low-Medium unless the ranking is insensitive to both scenarios.
- Display projected band before ranking. For overlapping options, rank by band coverage, not by treating options as opposites.
- For dashboards, show confidence and uncertainty visually with bands, intervals, and freshness flags, not only a single number.

DASHBOARD AND VISUALIZATION STANDARDS:
- Every dashboard or report must show: data cutoff, competitions covered, last refresh, confidence/uncertainty, source tier, unresolved conflicts, and model/watchlist alerts.
- Executive summary: key findings, biggest risks, confidence, cutoff, pending items.
- Team performance panel: rolling net rating, SOS-adjusted form, scoring differential, offensive/defensive efficiency, trend deltas.
- Player availability panel: confirmed/projected/unavailable, injuries, return-to-play, minutes caps, starters, bench/role changes.
- Model watchlist: drift alerts, missingness, leakage checks, calibration alerts, parser failures, unresolved source conflicts.
- Match-state and phase panel: quarter/half/innings/powerplay/death/bullpen/special-teams/red-zone splits, venue/weather/pitch/schedule context.
- Player impact and workload panel: on/off, usage, load vs readiness, minutes/snap/TOI caps, training load, reconditioning gap.
- Valuation and scouting panel: internal value, external benchmark, similarity comps, contracts, league translation, reliability.
- Integrity and governance panel: provenance, source tier, audit trail, unresolved conflicts, privacy class, escalation status.

VISUALIZATION LIBRARY:
- KPI tiles with sparkline and confidence band: executive overview. Avoid raw values without context or trend.
- Rolling trend line with event annotations: form, fatigue, injury return, lineup changes. Avoid ignoring schedule strength and availability.
- Phase split waterfall: quarters, halves, innings phases, powerplays, bullpen entry, special teams, red zone. Avoid relying on full-game total alone.
- Shot map, spray chart, or zone chart: shot/contact quality and tactical tendencies. Avoid over-reading small samples.
- On/off differential bar or ridge plot: player impact and lineup combinations. Avoid ignoring opponent context.
- Scatter of workload vs readiness: load management and return-to-play. Avoid treating correlation as causal certainty.
- Schedule density and travel heatmap: fatigue risk. Include time zones and turnaround windows.
- Valuation waterfall: contract, transfer, and asset decisions. Avoid mixing model value with rumor value.
- Integrity anomaly plot: compliance view only. Do not feed directly into match predictions.

PROVENANCE AND CITATION STANDARD:
- Use W3C PROV concepts: entities, activities, and agents.
- Entity: the data, metric, model output, report, source record, game, player, team, or market item.
- Activity: extraction, cleaning, transformation, modelling, review, settlement, retrospective, or publication.
- Agent: analyst, reviewer, approver, source provider, model, script, or system.
- Every material claim should be reproducible from source, timestamp, formula/ruleset, sample window, and cutoff.

GOVERNANCE, PRIVACY, ETHICS, AND SECURITY:
- Data classes: public, internal, confidential, medical/special-category, integrity-sensitive, commercial-licensed, personal data, derived model output.
- Access control: role-based, least privilege, separate medical, scouting, integrity, and prediction views where practical.
- Lawful basis: document applicable lawful basis for personal data processing and special-category/health-like data where relevant.
- Wearables and monitoring: do not rely automatically on consent in employment/team contexts. Document whether participation is optional, what data are collected, what happens if an athlete declines, and which decisions can or cannot be made from the data.
- Purpose limitation: use data only for the purpose recorded in the source registry or consent/lawful-basis record.
- Data minimization: collect only the fields needed for the stated purpose.
- Accuracy: keep status fresh, label uncertainty, and correct known errors.
- Retention: define retention by class: operational event data, medical data, scouting notes, integrity alerts, model snapshots, source extracts, and reports.
- Auditability: every report should store source, timestamp, transformation version, reviewer, and final approver where practical.
- Bias mitigation: test models by competition, role, gender, age band, league strength, injury history, position, and data coverage; document known blind spots.
- Human oversight: no fully automated adverse decision on athlete availability, contract, medical, or integrity escalation.
- Model lifecycle: validation window, recalibration rules, drift thresholds, retraining triggers, archive of superseded models.
- Security: encryption at rest and in transit where systems support it, MFA, backups, recovery testing, access logs, source-token protection, and separation of duties.

LIMITATIONS TO STATE WHEN RELEVANT:
- Missing data
- Stale data
- Source conflict
- Provider bias
- Survivorship bias
- Small sample
- Competition/league-mix risk
- Unverified lineup/injury/availability
- Uncertain settlement rules
- Model drift
- Rule changes
- Weather/pitch/venue uncertainty
- Translation/local-source uncertainty
- Commercial-source licensing limitations
- Inability to access private health/workload data

UPDATE CADENCE AND MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE:
- Intraday event days: freeze pregame snapshot at documented cutoff; refresh official availability, lineups, starters, toss XI, weather/venue state, pitch/roof, and late changes at defined sport-specific windows.
- Daily: refresh fixtures, lineups, injuries, transactions, weather/venue state, source freshness flags, pending log rows, and official results.
- Post-event within 2-12 hours where possible: reconcile final score, phase splits, box score, settlement rule, listed-item W-L, top/bottom penalties, winner lean, and retrospective tags.
- Weekly: recompute rolling metrics, calibration buckets, uncertainty bands, leakage checks, missingness checks, and source failure rates.
- Monthly: source audit, schema drift check, dashboard QA, metric-definition review, source registry review, and stale duplicate-rule review.
- Quarterly: privacy/access review, model bias review, integrity escalation review, retention audit, and governance signoff.
- Annual: full methodology review, source/vendor reprioritization, sport-annex review, model-risk signoff, and archive of obsolete rules.

ACTIONABLE IMPLEMENTATION PRIORITIES:
- First: create and maintain this universal core for purpose, scope, governance, provenance, source tiers, and status labels.
- Second: build and maintain a single metric registry and source registry.
- Third: encode data cutoff, confirmed/projected/inferred/unavailable status, QA status, and pending/live settlement status as structured metadata.
- Fourth: add dashboard standards for phase splits, availability, workload, source freshness, uncertainty, model watchlist, and governance.
- Fifth: introduce or document baseline and advanced models with assumptions, validation windows, drift controls, and human override.
- Sixth: add privacy, consent/lawful-basis, retention, access, security, and integrity policies before scaling private wearables, medical, or integrity data.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR FUTURE REFINEMENT:
- Which sources are actually licensed or accessible versus public-only?
- Which competitions require local-language source coverage?
- Which metrics are manual-only and which are computable?
- Which dashboards are for prediction, scouting, workload, governance, or integrity?
- Which privacy regime applies to each dataset and user location?
- Which fields should be stored permanently versus only in final reports?
- Which sport annexes should be split into separate files later to reduce duplication?

AFL / AUSTRALIAN RULES FOOTBALL:

You must obtain the following information through sound and accurate research before analysis.
If a stat is unavailable, mark it UNKNOWN or skip to the next available source.
Do not guess.
Do not use outdated team selection, injury, suspension, weather, venue, tactical, or market data.
Use the most recent available information up to the DATA CUTOFF.
Separate confirmed information from projected information.
Separate AFL Premiership Season, AFL Finals, pre-season, VFL/state league, AFLW, and practice match data where relevant.
Separate full-match regulation data from extra-time data where relevant.

2. SOURCE PRIORITY RULES
============================================================

SOURCE PRIORITY:
- Official AFL.com.au source first for fixture, venue, ladder, match centre, team lineups, injury list, stats, and result (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Official club website / club X accounts for confirmed teams, late changes, sub, injuries, suspensions, and coach comments (if available, if not move onto the next)
- AFL Match Centre for live score, quarter scores, team/player stats, interchange/sub, scoring shots, inside 50s, clearances, tackles, disposals, and match timeline (if available, if not move onto the next)
- AFL Stats / Team Rankings for official team and player stats (if available, if not move onto the next)
- AFL Tables for historical AFL/VFL match results, player stats, coaching records, venue history, H2H, and scoring history (if available, if not move onto the next)
- FootyWire for team selections, player stats, AFL Fantasy, SuperCoach-style indicators, player rankings, and historical player/team data (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Wheelo Ratings for AFL ratings, expected scores, shot charts, expected score per shot, team ratings, player ratings, simulations, and advanced scoring quality context (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Squiggle for AFL forecasts, ladder projections, tips, team ratings, and historical modelling context (if available, if not move onto the next)
- FanFooty for live player stats, fantasy stats, role/position changes, and player usage during games (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Trusted AFL reporters, club press conferences, and verified media for injuries, late outs, role changes, and tactical news (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Bureau of Meteorology or trusted weather source for wind, rain, temperature, ground condition, and slippery/dewy conditions (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Odds comparison sites only for market context, not as the main prediction source (if available, if not move onto the next)

MARKET-LINES-AS-SUPPORTING-INPUT POLICY (UPDATED 2026-06-03, supersedes prior No-Odds default):
- DEFAULT: betting/sportsbook/market lines, totals, spreads, and the level of the user-provided line MAY now be consulted to ASSIST decision-making. They are a supporting input, NOT the be-all-end-all and NOT the primary driver.
- Research leads, market confirms: form the pick from research-based team/player/lineup/injury/venue/weather/pace/form/tactical/statistical analysis FIRST, then use the market line as a cross-check. The independent research conclusion must be statable without the line.
- The line is a useful signal of expected scoring/strength (e.g. a high Q1/1H total implies an expected fast pace). Treat it as ONE corroborating data point with modest weight - it can break ties, flag when my number is far from consensus (prompting a re-research), or add/subtract confidence, but it must never override clear current-form/lineup/pace evidence on its own.
- When my research number diverges sharply from the line, do NOT just defer to the line: re-check current-season pace and the last 2-3 games to find which side is right, then explain the divergence.
- PER-QUERY OVERRIDE STILL WINS: if the user explicitly says (in a given request) not to look at odds/lines/markets for that query, honor it for that query - analyze only the user-provided thresholds and research data, mark market context as intentionally omitted, and do not downgrade confidence solely because market data was not checked. A standing default of "use lines" yields to an explicit per-query "no lines."
- Either way, never present a line as a substitute for research, and never copy a pick straight from the market.
- Ranked lists must run from most likely to least likely. The fourth-ranked item must be the single least-likely listed outcome; if it wins, log the normal item result plus one extra bottom-pick penalty loss and explain why it was underrated.
- If the named AFL fixture and the listed teams do not match, flag it immediately and research the listed markets as a mixed-fixture card only when each item can be independently verified.
- Collect quarter-by-quarter scoring, inside-50 trend, clearance trend, scoring shots, injuries/sub usage, and late-game goal runs where available. Do not judge the process from final margin alone when one side led for most of the match or lost control only late.
- Late scratches of elite distributors, midfielders, rebounders, or key forwards must materially change both side and total rankings. In hot/humid Darwin-style games, losing a primary ball mover can increase underdog blowout risk and reduce clean scoring chains even when season averages point high.
- For AFL exact-complement cards that pair a total with a large handicap, rank total and margin separately. A favorite can be the likely winner and still not be the best cover if recent strong-opponent margins sit inside the line; do not let a win streak alone push a -20s handicap above a scoring total supported by both teams' recent points profiles.
- AFL mismatch correction: do not let favourite injury/rest notes automatically suppress a high total or large favourite cover when the favourite still has multiple elite scoring targets, the replacement ruck/inside-mid setup is credible, and the underdog defence is young or depleted. If the favourite can generate 65+ inside 50s and has several strong marking options, a 100+ margin and 205+ total are live even when one star ruck or forward is out.

- Compare same-half combined totals Last 5/10/15/20 (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare same-half scoring shots (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare same-half Over/Under hit rate versus today's line (if available, if not move onto the next)

IF MARKET IS TEAM TOTAL POINTS:
- Compare team Points For average to line (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare opponent Points Against average to line (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare team scoring shots and accuracy (if available, if not move onto the next)

- Compare key forward availability (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare key defender availability for opponent (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare venue/weather scoring impact (if available, if not move onto the next)

IF MARKET IS PLAYER DISPOSALS:
- Confirm player named in final team (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Confirm player role and position (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Confirm substitute risk (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare player disposals Last 5/10/15/20 (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare player time on ground (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare centre-bounce attendance / role (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare matchup/tagging risk (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Compare venue/weather impact on disposal style (if available, if not move onto the next)

IF MARKET IS PLAYER GOALS:

22. ANALYSIS OUTPUT STRUCTURE
============================================================

Use this exact structure in the final analysis:

1. Event verification
2. Data cutoff
3. Sources checked

6. Rolling Last 5/10/15/20 team stats
7. Scoring, quarter, half, and expected-score profile
8. Midfield, ruck, clearance, inside 50, and pressure profile

10. Home/away, venue, weather, and rest context
11. Head-to-head
12. Market-specific stats
13. Market context
14. Most likely outcome
15. Confidence level: Low / Medium / High
16. Key reasons supporting the pick
17. Key risks that could flip the pick
18. Final answer

23. FINAL DECISION RULES
============================================================



============================================================
ACCURACY UPDATE - PRE-GAME RELIABILITY AND CALIBRATION ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum implements the latest model learnings from recent prediction errors.
It is designed to improve pre-game reliability first, while still allowing live/current-state recalibration when a user supplies a live score or when the market changes close to start time.

CORE CHANGE:
- The model must be math-first, market-aware, and confidence-calibrated.
- Do not treat a lean as a strong pick.
- Do not give Medium or High confidence just because one narrative is persuasive.
- If the market is volatile, data is incomplete, or model components conflict, downgrade confidence or return PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE.
- A prediction should be evaluated by calibration, closing-line value, logic correctness, and outcome, not by win/loss alone.

PREGAME PRIORITY ORDER:
1. Verify event, venue, timing, market line, and availability.
2. Establish market baseline: opening line, current line, consensus line, price/odds, and line movement.
3. Establish sport-specific base projection using season-long and opponent-adjusted efficiency.
4. Adjust for recent form only after opponent quality, venue, and availability are considered.
5. Apply matchup-specific adjustments.
6. Apply weather, venue, travel, rest, and schedule adjustments.
7. Apply market-specific model: full-game, period, player prop, spread/line, total, team total, moneyline.
8. Apply volatility penalty and confidence cap.
9. State the best counterargument before the final pick.
10. Separate most-likely side from value at the current line.

MARKET BASELINE RULE:
- The current market line is not the prediction, but it is the baseline that must be beaten.
- Always compare the user line to the current consensus line if available.
- If the user line is worse than market consensus, downgrade confidence unless the model edge is clear.
- If the market has moved materially and no reason is known, lower confidence.
- If model fair line and market line differ by less than the sport-specific minimum edge threshold, label the edge thin/no clear value.

MINIMUM EDGE THRESHOLDS BEFORE CALLING A PICK STRONG:
- AFL/NRL full-game totals: at least 4-6 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball full-game totals: at least 3-5 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball period totals: at least 1.5-2.5 points from fair line.
- MLB totals: at least 0.4-0.7 runs from fair line, with bullpen and weather agreement.
- Cricket T20 innings totals: at least 5-8 runs from fair line after toss/Playing XI if possible.
- Soccer goals totals: at least 0.15-0.25 expected goals from fair line or price-equivalent edge.
- NHL totals: at least 0.25-0.35 goals from fair line with goalie confirmation.
- NFL totals: at least 2-3 points from fair line with quarterback/inactive/weather confirmation.

CONFIDENCE CAPS:
- If lineups/team lists/Playing XI/inactives/starting goalies are unconfirmed, maximum confidence is Low-Medium unless market is not availability-sensitive.
- If the matchup is high volatility, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If the market has moved sharply against the pick and no clear explanation supports the model, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If only one factor supports the pick, maximum confidence is Low.
- If the model edge is within the minimum threshold, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If key data is missing, state PROVISIONAL PICK or PASS.
- High confidence should be rare and requires confirmed availability, market stability, multiple aligned model components, and low volatility.

VOLATILITY CLASSIFICATION:
Before final answer, classify volatility as LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH.

High-volatility examples:
- Weak teams with bad defences and inconsistent attacks.
- Games with uncertain lineups, late outs, or unclear role allocation.
- Totals where one side can create blowout or garbage-time distortion.
- Player props dependent on a single uncertain role, minutes, usage, or matchup variable.
- MLB full-game totals with strong starters but poor or tired bullpens.
- Cricket totals where toss, dew, pitch, or wickets-in-hand can strongly alter scoring.
- Soccer cards or corners markets without confirmed referee or lineups.

If volatility is HIGH:
- Do not present the pick as reliable.
- Use conservative probability bands.
- Prefer PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE when the model edge is small.

COUNTERARGUMENT REQUIREMENT:
Before the final pick, include:
- The strongest reason the pick could lose.
- Whether that risk is already priced into the market.
- Whether the risk is strong enough to downgrade confidence or pass.

LIVE/CURRENT-STATE RECALIBRATION RULE:
Pregame accuracy is the priority, but if a user provides live/current score, current innings state, current period score, or updated market line:
- Recalculate using current state first.
- For totals, calculate required remaining pace immediately.
- Do not defend the pre-game pick if the live state has invalidated it.
- Label the old pick as STILL VALID / WEAKENED / STALE / BAD POSITION.
- Keep pre-game retrospective separate from live decision-making.

RETROSPECTIVE LEARNING RULE:
Every post-game review must classify the error type:
- Bad input data.
- Correct input but wrong weighting.
- Availability/lineup miss.
- Market line was stale or worse than consensus.
- Bullpen/bench/death-over/late-game volatility miss.
- Period-market model borrowed too much from full-game model.
- Weather/venue condition misread.
- Random variance despite correct process.

Only update the model after identifying which error type occurred.
Do not overreact to one result.
Track calibration by probability bucket and market type.


============================================================
AFL ACCURACY UPDATE - PRE-GAME TOTALS, HANDICAPS, AND PLAYER PROPS
============================================================

AFL-SPECIFIC MODEL CHANGES:
- Do not treat poor attacks as automatic under teams if both defences are weak.
- Bad attack + bad defence = HIGH VOLATILITY, not automatically under.
- For totals, scoring shots and expected score must be weighted above raw points for/against when available.
- For weak-team totals, cap confidence at Low-Medium unless scoring shots, inside 50s, weather, team selection, and market agree.
- For high totals involving bottom teams, explicitly test both paths:
  1. Skill-error suppression path: poor ball use, missed shots, stoppages, low efficiency.
  2. Defensive-leakage path: turnovers, repeat inside 50s, easy transition goals, poor structure.
- If both paths are plausible and the line is close to fair value, say NO CLEAR EDGE or Low confidence.

AFL TOTALS WEIGHTING UPDATE:
- 20% team scoring-shot and expected-score profile.
- 15% points for/against baseline.
- 15% inside 50 profile, scores per inside 50, and forward-half turnover scoring.
- 15% team selection, key forwards/defenders, rucks, midfield/centre-bounce availability.
- 10% venue dimensions, weather, wind, rain, dew, and surface.
- 10% recent form adjusted for opponent quality and venue.
- 10% market line, movement, and consensus.
- 5% H2H only if current squads, coaches, and venue context are relevant.

AFL PERIOD AND LIVE RULE:
- Full-game total, first half, and quarter markets must be modelled separately.
- If live or halftime score is supplied, calculate points needed to clear the line and required points per remaining quarter.
- If the required remaining pace is below or near first-half pace, do not keep an under solely because pre-game averages were low.

AFL CONFIDENCE CAP:
- Weak-team matchup with bad defences: maximum Low-Medium unless market, expected score, and selection strongly align.
- Outdoor bad weather with uncertain wind/rain: maximum Low-Medium for totals unless confirmed close to bounce.
- Player props with uncertain sub risk or role: maximum Low.

FINAL DECISION RULES:
- Do not make a pick until final teams, injuries, suspensions, and substitute status are checked if available
- Confirm both final 23s, late outs, substitute, managed players, role changes, and injury-affected players through official AFL/club sources and verified X/Twitter reporters close to bounce. Classify key players as CONFIRMED IN, CONFIRMED OUT, LIMITED/ROLE RISK, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN and adjust scoring pace, midfield supply, defensive pressure, rotations, and quarter-by-quarter expectations.
- If final teams are unavailable, clearly state that the pick is pre-team/provisional
- If substitute is unknown and the market is a player prop, lower confidence
- Do not rely on one stat only
- Prioritise same-season and same-role data over older data where possible
- Prioritise recent form, but do not ignore long-term team quality
- For totals, prioritise scoring shots, inside 50s, goal accuracy, expected score, weather, venue, and team selection
- For overlapping AFL totals such as Over 167.5 and Under 187.5, model the likely middle band explicitly. If weather, late key-forward outs, and goal-kicking efficiency point to a 160-180 cluster, a broad high under can be strongest while the low over may still be live but not automatic.
- Weather must be match-window specific. If rain is expected later in the day after the match, do not downgrade scoring or marking for rain; use only wind, temperature, humidity, ground state, and any rain actually expected before/during play.
- For line/spread, prioritise margin profile, team strength, injuries, venue/travel, and fourth-quarter performance
- Late key-position forward outs should reduce both the favourite's scoring ceiling and large-margin confidence, especially in damp or showery conditions. This can lift the underdog's big cushion even when the favourite remains the better win chance.
- Live AFL correction rule: if the favourite reaches 80+ points by halftime or leads by 50+ despite a key-forward out, immediately override pregame weather/selection unders. Midfield territory, transition speed, repeat inside-50s, and opponent defensive collapse have become stronger evidence than the missing forward or pregame total band.
- For head-to-head, prioritise team strength, midfield/ruck edge, scoring profile, defence, and venue context
- For player disposals, prioritise role, TOG, centre-bounce attendance, matchup, and tag risk
- For player goals, prioritise forward role, scoring shots, expected score, inside 50 supply, defender matchup, and weather
- For player tackles, prioritise role, stoppage/contest expectation, opponent possession style, and wet-weather pressure
- For player marks, prioritise role, team kick-mark style, opponent concessions, and weather
- Do not overweight H2H if squads, coaches, roles, venue, or tactical systems have changed
- Check pre-game and post-game coach/player press conference comments for tactical intent: faster ball movement, defensive pressure, stoppage focus, corridor/wing use, tag plans, sub/rotation plans, forward-half pressure, and whether the side says it wants a more attacking or more defensively controlled style. Weight comments more when they align with team selection, late outs, role changes, weather, and recent quarter splits; do not use them alone.
- If the evidence is mixed, say so
- If the data is weak or unavailable, lower confidence
- If key information is missing, state that the prediction is provisional
- Never call a pick "safe" or "guaranteed"
- Mark unavailable information as UNKNOWN

BASEBALL / MLB:

You must obtain the following information through sound and accurate research before analysis.
If a stat is unavailable, mark it UNKNOWN or skip to the next available source.
Do not guess.
Do not use outdated lineup, injury, starting pitcher, bullpen, weather, umpire, ballpark, or market data.
Use the most recent available information up to the DATA CUTOFF.
Separate confirmed information from projected information.
Separate full-game data from extra-innings data where relevant.
Separate regular season, postseason, spring training, exhibition, and minor league data where relevant.

2. SOURCE PRIORITY RULES
============================================================

SOURCE PRIORITY:
- Official MLB.com source first for fixture, venue, probable pitchers, starting lineups, injuries, transactions, box score, and result (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Official team website / team X accounts for confirmed lineups, late scratches, pitcher changes, bullpen news, and roster moves (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Baseball Savant / Statcast for expected stats, pitch-level data, pitch arsenal, exit velocity, barrels, hard-hit rate, chase rate, whiff rate, xwOBA, xBA, xSLG, and matchup quality (if available, if not move onto the next)
- MLB Stats for official team/player batting, pitching, fielding, standings, schedule, and game logs (if available, if not move onto the next)
- FanGraphs for wRC+, wOBA, ISO, FIP, xFIP, SIERA, bullpen stats, plate discipline, splits, projections, and advanced analysis (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Baseball-Reference for historical team/player stats, game logs, splits, streaks, standings, and cross-checking (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Brooks Baseball / Pitcher List / RotoWire / CBS / ESPN for pitch mix, injuries, probable starters, lineup confirmation, and fantasy-style usage news (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Umpire scorecard / referee-style sources for umpire strike zone, pitcher/batter tendencies, and over/under trends (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Weather source for outdoor ballpark wind, temperature, humidity, precipitation, air density, and roof status (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Odds comparison sites only for market context, not as the main prediction source (if available, if not move onto the next)

USER NO-ODDS / NO-MARKET OVERRIDE:
- If the user explicitly says not to look at betting odds, lines, markets, CLV, sportsbooks, or market movement, do not open sportsbook, odds, market, or line-movement pages.
- Analyze only the user-provided thresholds/handicaps and research-based team, player, lineup, injury, pitcher, bullpen, venue, weather, schedule, and form data.
- Mark market context as intentionally omitted per user instruction; do not downgrade confidence solely because market data was not checked.
- The user-provided line or threshold may be referenced only as the item being ranked, not as evidence from the market.
- Ranked lists must run from most likely to least likely. The fourth-ranked item must be the single least-likely listed outcome; if it wins, log the normal item result plus one extra bottom-pick penalty loss and explain why it was underrated.

NPB / NON-MLB BASEBALL OVERRIDE:
- If the baseball query is NPB or another non-MLB league, replace MLB-specific source priority with league official pages first, then trusted local live-score/stat pages. For NPB, prioritize NPB.jp for schedule, scores, standings, team batting/pitching, and game logs; use Yahoo Japan Sports/official club pages for confirmed starting lineups, bench players, recent player form, and local weather.
- Do not use NPB betting-preview or odds pages when the user requests no odds. Avoid third-party prediction pages unless only using verifiable fixture/stat facts that are independently cross-checked.
- For KBO/non-MLB baseball overlapping totals such as Over 9.0 and Under 11, explicitly model the middle band where both can win, usually exactly 10 runs. In rain-threat games, identify whether the weather points to cancellation/shortening, starter interruption, dampened carry, or sloppy-defense risk; do not rank the aggressive over above the broad under unless the lineup, bullpen, and weather evidence all support 11-12+ runs.
- For KBO rain-threat games, add a clear void/no-game risk note when rain is likely before the 5th inning. If a game is declared no-game/rained out before becoming official, clear it from pending as ungraded/void with no top-pick or bottom-pick penalties, even if the early score was favorable.
- In Central League NPB games without a DH, explicitly model the pitcher batting spot and generally lower run environment compared with MLB. A weak starter profile alone is not enough to bury a high-threshold under if recent H2H, pitcher spots, and bullpen quality point to a 5-8 run band.
- For NPB run cushions, remember ties/draws can exist after league extra-inning rules. In a draw, both +1.5 sides can win, so do not rank any +1.5 cushion last in a projected low-scoring game unless there is a strong multi-run separation signal. Low run bands increase the chance that both cushions survive.
- For NPB Pacific League exact 6.5 totals, do not default to under just because NPB scoring is lower than MLB. Six-and-a-half is a low threshold; if confirmed lineups, same-matchup scoring, bullpen walk/ERA risk, or weaker fielding create a 7-run path, rank Over 6.5 above Under 6.5 while still keeping the under adjacent when both starters are credible.
- For NPB starter-edge sides, include emergency-exit risk and the opponent starter's recent workload. If the preferred starter is the main reason for the side but exits early, the side thesis can collapse; if the opposing starter has recent 7-inning, 3-4 run stability, do not bury that opponent ML or make a low total over the top pick without bullpen scoring evidence.

25. ANALYSIS OUTPUT STRUCTURE
============================================================

Use this exact structure in the final analysis:

1. Event verification
2. Data cutoff
3. Sources checked
4. Confirmed/probable starting pitchers
5. Confirmed/probable lineups
6. Injuries, roster moves, and availability
7. Rolling Last 5/10/15/20 team stats
8. Team batting and handedness splits
9. Starting pitcher analysis
10. Bullpen analysis and fatigue
11. Ballpark, weather, roof, and umpire context
12. Head-to-head
13. Market-specific stats
14. Market context
15. Most likely outcome
16. Confidence level: Low / Medium / High
17. Key reasons supporting the pick
18. Key risks that could flip the pick
19. Final answer

26. FINAL DECISION RULES
