============================================================
ACCURACY UPDATE - PRE-GAME RELIABILITY AND CALIBRATION ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum implements the latest model learnings from recent prediction errors.
It is designed to improve pre-game reliability first, while still allowing live/current-state recalibration when a user supplies a live score or when the market changes close to start time.

CORE CHANGE:
- The model must be math-first, market-aware, and confidence-calibrated.
- Do not treat a lean as a strong pick.
- Do not give Medium or High confidence just because one narrative is persuasive.
- If the market is volatile, data is incomplete, or model components conflict, downgrade confidence or return PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE.
- A prediction should be evaluated by calibration, closing-line value, logic correctness, and outcome, not by win/loss alone.

PREGAME PRIORITY ORDER:
1. Verify event, venue, timing, market line, and availability.
2. Establish market baseline: opening line, current line, consensus line, price/odds, and line movement.
3. Establish sport-specific base projection using season-long and opponent-adjusted efficiency.
4. Adjust for recent form only after opponent quality, venue, and availability are considered.
5. Apply matchup-specific adjustments.
6. Apply weather, venue, travel, rest, and schedule adjustments.
7. Apply market-specific model: full-game, period, player prop, spread/line, total, team total, moneyline.
8. Apply volatility penalty and confidence cap.
9. State the best counterargument before the final pick.
10. Separate most-likely side from value at the current line.

MARKET BASELINE RULE:
- The current market line is not the prediction, but it is the baseline that must be beaten.
- Always compare the user line to the current consensus line if available.
- If the user line is worse than market consensus, downgrade confidence unless the model edge is clear.
- If the market has moved materially and no reason is known, lower confidence.
- If model fair line and market line differ by less than the sport-specific minimum edge threshold, label the edge thin/no clear value.
- If the user explicitly says not to use betting odds/lines, skip market sources entirely and rank only from baseball evidence: starters, bulk arms, bullpens, lineups, injuries, park, roof/weather, defense, recent form, and matchup quality.

OVERLAPPING TOTALS / RUN-LINE RANKING RULE:
- When multiple listed outcomes can both win, such as Over 6.5 and Under 9.0, rank by realistic score-path coverage and margin of error.
- A broad middle-friendly total should usually outrank a thin over/under unless starting pitching, bullpen availability, lineup strength, and park/weather all align strongly.
- For run-line cushions, compare expected closeness against blowout pathways: starter mismatch, bullpen fatigue, defensive weakness, and late-inning scoring volatility.

MINIMUM EDGE THRESHOLDS BEFORE CALLING A PICK STRONG:
- AFL/NRL full-game totals: at least 4-6 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball full-game totals: at least 3-5 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball period totals: at least 1.5-2.5 points from fair line.
- MLB totals: at least 0.4-0.7 runs from fair line, with bullpen and weather agreement.
- Cricket T20 innings totals: at least 5-8 runs from fair line after toss/Playing XI if possible.
- Soccer goals totals: at least 0.15-0.25 expected goals from fair line or price-equivalent edge.
- NHL totals: at least 0.25-0.35 goals from fair line with goalie confirmation.
- NFL totals: at least 2-3 points from fair line with quarterback/inactive/weather confirmation.

CONFIDENCE CAPS:
- If lineups/team lists/Playing XI/inactives/starting goalies are unconfirmed, maximum confidence is Low-Medium unless market is not availability-sensitive.
- If the matchup is high volatility, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If the market has moved sharply against the pick and no clear explanation supports the model, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If only one factor supports the pick, maximum confidence is Low.
- If the model edge is within the minimum threshold, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If key data is missing, state PROVISIONAL PICK or PASS.
- High confidence should be rare and requires confirmed availability, market stability, multiple aligned model components, and low volatility.

VOLATILITY CLASSIFICATION:
Before final answer, classify volatility as LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH.

High-volatility examples:
- Weak teams with bad defences and inconsistent attacks.
- Games with uncertain lineups, late outs, or unclear role allocation.
- Totals where one side can create blowout or garbage-time distortion.
- Player props dependent on a single uncertain role, minutes, usage, or matchup variable.
- MLB full-game totals with strong starters but poor or tired bullpens.
- Cricket totals where toss, dew, pitch, or wickets-in-hand can strongly alter scoring.
- Soccer cards or corners markets without confirmed referee or lineups.

If volatility is HIGH:
- Do not present the pick as reliable.
- Use conservative probability bands.
- Prefer PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE when the model edge is small.

COUNTERARGUMENT REQUIREMENT:
Before the final pick, include:
- The strongest reason the pick could lose.
- Whether that risk is already priced into the market.
- Whether the risk is strong enough to downgrade confidence or pass.

LIVE/CURRENT-STATE RECALIBRATION RULE:
Pregame accuracy is the priority, but if a user provides live/current score, current innings state, current period score, or updated market line:
- Recalculate using current state first.
- For totals, calculate required remaining pace immediately.
- Do not defend the pre-game pick if the live state has invalidated it.
- Label the old pick as STILL VALID / WEAKENED / STALE / BAD POSITION.
- Keep pre-game retrospective separate from live decision-making.

RETROSPECTIVE LEARNING RULE:
Every post-game review must classify the error type:
- Bad input data.
- Correct input but wrong weighting.
- Availability/lineup miss.
- Market line was stale or worse than consensus.
- Bullpen/bench/death-over/late-game volatility miss.
- Period-market model borrowed too much from full-game model.
- Weather/venue condition misread.
- Random variance despite correct process.

Only update the model after identifying which error type occurred.
Do not overreact to one result.
Track calibration by probability bucket and market type.


============================================================
MLB ACCURACY UPDATE - STARTERS, BULLPENS, WEATHER, AND TOTALS
============================================================

MLB-SPECIFIC MODEL CHANGES:
- Separate First 5 and full-game models. A correct starting-pitcher read is not enough for a full-game total.
- Full-game totals must include a standalone late-innings volatility score.
- Do not let one strong starter override bad bullpen, tired bullpen, poor defence, or hitter-friendly weather.
- Do not let one weak starter override pitcher-friendly park, strong opposing starter, roof/weather suppression, or weak lineups.
- If the line is 8.0, explicitly account for push probability at exactly 8 runs.
- If the best available market is 7.5 but the user line is 8.0, state that the over is materially less attractive.

MLB TOTALS WEIGHTING UPDATE:
- 25% starting pitcher skill and current form: FIP/xFIP/SIERA, K-BB%, WHIP, HR risk, xwOBA, pitch mix.
- 20% bullpen quality, fatigue, recent workload, leverage availability, and closer/setup availability.
- 20% lineup strength, confirmed batting order, handedness splits, injuries, and plate-discipline matchup.
- 15% ballpark, roof, weather, wind, temperature, humidity, and umpire if available.
- 10% Statcast contact quality: barrel rate, hard-hit rate, xSLG/xwOBA, launch profile.
- 5% recent scoring form, adjusted for opponent quality.
- 5% market line, movement, and consensus.

MLB BULLPEN VOLATILITY SCORE:
Assign LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH before full-game totals.
Increase volatility if:
- Previous game used high-leverage relievers.
- Doubleheader or extra-innings game occurred recently.
- Starter has low expected innings/pitch-count limit.
- Bullpen ERA/FIP/xFIP and walk rate are poor.
- Closer/setup arms are unavailable or overworked.

MLB CONFIDENCE CAP:
- If lineups are unconfirmed: maximum Low-Medium for totals and Low for hitter props.
- If roof/weather or umpire is unknown and material to the market: maximum Low-Medium.
- If full-game total depends mostly on bullpen behaviour: maximum Low-Medium unless bullpen data strongly supports it.

FINAL DECISION RULES:
- Do not make a pick until probable/confirmed starting pitchers are checked if available
- Do not make a pick until lineups are checked if available
- Confirm both teams' starting lineups, batting order, catcher, defensive alignment notes if available, and active bench/late scratches close to first pitch. For day-to-day/questionable players, check official MLB/team lineups plus verified team/beat-reporter X/Twitter updates; classify as CONFIRMED IN, CONFIRMED OUT, LIMITED/PINCH-HIT RISK, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN and adjust early scoring, platoon strength, defense, and bullpen/bench paths.
- If the user explicitly says not to look at betting odds, lines, prices, or markets, use a RESEARCH-ONLY mode: do not open odds pages, do not quote odds, do not infer value from price, and rank only by team/player research, form, injuries, starters, bullpen, park, weather/roof, umpire if available, and matchup context
- The bottom-ranked item must be the least-likely listed outcome. If it wins, treat that as a ranking failure and add a bottom-pick penalty loss in the prediction log.
- The top-ranked item is the declared strongest edge. If it loses, treat that as a major ranking failure, add a top-pick penalty loss in the prediction log, and write a serious retrospective identifying overweighted evidence, missed warnings, and a concrete future rule change.
- The second-ranked item is also part of the strongest cluster. If rank 2 loses, do not add the rank-1 top-pick penalty solely for that reason, but do run the same serious researched retrospective and update this sports doc with any new lesson. If ranks 1 and 2 both lose, classify the card as a major process failure and investigate the shared bad assumption first.
- Include relevant pre-match, mid-match, and post-match comments from coaching staff and players when they materially affect the analysis. Prioritize official press conferences, league/team recaps, and verified beat reporters. Tactical, matchup, workload, injury-limitation, motivation, rotation, fatigue, and adjustment comments can change rankings; generic confidence or effort quotes are lower-weight unless supported by tactical detail or later game evidence.
- For MLB run lines and totals, collect inning-by-inning scoring where available, including first-five scoring, starter innings, bullpen-entry inning/score, late-inning runs allowed, and extra-innings exclusion when relevant. Do not let a final score hide whether the read was right early but wrong after bullpen exposure.
- Before ranking a full-game under, compare it to the first-5 under thesis. If the starter matchup supports run suppression but either bullpen is tired, injured, below league average, or likely to enter early, the under thesis may be first-5 only rather than full-game.
- Full-game unders require both starter run suppression and late-inning support: bullpen quality/fatigue, defensive quality, catcher framing, umpire run environment if available, weather, and lineup depth must not point to late runs.
- If both starters have poor contact/strikeout profiles, both bullpens are vulnerable, and recent series games already showed late-inning scoring, rank modest overs above high-threshold unders even when weather is cool or neutral.
- Do not rank a strong home team +1.5 as the least-likely outcome solely because the opposing starter has an edge. If the top total read is an under or middle-band game, both +1.5 sides can be highly live; account for home bullpen, late-game leverage, and one-run/two-run game paths.
- When one option is a no-cushion favorite to win and another is a broad +2.5 run cushion, do not let team reputation alone decide the order. Check handedness splits, starter volatility, bullpen fatigue, and one-run/two-run profiles; the +2.5 can be more likely than the favorite moneyline even when the favorite still has the better roster.
- Do not automatically make that no-cushion favorite the bottom pick. If the favorite has a strong home/team-quality edge and the total band still supports common favorite win margins, keep the favorite above true long-shot paths even when a +2.5 cushion is attractive.
- In roof-closed, pitcher-friendly parks, a wide under such as 9.5 can outrank a modest over such as 7.0 when both starters are credible and the park suppresses home runs. The modest over remains live if one starter has WHIP/command volatility or the bullpens are stressed, but do not rank it above close-game +1.5 cushions by default.
- For overlapping MLB totals such as Over 7.0 and Under 9.0, model the exact middle band: 8 runs can make both win, 7 pushes the over and wins the under, and 9 wins the over while pushing the under. Rank by the most likely run cluster, not by treating the two totals as direct opposites.
- For exact MLB 7.5 total complements in cool or park-suppressed games, do not call the under safe if the projected cluster is 7-8. Starter quality can support the low side, but weak team defense, walk traffic, middling bullpens, and recent same-series scoring can keep 8+ runs live; keep the total complements adjacent unless one side has clear failure-path separation.
- A home underdog +1.5 can outrank a road favorite moneyline when recent same-series games were one-run games, the underdog is in confirmed strong form, and the weather/starting-pitching setup supports a close 6-8 run game.
- If starting pitchers are unconfirmed, clearly state that the pick is provisional
- If lineups are unconfirmed, clearly state that the pick is pre-lineup/provisional
- If weather or roof status is unknown for an outdoor/retractable-roof ballpark, lower confidence for totals and home-run markets
- If umpire is unknown, lower confidence for strikeout, walk, NRFI/YRFI, and total markets
- Do not rely on one stat only
- Prioritise starting pitcher, handedness splits, bullpen availability, lineup strength, ballpark, and weather for baseball
- Do not let an elite starting pitcher automatically force a full-game under when the opponent has elite power, strong recent run form, and a homer-friendly park. Model the ace-damage path separately, especially when the park boosts home runs or the opposing lineup has multiple fly-ball/pull-power threats.
- For totals in homer-friendly parks, separate run-suppression skill from home-run volatility. A strong strikeout starter can still allow a total to clear if two or three swings produce multi-run innings.
- Prioritise recent form, but do not ignore season-long quality
- Prioritise xwOBA, barrel rate, hard-hit rate, K%, BB%, FIP/xFIP/SIERA, and pitch-type matchups over ERA/AVG alone
- For full-game totals, include starting pitchers, bullpens, park factors, weather, lineups, and umpire
- For First 5 markets, prioritise starting pitchers and early-inning offense over bullpen
- For First 5 unders, prioritize starter form/depth, first-inning scoring tendencies, platoon splits against the starter, early lineup quality, and whether the starter is likely to work at least five innings.
- For moneyline, prioritise starting pitcher edge, bullpen edge, lineup edge, defense, rest, and home/away context
- For run line, prioritise run differential, bullpen volatility, one-run game profile, and late-inning scoring
- For baseball sides and totals, mandatory pre-rank checks include starting-pitcher rest/workload, relief-pitcher rest and leverage availability, field conditions, weather/roof/wind, and team/player fielding-error risk. Do not finalize rankings until these are checked or explicitly marked UNKNOWN.
- For hitter props, prioritise confirmed lineup spot, handedness matchup, pitch-type matchup, Statcast quality, and park/weather
- For pitcher props, prioritise workload, pitch count, opponent K%, pitch mix, umpire, and manager leash
- Do not overweight H2H if lineups, starting pitchers, bullpens, managers, ballparks, or weather differ
- Check pre-game and post-game manager/player press conference comments for tactical intent: bullpen availability, opener/bulk plans, starter leash/pitch count, lineup aggressiveness, platoon plans, defensive alignment, base-running pressure, and whether the club says it wants a more aggressive or conservative offensive approach. Weight comments more when they align with confirmed lineups, recent bullpen usage, injuries, and series context; do not use them alone.
- If the evidence is mixed, say so
- If the data is weak or unavailable, lower confidence
- If key information is missing, state that the prediction is provisional
- Never call a pick "safe" or "guaranteed"
- Mark unavailable information as UNKNOWN

============================================================
2026-05-28 SETTLEMENT LEARNINGS - T20 AND DOMESTIC BASKETBALL
============================================================

These rules come from the settled SRH-RR, Anadolu Efes-Turk Telekom, and Hapoel Beer Sheva/Dimona-Elitzur Netanya rows. Apply them before the next comparable cricket or basketball card.

T20 LIVE ARITHMETIC OVERRIDE:
- If a T20 side batting first reaches 80/0 after 6 overs or 100+ by roughly 7-8 overs on a confirmed flat, short-boundary, high-scoring surface, the pregame projected innings band must be rebuilt immediately.
- In that state, required-run arithmetic overrides the original pregame middle-band read. If a side needs fewer than a run-a-ball or close to a run-a-ball to clear a modest innings over, that over cannot stay third or fourth.
- A broad high under that was reasonable pregame can become the weakest listed item live if the required suppression is extreme, such as needing the bowling side to hold the batting side to about 118 or fewer from the final 76 balls after a 103-run start.
- Do not preserve the pregame winner lean if the live innings has created a 225-245+ projected first-innings band. Re-run the match-winner path from scoreboard pressure, chase depth, dew, wickets in hand, and bowling resources.

T20 PITCH-REPORT CORRECTION:
- When the report says flat track, true pace/bounce, short boundaries, high first-innings average, 200+ venue trend, or similar batting language, a named bowling-strength argument is not enough to make a high under the top pick.
- SRH-RR confirmed that an underperforming bowling unit on a road can make 222+ and the batting side win ordinary outcomes, not tail outcomes. The future top-pick gate must ask whether the surface and current powerplay evidence already defeat the under.

BSL / EUROPEAN DOMESTIC PLAYOFF TOTALS:
- In 4-vs-5 playoff openers, season home scoring averages and one recent high-scoring H2H are not enough to bury the under. Playoff Game 1 defense, half-court pace, longer core minutes, and opponent scoring control can drag a projected 172-180 band into the low 160s.
- For full-game totals, compare the over thesis against tactical suppression paths quarter by quarter. If the favorite can win by 9-12 while the opponent scores only mid-70s, the side can be right and the over can still be wrong.
- Do not make Under 171-175-type totals fourth in Turkish BSL or similar domestic playoff games unless direct pace, recent same-matchup playoff pace, injury/rotation news, or coach comments clearly support a sustained high-possession game.

LATE-SEASON DOMESTIC BASKETBALL MOTIVATION:
- A mathematically safe or already-qualified home favorite can underperform the side expectation even when H2H, table position, and home court point to the favorite.
- If the opponent has relegation, lower-house, survival, or seeding urgency, keep that underdog's small cushion live. Netanya's outright win at Beer Sheva showed that motivation can flip the side while the high-threshold under remains correct.
- Separate side and total in these spots. A low/controlled total can support the underdog cushion by increasing one-possession and small-margin paths, even when the favorite has the better season profile.

KBO / BASEBALL OVERLAP TOTAL DISCIPLINE:
- Warm hitter-friendly baseball environments require middle-high cluster discipline. If venue/weather, recent scoring, bullpen exposure, or defensive volatility make 10-13 runs plausible, do not rank a broad under near 12 first while burying a modest over near 9.5 last unless the starter, bullpen, and fielding evidence clearly suppress late run clusters.
- If both teams have recent comeback or bullpen-volatility paths, explicitly test the 10, 11, 12, and 13-run outcomes before ranking overlapping totals. A broad under and modest over may both be live, but the modest over should not be treated as the least-likely outcome when late scoring paths are ordinary.
- Daegu/Samsung Lions Park and similar KBO run-friendly parks need a 9th-inning bullpen-implosion stress test. Mild weather does not erase park power, starter traffic, defensive errors, or a tired/volatile relief chain. If one late inning can realistically add 5-6 runs, a broad under around 12.5 should not be the top pick and an underdog +1.5 should not be buried merely because the home side has the better lineup.

LOGGING RULE:
- Preserve failed pregame cards and successful live reassessments as separate rows. Do not let a correct live correction erase the original ranking error; both lessons are needed.

============================================================
GLOBAL LINEUP, BENCH, AND AVAILABILITY SOURCE HIERARCHY - ADDED 2026-05-27
============================================================

PURPOSE:
Recent misses have repeatedly come from treating projected, stale, or incomplete availability information as confirmed. Before any pre-game analysis, every sport must now separate availability into one of these statuses:
- CONFIRMED IN
- CONFIRMED OUT
- CONFIRMED STARTER
- CONFIRMED BENCH / SUBSTITUTE / RESERVE
- LIMITED / MINUTES / SNAP / TOI / WORKLOAD RISK
- PROJECTED ONLY
- UNKNOWN

UNIVERSAL RULES:
- Official league, competition, club/team, PR, or match-centre information controls over aggregators.
- X/Twitter checks must prioritise official team accounts, official PR/media accounts, league accounts, and verified beat reporters. If X/Twitter is inaccessible or no readable verified source appears, state that clearly and lower confidence.
- Aggregators are useful for speed, but they are confirmation only when they cite or match an official source.
- Do not treat "probable", "expected", "projected", "likely", "line rush", "warmup", or "not on early injury report" as final.
- If the player's role affects the pick, state the exact verification time and source type used.
- If confirmed lineups are not yet released by that sport's normal cutoff, mark the analysis PROVISIONAL and rerun the availability check if a newer query arrives before game start.
- For American sports, StatMuse remains required for statistical lookups, but StatMuse is not the final authority for active status, starters, bench, inactives, scratches, goalies, or minute/snap limits.

MLB:
- Primary stats source: StatMuse, then Baseball Savant / MLB.com / FanGraphs when needed.
- Primary lineup source: MLB.com Starting Lineups and MLB Gameday/box score.
- Primary roster/injury source: MLB.com Transactions and official team injury/roster pages.
- Fast confirmation sources: official team X accounts, team PR X accounts, MLB beat reporters, MLB app/Gameday warmup and box-score tabs.
- Backup aggregators: Baseball Press, RotoWire, FantasyPros, Lineups.com, CBS/ESPN game pages, but only as backup if they match official/team source timing.
- Bench/bullpen rule: when the game status moves to warmup or lineups are active, use MLB Gameday/box score for listed bench and bullpen. If bench/bullpen are not visible, state UNKNOWN rather than assuming from the 26-man roster.
- Pitcher rule: distinguish probable starter, confirmed starter, opener, bullpen game, and late scratch. If a starter is an opener/reliever, model bullpen exposure explicitly.

NBA:
- Primary stats source: StatMuse, then NBA.com/stats for lineup and phase splits.
- Primary injury source: official NBA Injury Report at official.nba.com. Teams must report affected players by the day-before/game-day reporting windows, and reports update continually through the day.
- Primary starting lineup source: NBA.com Today's Lineups, official team PR/team X accounts, and confirmed team-reporter posts close to tip.
- Bench/rotation source: official active/inactive status, recent rotation, coach comments, beat reports, and NBA box/gamebook once starters are announced. Do not assume a player is available just because he is absent from an early discussion thread.
- Timing rule: if a player is Questionable, Doubtful, Game Time Decision, or returning from injury, keep the pick provisional until the last official report/team PR confirmation and lineup announcement close to tip.
- Analysis rule: when a star/creator/defender is late OUT or IN, rerun Q1 and 1H pace/efficiency logic instead of only adjusting full-game strength.

WNBA:
- Primary stats source: StatMuse where available, then WNBA.com/stats.
- Primary injury source: official WNBA Injury Report. Teams must report affected players by 5 p.m. local time the day before non-back-to-back games, by 1 p.m. local time for the second game of a back-to-back, and reports update continually.
- Primary lineup source: WNBA official/team X accounts and WNBA game pages. Starting lineups are not always centrally final far ahead of tip, so mark starters as PROJECTED unless official or team source confirms.
- Bench/rotation source: team PR, verified beat reporters, recent rotation, and game notes. If lineups are uncertain, reduce confidence especially for Q1 and 1H markets.

NFL:
- Primary stats source: StatMuse, then NFL.com Next Gen Stats / official team stats / Pro Football Reference as needed.
- Primary injury and availability source: official NFL injury reports, official team reports, and official inactives.
- Timing rule: game-day inactives are the final availability gate and are released about 90 minutes before kickoff.
- Role sources: official team X/PR, beat reporters, pre-game warmups, coach comments, and active roster elevations.
- Must-check roles: quarterback, offensive line, WR/TE/RB, cornerbacks/safeties, edge rushers, kickers/punters, defensive signal callers, and any snap-count limit.

NHL:
- Primary stats source: StatMuse where supported, then NHL.com/stats, Natural Stat Trick, Evolving-Hockey, MoneyPuck as needed.
- Primary lineup/goalie source: NHL.com projected lineups, official team X/PR, morning skate reports, warmup reports, and verified beat reporters.
- Starting goalie rule: projected goalie is not confirmed unless team/coach/beat warmup confirmation supports it. Treat "expected" as PROJECTED ONLY.
- Line/bench rule: check line rushes, defensive pairs, power-play units, scratches, call-ups, injuries, and warmup absences. If scratches are unconfirmed, mark lineup risk.
- Period markets: starting goalie, top-six/top-pair availability, special teams units, and back-to-back goalie workload must be refreshed close to puck drop.

KBO:
- Primary official source: KBO official site/game centre for schedule, score, venue, official game information, entries, and starters where available.
- Korean source stack: Naver Sports KBO game centre, official team social accounts, Korean beat reporters, and sports news outlets for "starter/lineup" and injury reports.
- English backup: MyKBO Stats/MyKBO Live for schedules, starters, roster changes, lineups, and notifications, but it is unofficial and must be treated as a translation/backup source.
- Timing rule: confirmed batting lineups may appear close to first pitch. Until the KBO/Naver/team source confirms the starting order, label lineups PROJECTED.
- Key checks: foreign player availability, catcher/rest changes, designated hitter/position changes, bullpen workload from prior games, venue changes, rain postponement risk, and roster registration/deregistration.

NPB:
- Primary official source: NPB.jp for announced starting pitchers and player registration/deregistration.
- Lineup source: official team accounts, NPB game pages where available, Yahoo Japan Sports/SportsNavi game centre, and Japanese beat reporters.
- Timing rule: starting pitchers are usually announced ahead of game day, but starting batting orders are typically close to first pitch. Treat early lineups as projected until official/team/SportsNavi confirmation.
- League rule check: Central League pitcher/DH rule context matters by season/competition. Do not import MLB DH assumptions unless the game format confirms it.

CRICKET / IPL / T20:
- Primary official source: IPL/BCCI match centre, official team X accounts, toss broadcast, and official playing conditions.
- Playing XI rule: in IPL, captains nominate 11 players plus up to 5 substitute fielders after the toss. Toss is held not earlier than 30 minutes and not later than 15 minutes before start. Therefore, pre-toss XI analysis is PROJECTED ONLY.
- Impact/substitute rule: explicitly identify the starting XI, impact/substitute options, overseas-player balance, wicketkeeper, death bowlers, powerplay hitters, and whether the side is batting or bowling first.
- Backup sources: Cricbuzz and ESPNcricinfo live scorecard/commentary for toss, XIs, Impact Player/substitute notes, pitch report, and captain comments.
- Analysis rule: once toss/XI is known, rerun innings and powerplay projections. Do not keep a pre-toss pick unchanged if the batting order, impact-player path, or death bowling changes materially.

SOCCER / FOOTBALL AVAILABILITY SOURCE STACK:
- Primary source: official league match centre, official club X/accounts, and official club website.
- Timing rule: Premier League starting XIs and formations are available on league platforms and club platforms about 75 minutes before kickoff; many other competitions release about 60 minutes before kickoff.
- Must-check roles: goalkeeper, centre backs, fullbacks/wingbacks, main striker, creators, set-piece takers, penalty takers, defensive midfield screen, and bench attacking/defensive substitutes.
- If starting XIs are not out, the pick is pre-lineup/provisional. Once lineups drop, rerun goals/first-half logic for formation, striker availability, defensive absences, and bench strength.

NRL:
- Primary source: NRL.com Team Lists, club official sources, NRL Casualty Ward, judiciary/suspension reports, and official club X accounts.
- Timing rule: initial squads are named Tuesday, 19-man squads are named 24 hours before kickoff, and the final 17 active players are confirmed one hour before kickoff.
- Must-check roles: spine 1/6/7/9, goal kicker, middle rotation, edge back-rowers, starting props, hooker bench use, 18th player, HIA/returning player workload, and late mail.
- Do not treat Tuesday team lists as final. The one-hour final 17 is the final availability gate.

SMALLER BASKETBALL LEAGUES / INTERNATIONAL CLUB BASKETBALL:
- Primary source: official league game centre, official club accounts, official federation pages, and live scorecard.
- Backup sources: FIBA LiveStats, Flashscore/SofaScore/RealGM/Proballers where available.
- Starting lineups and bench roles are often not reliable until game start. If official starters are unavailable, mark starters PROJECTED and use last-game/season rotations with lower confidence.
- For Q1/1H markets, late missing import/star guard/primary big must trigger a full rerun of early pace and bench-unit logic.

FINAL AVAILABILITY CHECK BEFORE EVERY PICK:
1. Log the data cutoff time in the user's current timezone.
2. Check the prior pending result first if there is one.
3. Check official league/team source for injuries/availability.
4. Check official lineup/team sheet/inactives/final 17/toss XI/goalie confirmation at the sport-specific release window.
5. Search X/Twitter or indexed X results for official team/PR/verified beat confirmation.
6. Cross-check one fast aggregator only as a backup, never as the primary source.
7. State any UNKNOWN or PROJECTED availability before ranking.
8. If a top pick depends on an unconfirmed player, lower confidence or move it down.

============================================================
2026-05-27 COMPREHENSIVE RESULTS-LOG AUDIT
============================================================

AUDIT SUMMARY:
- The log shows that raw item selection is less of a problem than ranking calibration. The adjusted record is dragged down mainly by bottom-pick penalties, meaning the analysis often identifies the right game script but wrongly buries a live path.
- Explicit bottom-pick penalty rows are concentrated in cricket/T20, MLB, NBA/WNBA, NPB/KBO, and first-half soccer. This means future rankings need a stricter fourth-pick gate.
- Only two logged top-pick penalties are currently explicit, but both were severe: an NBA playoff availability/adjustment miss and an IPL pitch-report/boundary miss. Future top picks must pass a stronger evidence-conflict test.
- The most repeated failure types are: projected-band mistakes, treating overlapping options as opposites, overusing stale H2H/rematch data, underweighting confirmed availability, underweighting current pitch/venue/weather, and failing to rerank after live phase information.

GLOBAL TOP-PICK GATE:
- Before ranking item 1, ask whether it is supported by at least two independent current signals: confirmed lineup/availability, current venue/pitch/weather, same-series phase data, recent role/usage, tactical comments, or recent form.
- Do not make a narrow exact-threshold item the top pick if a wider overlapping item has more ways to win inside the projected band.
- Do not make a top pick that depends on PROJECTED ONLY availability, unresolved injury/news conflict, unconfirmed toss/XI, unconfirmed goalie/starter, or an uncertain import/bench role unless all other options are clearly thinner.
- If the strongest evidence source directly contradicts the top pick, such as a road-like pitch report against a powerplay under or a creator absence against an early over, demote the pick unless there is clear stronger counter-evidence.

GLOBAL BOTTOM-PICK GATE:
- The fourth-ranked item must be outside the most likely projected band or require the most fragile game script. If the expected band includes the fourth item winning, it cannot be fourth.
- Before placing any item fourth, write the shortest possible "how this wins" path. If that path is ordinary rather than extreme, move it above a thinner option.
- Do not rank a direct low over, broad under, +1.5 cushion, or low first-half goal over last merely because the opposite side is preferred. The fourth slot is for the least likely path, not the least preferred side of a pair.
- If both overlapping items can win together, neither should be buried unless the projected band strongly misses that overlap.

PROJECTED-BAND RULE:
- For every card, define the likely band first, then rank items by how much of that band they cover.
- Include push points for whole-number totals. Treat 7, 8, 9, 10, 11-type baseball bands and 40-50 Q1 / 80-100 1H basketball bands explicitly.
- Use the band to catch contradictions: a projected 55-64 T20 powerplay means a PP Over 57.5 cannot be fourth; a projected 6-8 MLB game means both Under 8.5 and Over 6.5 can be live.

PHASE-SEPARATION RULE:
- Never package phase markets together unless the same mechanism controls both.
- Basketball Q1 and 1H can split through Q2 bench scoring, defensive adjustment, foul pressure, or shooting regression.
- Cricket powerplay and full innings can split through wickets in hand, middle-over spin, death hitting, impact substitute, or chase/dew context.
- CRICKET CHASE-CAP RULE (2026-06-07, JB Bruges vs Ghent Gladiators, EUT20 Belgium): a SECOND-INNINGS total market is structurally capped by the target. The chasing side's innings terminates on the winning run (or after 20 overs), so its final total cannot exceed ~target + a few runs. When a 2nd-innings total line is set far ABOVE the chase target (e.g. Over/Under 254.5 while the side is only chasing 167 after the 1st innings made 166/6), the UNDER is a ~99.9% structural lock and the OVER is a near-impossibility - rank Under #1 and Over as the clear least-likely bottom, and treat this as a STRUCTURAL fade, NOT a complementary-pair coinflip. Always confirm the 1st-innings total and toss first: the line only makes sense once you know the chase target. The genuine coinflip in such a card is the powerplay/phase market, not the capped total.
- CRICKET CHASE POWERPLAY vs REQUIRED RATE: when ranking a 2nd-innings powerplay (first-6-overs) over/under, compare the line's implied rate to the required run rate. If the chasing side needs ~8.35/ov (167 off 20) and the powerplay line implies only ~7.9/ov (47.5), the field-restriction phase is the EASIEST place to meet/beat the required rate with wickets in hand, so lean the powerplay OVER on a batting-friendly, short-boundary ground with explosive top-order batters - UNDER needs an early-wicket or cautious start. But keep it low confidence (~55/45): associate/EU minnow cricket has high early-collapse variance on new-laid grass pitches with an international new-ball bowler (e.g. Parnell).
- CRICKET rain/DLS void awareness for live cards: at rain-affected venues (EUT20 Belgium Stars Arena Hofstade had a Match-1 DLS finish and a Match-2 abandonment on the same opening days), flag abandonment/void and DLS-recalculation risk on any live 2nd-innings market. Rain does not let a chase total exceed its structural cap, but it can VOID phase markets or shorten the innings, so settle phase legs only against the official over-by-over once available.
- Baseball early starter quality and full-game total can split through bullpen exposure, defensive mistakes, and late pinch-hit/bench matchups.
- Soccer first-half Over 0.5 and Under 2.5 can both win; compare 0-0 risk against 3+ goal risk instead of treating them as opposites.

CURRENT-EVIDENCE PRIORITY:
- Current confirmed availability beats season averages.
- Current pitch report beats old venue averages.
- Match-window weather beats full-day weather.
- Same-series phase data beats older H2H only after a repeatability test.
- Coach/player comments count only when they describe tactical intent, workload, availability, tempo, role, or matchup changes and align with confirmed team news.
- If current evidence and historical evidence conflict, state the conflict and lower confidence instead of forcing a strong ranking.

CRICKET / T20 AUDIT RULES:
- Live or official pitch reports are high-priority. If the report says road, 220 par, short boundaries, hard surface, fast outfield, or true carry, modest powerplay and innings overs cannot be buried without concrete swing/seam, XI, or bowling-form evidence.
- Toss and confirmed XI reset the model. Pre-toss analysis must be rerun after toss if the batting side, Impact Player path, overseas balance, powerplay hitters, wicketkeeper, or death bowling changes.
- Wickets in hand are the main live ceiling control. A 54/3 powerplay at Wankhede or a 120/5 middle state can make a high innings over fragile even if the venue is batting-friendly.
- Missing named openers does not automatically kill a modest over if replacement batters have boundary profiles and the pitch/boundaries support pace-on scoring.
- At altitude or short-boundary grounds, do not put low powerplay overs last when the expected powerplay band includes the low/mid-60s.

MLB AUDIT RULES:
- Build a side/total cluster before ranking: 3-5, 5-7, 7-8, 8-10, 10+.
- In low-run clusters, +1.5 cushions and broad high unders often outrank no-cushion moneylines. Do not bury either +1.5 side unless multi-run separation is clearly more likely than a one-run/close game.
- Do not overcorrect by burying a favorite ML when the favorite's ordinary win path is still strong. Compare favorite ML against the cushion, not against an imaginary guarantee.
- Starter edge is fragile. Model early-exit, pitch-count, opener/bullpen-game classification, recent workload, command/WHIP, and homer/contact profile.
- Bullpen and defense are not afterthoughts. Poor routes, weak arms, infield range, walk traffic, and tired relief can turn modest overs and underdog cushions live.
- Park and weather must be exact: roof, marine layer, wind direction, humidity, temperature, and same-series scoring are all current-context inputs.

BASKETBALL AUDIT RULES:
- For NBA/WNBA and American basketball, official injury reports and verified team/beat X/Twitter updates are mandatory availability gates. StatMuse is required for stats but not for final availability.
- Basketball pace must be explicit, not implied from points per game. Before ranking any NBA/WNBA or basketball total/spread, record season pace/possessions per game, last 5 and last 10 game pace, home/away pace, phase pace where relevant, and whether the opponent speeds teams up or slows them down. If official pace is unavailable, estimate possessions from FGA + 0.44*FTA - ORB + TO for both teams and label it ESTIMATED.
- Separate pace from efficiency. A slow team with elite shooting can still clear a low over, and a fast team with poor shot quality can stay under. Rank totals only after combining possession volume with offensive rating, defensive rating, eFG/TS, turnover rate, free-throw rate, offensive rebounding, transition frequency, and bench scoring.
- For WNBA/NBA early-season cards, compare season pace against last 3/5 game pace because small-sample schedules, injuries, and lineup experimentation can distort PPG. When pace and PPG disagree, explain which one controls the projection.
- For spreads, pace changes margin risk. Slow pace increases close-game and underdog-cushion value; fast pace raises separation/blowout paths if one team has a clear efficiency, turnover, rebounding, or bench edge.
- WNBA early-season totals need a late-game scoring-path check before ranking high unders first. Even strong defensive teams can clear 176-180 if both sides have efficient star guards, bench/frontcourt replacement scoring, offensive rebounding, transition chances, or foul/free-throw pressure. Missing one star scorer is not automatically an under if assists, bench usage, and interior finishing replace that usage.
- A missing primary or secondary creator affects Q1 as well as Q2 if it changes spacing, pressure release, turnovers, and half-court shot quality.
- In playoff rematches, run a repeatability test before carrying over a prior fast Q1/1H. Identify whether the prior score came from hot shooting, transition leakage, fouls, turnovers, or a failed coverage that can be corrected.
- Home response and coach/player "start faster" comments matter, but they must be weighed against confirmed injuries rather than used as generic motivation.
- For live Q1 updates, calculate the exact points needed for each 1H item before applying Q2 regression. A cooling Q2 can still clear the half if the required number is low.
- For smaller leagues and PBA, import clearance and active status are binary. Expected paperwork or expected replacement imports are not enough.
- For winner leans, separate early scoring from late-game control. Fourth-quarter defending-champion response, title-clinch pressure, and safe-vs-survival motivation can flip sides even when early totals are right.

KBO / NPB AUDIT RULES:
- KBO rain and venue are core inputs, not notes. If rain threatens before the fifth inning, add a void/no-game risk line and do not overstate any scored edge.
- KBO venue changes must be verified from official/Korean sources before using park assumptions.
- NPB draw and extra-inning rules make +1.5 cushions more valuable in low-scoring games. Do not bury a cushion side in a projected pitching duel.
- In NPB, starting-pitcher edge needs emergency-exit and opposing-starter workload checks. One injury or early hook can flip both side and total logic.

BASEBALL REST / DEFENSE / FIELD-CONDITION CHECKLIST:
- Before ranking any MLB, KBO, NPB, or other baseball card, record starting-pitcher rest days, previous start date, previous pitch count, recent innings workload, recent velocity/command trend if available, injury/return status, and likely pitch-count or manager leash. A starter on short rest, coming off a high-pitch outing, returning from injury, or showing command/velocity decline must be downgraded for both side and full-game under logic.
- Record bullpen rest and relief-pitcher availability, not only bullpen season ERA. Check prior 1, 2, 3, and 5 days: relievers used, pitches thrown, back-to-back usage, extra-inning exposure, travel after night game, closer/setup availability, handedness availability, and whether a long reliever/bulk arm is fresh. If leverage relievers are tired or unavailable, full-game unders and favorite leads are less stable.
- Separate starter thesis from full-game thesis. A starter edge can support first-5 or early-inning suppression, but full-game sides/totals need bullpen rest, bullpen quality, defensive quality, bench/platoon options, and late-game manager path.
- Record fielding and error risk by team and by likely starting defenders. Use team fielding percentage/errors, recent errors, defensive efficiency, OAA/DRS/UZR when available, catcher defense/framing/throwing, infield range, outfield route/arm quality, and whether any player is out of natural position. Poor defense can extend innings, inflate pitch counts, create unearned runs, and make modest overs and underdog cushions more likely.
- For KBO/NPB or leagues without Statcast-style defensive metrics, use official errors, recent box-score errors, lineup position changes, local reporting, catcher/shortstop/center-field stability, and team fielding rankings where available. If unavailable, mark fielding-error risk UNKNOWN and cap confidence for close totals.
- Record field and surface conditions: venue, grass/turf, roof/open-air status, drainage, recent rain, wet infield/outfield, dirt quality, mound condition, foul territory, altitude, park dimensions, wall height, and whether the park suppresses or boosts home runs, doubles/triples, and foul outs. Wet or slick fields can increase errors and singles, while heavy air or damp grass can suppress carry.
- Record match-window weather, not whole-day weather: temperature, humidity, wind speed/direction by inning window, rain probability during game, marine layer/air density, roof status, and delay risk. State whether weather most likely causes carry boost, carry suppression, sloppy defense, starter interruption, bullpen disruption, or void/no-game risk.
- For totals, explicitly state the run cluster after these checks: low 3-5, controlled 5-7, middle 7-9, high 9-11, or blow-up 12+. Then rank broad unders, modest overs, moneylines, and run cushions by how much of that cluster each item covers.
- For run cushions, fielding and bullpen rest can be as important as starter matchup. A rested bullpen and clean defense support +1.5/+2.5 cushions in low/middle bands; tired relief and poor defense increase late multi-run separation risk.
- Never bury a modest over or an underdog cushion simply because the preferred starter is better. First ask whether tired relievers, weak fielders, weather carry, or sloppy field conditions create ordinary paths for extra runs or a close cover.

BASEBALL PLAYER FORM WINDOW REQUIREMENT:
- Batters: compare last 5/10/15/20 games against season and career/multi-season baseline for plate appearances, AVG/OBP/SLG/OPS, wOBA/wRC+ where available, K%, BB%, ISO, hard-hit/barrel/exit velocity where available, platoon split, lineup spot, recent rest, and injury status.
- Starting pitchers: compare last 5/10 starts against season and career/multi-season baseline for innings, pitch count, rest days, ERA/FIP/xFIP/SIERA where available, WHIP, K-BB%, HR/FB, ground-ball rate, velocity, pitch mix, command, quality starts, times-through-order decline, and manager leash.
- Relief pitchers: compare last 5/10/15 appearances against season and career/multi-season baseline for days since last pitch, pitches thrown, back-to-back usage, ERA/FIP, WHIP, K-BB%, inherited runners, leverage role, closer/setup availability, and handedness matchup.
- Fielders: compare last 5/10/15/20 starts against season and career/multi-season baseline for errors, fielding percentage, OAA/DRS/UZR where available, catcher framing/throwing, position changes, range concerns, and out-of-position risk.
- Sort each key player's current window against historical baseline before using that player to upgrade/downgrade a side, run line, total, first-5 angle, or late-game bullpen/defense path.
- Treat recent improvement as more reliable when the last 10/15/20 windows agree and the mechanism is visible. Treat career reputation as less reliable when the player has a sustained current drop plus injury, age, velocity, role, command, contact-quality, or defensive-position evidence.
- When a starting pitcher has historical upside but a tiny ugly current sample after injury/absence, do not automatically make the opposing run cushion the top pick. Separate "current sample is bad" from "true talent has collapsed"; if the opponent offense is inconsistent, stranded-run risk is high, and the total band is more stable than the side, prioritize overlap totals over the side cushion.

SOCCER AUDIT RULES:
- Current confirmed XI beats stale H2H. If current attackers and defensive absences point to transition chances, do not let old exact-one-goal halves bury Over 0.5.
- For first-half goal cards, map the exact overlap: Over 0.5 plus Under 2.5 wins at 1 or 2 goals; Over 0.5 plus Under 1.5 wins only at exactly 1 goal.
- Compare the likelihood of 0-0, exactly 1, exactly 2, and 3+ first-half goals before ranking.

AFL AUDIT RULES:
- Weather must be match-window weather, not full-day weather. Record the hour before bounce, each quarter window, and the hour after.
- If live scoring shows a favorite already at 80+ by halftime or leading by 50+, override pregame under logic and rerank from territory dominance, repeat inside-50s, transition speed, and opponent defensive collapse.

RETROSPECTIVE TAGGING RULE:
- Every future miss must tag the main error category: availability, source conflict, projected band, phase split, venue/pitch/weather, stale H2H/rematch, motivation/context, live-state arithmetic, side-vs-total separation, or settlement/rule issue.
- Every strong hit should also record what was right so the process repeats correct behavior, not only avoids mistakes.
- If a user challenges a live or pending result, refresh the current score/state immediately and update the log before defending the old ranking.

MINIMUM PRE-FINAL CHECKLIST:
1. Pending log checked and settled if possible.
2. Event, teams, venue, start time, and market labels verified.
3. Availability/source status stated as CONFIRMED, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN.
4. Projected band written for the relevant phase/full game.
5. Top-pick gate passed.
6. Bottom-pick gate passed.
7. Main risk that would flip the ranking stated.

============================================================
2026-05-27 SECOND-PASS RESULTS-LOG CALIBRATION ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum comes from a second review of the prediction results log snapshot after 93 logged card rows and the summary record of 349 settled listed items. The main problem is not finding possible winners; it is ranking calibration under uncertainty, especially when a live path is ordinary but gets placed fourth, or when the prior miss causes an overcorrection on the next similar game.

PRE-PICK EVIDENCE LEDGER:
- Before final ranking, write a short ledger with five fields: projected band, evidence supporting item 1, evidence against item 1, ordinary path for item 4 to win, and the exact current source freshness for volatile facts.
- If the evidence against item 1 is a stronger or fresher source than the evidence for it, demote item 1 or cap confidence.
- If the ordinary path for item 4 uses normal game mechanics rather than an extreme tail event, item 4 cannot stay fourth.
- Do not skip the ledger because the card looks obvious. The largest penalties have usually come from a simple overlooked live path, not from an unknowable upset.

SOURCE-FRESHNESS RULE:
- For volatile inputs, record the time and source type when available: injury report, lineup, starter/goalie, team sheet/final 17, toss/XI, import clearance, pitch report, weather, roof, venue, and official game status.
- If a source has no visible timestamp or conflicts with a newer official/team/verified report, call it STALE or CONFLICTING and do not let it drive a top pick.
- Aggregators can support a fact only when they agree with official or verified current reporting. For availability, lineups, weather, venue, pitch, and game status, official/team/league/current broadcast evidence beats database averages.

SCENARIO-SPLIT RULE:
- If a key player/import/creator/starter/goalie/toss/XI/weather status is unresolved and materially affects the ranking, create two short scenario rankings or state that the pick is provisional.
- A single blended ranking is only allowed when both scenarios lead to the same top and bottom picks.
- If the top pick changes between scenarios, confidence is capped at Low-Medium and the answer must identify the trigger that would rerank the card.

POST-MISS OVERCORRECTION GUARD:
- After a miss, do not automatically flip the next similar matchup to the opposite side. First test whether the prior miss was caused by a repeatable mechanism or a one-game state.
- Ask: was it hot shooting, cold shooting, turnovers, foul rate, rain, early wickets, bullpen collapse, import absence, creator absence, tactical adjustment, or motivation? Then ask whether that exact mechanism is still present.
- A previous game can teach "unders were underrated" and the next game can still clear an over if the new context adds home response, pace intent, healthier spacing, better pitch, shorter boundaries, or an easier threshold.

SINGLE-ANCHOR FRAGILITY RULE:
- Do not make a top pick depend mainly on one person or one condition without a backup path. This applies to ace starters, high-usage creators, imports, openers, goalies, quarterbacks, pitch reports, roof/weather assumptions, and referee/umpire environments.
- Before final ranking, ask what happens if the anchor exits early, is limited, loses control, is replaced, or the condition changes. If that failure path also damages the bottom pick, rerank.
- Starter edge, player quality, or bowling reputation must be converted into current role evidence: workload, leash, recent command, confirmed active status, matchup role, and phase-specific impact.

MOTIVATION-WITH-MECHANISM RULE:
- Motivation is useful only when tied to a basketball pace/defense/rotation change, cricket batting intent, soccer pressing/directness, AFL territory/transition, baseball lineup/rest usage, or another observable mechanism.
- "Must win", "home response", "title clinch", "safe from relegation", or "revenge" cannot move a pick by itself. State how it changes shot profile, possession pace, defensive pressure, substitution pattern, risk tolerance, or late-game control.

SETTLEMENT AND PENDING HYGIENE:
- Pending and live rows must be settled or explicitly carried forward before a new prediction. If the game was void, abandoned, no-game, DLS-shortened, overtime-included, extra-time-excluded, or push-eligible, record the settlement rule before grading.
- For pending rows, do not use partial wording as final retrospective. Refresh the final score/state, phase splits, and settlement rule, then replace the live note with a final outcome.

SPORT-SPECIFIC SECOND-PASS ADDITIONS:
- NBA/WNBA and other basketball: build a series-response matrix in rematches. For each prior game, identify whether the early total came from shooting variance, transition leakage, turnovers, fouls, coverage failure, creator absence, or bench minutes. Rank Q1 and 1H only after deciding which mechanisms are repeatable.
- NBA/WNBA and other basketball: when a team is coming off a bad loss, weigh "start faster" comments against actual creation. A home response can lift Q1 pace, but missing creators can still damage spacing and shot quality. The ranking must show which force wins and why.
- Cricket/T20: write the exact pitch-report descriptors before ranking. If the report says road, 220 par, short boundaries, hard surface, fast outfield, true carry, or similar batting language, a powerplay or innings under cannot be item 1 unless current swing/seam, bowling form, or confirmed batting weakness directly supports it.
- Cricket/T20: named bowling strength is not enough by itself. Check actual recent powerplay wickets/economy, death role, boundary prevention, and whether the pitch/boundaries weaken that bowling edge.
- MLB/KBO/NPB: side picks need a close-game map. If the projected run cluster is low or middle, compare moneyline, +1.5, broad under, and modest over by one-run/two-run paths rather than team reputation.
- MLB/KBO/NPB: weather-delay and official-game risk must be separated from scoring direction. Rain can mean void/no-game, dampened carry, bullpen disruption, sloppy defense, or starter interruption; state which path is most likely.
- Smaller basketball/PBA: import or lead-guard availability is binary until confirmed. If paperwork/clearance is not confirmed, model the team without that player for Q1 and 1H, not only for full game.
- Soccer first-half cards: rank by exact goal-count buckets: 0-0, exactly 1, exactly 2, and 3+. Do not use old H2H exact-one-goal halves without checking current XIs, creators, defenders, and tactical intent.

============================================================
2026-05-27 SOURCE QUALITY AND INFORMATION UPGRADE ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum upgrades where information comes from. The previous results log shows that many misses came from stale, incomplete, or lower-authority information: projected lineups treated as confirmed, old venue averages beating current pitch reports, stale H2H beating current XI/availability, and fast aggregator pages lagging official status.

SOURCE TIERING:
- TIER 0 - CURRENT OFFICIAL EVENT CONTROL: official league/team match centre, official injury report, official lineup/team sheet/final 17/inactives, toss/Playing XI, official scorecard, official gamebook, official venue/roof/weather status, official broadcast pitch report. These sources control volatile facts.
- TIER 1 - OFFICIAL OR PRIMARY STAT DATA: NBA.com/stats, WNBA.com/stats, MLB.com, Baseball Savant/Statcast, NHL.com stats, NFL Next Gen Stats, AFL/NRL official stats, official cricket scorecards, NPB.jp, KBO/Naver official-style game centres, and official club/federation stat pages.
- TIER 2 - SPECIALIST ANALYTICS AND DATABASES: FanGraphs, Baseball Reference, Pro Football Reference, FBref/StatsBomb/Opta where available, Understat, Natural Stat Trick, MoneyPuck, Wheelo Ratings, Squiggle, AFL Tables, FootyWire, Statiz, SportsNavi/Yahoo Japan, RealGM, Proballers, FIBA LiveStats, and league-specific historical databases.
- TIER 3 - VERIFIED NEWS AND LOCAL REPORTING: official club accounts, team PR accounts, verified beat reporters, reputable local-language outlets, press conferences, game notes, and trusted local recaps.
- TIER 4 - AGGREGATORS AND LIVE-SCORE PAGES: ESPN, CBS, FOX, Flashscore, SofaScore, Livesport, RotoWire, Lineups.com, FantasyPros, Covers score pages, and similar pages. Use these for speed, cross-checking, and timestamps, not as the final authority for volatile facts.
- TIER 5 - PREDICTION, BETTING, FAN, AND FORUM CONTENT: betting previews, tipster pages, unsourced social posts, forum comments, and model-pick pages. Do not use these as evidence for a research-only prediction. At most, use them to discover a fact that must then be verified from a higher tier.

STATMUSE AND FAST-QUERY RULE:
- StatMuse is useful for fast statistical discovery in American sports, but it is not the final source for availability, starters, lineups, injuries, scratches, bench roles, game status, or official settlement.
- If a StatMuse answer affects the ranking, cross-check the underlying fact against official stats, official box scores, NBA/WNBA stats, MLB.com/Baseball Savant, NHL.com, NFL.com, or another primary database before final confidence.
- If StatMuse conflicts with official stats, official source wins. If StatMuse gives a broad trend but official/advanced sources show role, lineup, or phase-specific conflict, use the official/advanced context.

FOUR-SOURCE WORKFLOW BEFORE RANKING:
1. Event control source: official fixture, start time, venue, home/away, competition, game status, and market-label sanity.
2. Availability source: official injury report, official lineup/team sheet/inactives/toss XI/final 17/starters/import clearance/goalie starter.
3. Statistical source: official stats or specialist analytics for the exact market type, including phase splits where relevant.
4. Context source: weather/roof/pitch, coach/player comments, local beat reports, travel/rest, motivation, and settlement rules.

SOURCE FRESHNESS WINDOWS:
- NBA/WNBA injury and starters: use the latest official injury report plus team/PR/verified updates close to tip. The official NBA injury report can update repeatedly through the day; WNBA teams report by 5 p.m. local the day before non-back-to-back games and by 1 p.m. local for the second game of a back-to-back.
- MLB: use MLB.com Starting Lineups/Gameday for confirmed lineups, starters, batting order, handedness, catcher, bench, bullpen, and game status. Probable starters are not the same as confirmed lineups.
- Cricket/T20: toss, Playing XI, impact/substitute options, pitch report, and captain comments reset the model. Pre-toss analysis is provisional for team innings and powerplay markets.
- Soccer: use official league/club lineups once available. Premier League lineups are available about 75 minutes before kickoff on official platforms; other leagues often release 60-75 minutes before kickoff.
- NRL: Tuesday 21-man lists are not final. Check 24-hour 19-man cutdown and final 17 one hour before kickoff.
- AFL: check official team selections, late changes, substitutes, and final team sheets close to match start. For match-window weather, use hourly venue weather rather than full-day rain probability.
- NFL: official inactives are the final gate and are normally released 90 minutes before kickoff. Injury/practice reports before that are important but not final.
- NHL: projected lineups and goalies are not confirmed. Use NHL.com, team/PR accounts, verified beat/warmup reports, and official game status for final goalie, scratches, lines, and power-play units.
- KBO/NPB: use local-language source stacks. KBO should prioritize KBO official/Naver/team sources, with MyKBO as English backup. NPB should prioritize NPB.jp announced starters, player registration/deregistration, official club posts, Yahoo Japan/SportsNavi game pages, and Japanese beat reports.
- Weather: for United States venues, use NWS hourly forecasts when available. For Australia, use BOM. For global events, Open-Meteo is useful for hourly forecasts because it combines national weather-service model outputs; still prefer a national meteorological service when it is available and specific enough.

SOURCE-CONFLICT PROTOCOL:
- If sources conflict, rank them by authority, timestamp, and specificity. Current official league/team source beats aggregator; current local verified reporter beats old preview; live broadcast pitch report beats old venue average.
- If two high-authority sources conflict inside the final hour, write both statuses and create a scenario split. Do not hide the conflict inside one confident ranking.
- If only lower-tier sources are available for a volatile fact, mark it UNKNOWN or PROJECTED and cap confidence.

ADVANCED-DATA CAUTIONS:
- Baseball Savant/Statcast expected stats are strong process signals because they use batted-ball quality and tracking inputs, but they do not replace confirmed lineup, park, weather, bullpen, or starter leash.
- Soccer xG varies by provider. FBref/StatsBomb, Opta, Understat, and other models can differ because they use different contextual inputs. Use xG directionally, and do not mix providers without noting the source.
- Hockey public xG and goalie goals-saved-above-expected models differ by shot-quality assumptions. Use MoneyPuck/Natural Stat Trick as process signals, but confirm goalie start, workload, defensive pairings, and special-teams context.
- Aggregator "projected starters" or "expected lineups" must not be upgraded to confirmed unless tied to a Tier 0/Tier 1 source.

LOCAL-LANGUAGE SEARCH REQUIREMENT:
- For non-English leagues, search at least one local-language source or local-language query when the market depends on lineups, imports, injuries, venue, weather, or official game status.
- KBO query terms to use where needed: Korean-language terms for starting lineup, starting pitcher, roster/entry status, rain cancellation, lineup, and team names in Korean.
- NPB query terms to use where needed: Japanese-language terms for announced starter, starting lineup, public roster notice, registration/deregistration, rain cancellation, and team names in Japanese.
- Soccer/local Europe and South America: use official club/league match centre plus local terms for "lineup", "injury", "suspended", "rotation", and "press conference" when English sources are thin.

ANALYSIS OUTPUT SOURCE NOTE:
- Every prediction answer should include a compact source note, not a long bibliography: official status source, availability source, statistical source, weather/pitch/context source, and any UNKNOWN/PROJECTED facts.
- If research-only/no-odds mode is active, state that odds/market movement sources were intentionally not checked and do not use sportsbook or betting-preview sites to fill missing evidence.
