2026-05-27 PENDING-LOG SETTLEMENT LEARNING ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum comes from settling the three pending rows from 2026-05-27: Twins 5-3 White Sox in 11 innings, Thunder 127-114 Spurs, and Phillies 4-3 Padres.

MLB 7-8 RUN CLUSTER CONFIRMATION:
- Twins-White Sox landed exactly at 8 runs and Phillies-Padres landed exactly at 7 runs. This confirmed that a broad under and a modest over can both be correct when the projected band is 7-8.
- In overlapping MLB totals, do not rank the modest over as weak merely because the broad under is strongest. A low/middle scoring script can still cash both sides.
- Extra innings can cash a modest over without invalidating the broad under. If the card includes full-game totals, model the automatic-runner/extra-inning path for 7.0, 7.5, 8.0, and 9.0 thresholds.

MLB CLOSE-BAND MONEYLINE RULE:
- If the projected total band is 6-8, no-cushion moneylines remain ordinary paths unless one team has a clear multi-run separation edge.
- Do not bury the opponent ML solely because the starter profile points the other way. One solo-homer cluster, one quality-start rebound, one bullpen inning, or one extra-inning swing can flip a close-band game.
- In low/middle bands, compare moneyline paths against +1.5 cushions and broad totals by score shapes: 3-2, 4-3, 5-3, 5-4, 6-2, and 6-4. If several normal shapes include the supposed bottom ML, it cannot be fourth.
- Petco/cool weather, roof/park suppression, or a strong starter can suppress total ceiling while still allowing HR-driven side volatility. Park suppression is not the same as side certainty.

NBA Q2 FOUL/FREE-THROW OVER PATH:
- Spurs-Thunder Game 5 reached a 127-point first half after a 56-point Q1 and 71-point Q2. The missed path was second-quarter foul pressure and free throws, not only pace.
- For NBA playoff first-half markets, model Q2 as its own phase: bench attack, bonus timing, defensive physicality, officiating/foul accumulation, matchup hunting, and role-player shooting can create a large second-quarter surge.
- If Q1 lands high enough that the 1H over needs only around 50-52 points, do not keep the 1H over last unless Q2 bench/whistle/shot-quality evidence is clearly under-friendly.
- A missing creator can damage spacing, but replacement starters and bench guards can still create enough pressure if the home team has response intent and the opponent fouls or overhelps.

POST-CORRECTION BALANCE RULE:
- The Game 4 under lesson and Game 5 over lesson were both true in context. Future rematch analysis must hold both before ranking: prior defensive correction, current home response, current lineup replacements, and threshold distance.
- After a live correction, avoid anchoring on only the first corrected idea. Re-run the full projected band from the new score, then test the most likely Q2/late-inning/late-period mechanism before finalizing the top and bottom picks.


============================================================
2026-05-25 PENDING-RESULT SWEEP LEARNING ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum comes from resolving the 2026-05-24 pending results log entries after final scores and phase splits became available.

GLOBAL PROCESS:
- At the start of every new request, open the prediction results log first and settle any pending/live rows before producing new analysis.
- Do not run an hourly/background automation sweep unless the user explicitly asks for one. The normal workflow is request-triggered only: resolve the previous/pending log entry when the next query is provided.
- For all American sports queries, use StatMuse as a required first statistical lookup for team/player trends, game logs, recent form, splits, scoring, matchup, and market-specific stat context. This applies to NBA, WNBA, MLB, NFL, NHL, NCAA/college sports where StatMuse supports the query, and other US/American franchise-sport contexts where available. StatMuse is for statistics; it does not override official availability or lineup sources.
- For all American sports queries, check X/Twitter for starting lineup, injury, bench, inactives, scratches, minutes/snap/TOI limits, bullpen/bench availability, starting pitcher/goalie/QB status, and late role news. Prioritize official team/PR accounts, league accounts, and verified beat reporters. If X/Twitter cannot be verified or is unavailable, explicitly state that and cap confidence when availability matters.
- MLB close-to-first-pitch lesson: if StatMuse, ESPN, or an aggregator conflicts with MLB.com on same-day record, game status, starters, lineup, or weather/roof context, treat MLB.com as controlling for volatile facts and use StatMuse only for statistical form/trend context. When official lineups show a likely low run band, do not automatically bury +1.5; rank it below the under only when favorite form, starter edge, and recent multi-run context clearly point that way.
- MLB overlapping total lesson: when the user gives Over 7.0 and Under 9.0 together, explicitly model the 8-run middle band where both can win and the 9-run push risk for Under 9.0. A strong starter edge can make Under 9.0 the safest top pick, but warm weather, homer-friendly lineups, and tired or mediocre bullpens mean Over 7.0 should not be buried unless the expected cluster is clearly 3-5 total runs.
- For each settled row, update the final score/state, phase scores, listed-item W-L, bottom-pick penalty status, winner lean if present, and retrospective.
- After settlement, update this combined sports doc with any durable lessons before starting a fresh prediction query.
- The research cutoff for a new analysis is the exact prompt time in the user's active timezone. Later information is valid only for settlement, live correction, and retrospective learning.
- Search X/Twitter or reliable indexed social sources for late availability and lineup news. If no verified update is found, explicitly record that no verified X/Twitter availability update was found and rely on official league/team pages or trusted news reports.

BASKETBALL LESSONS:
- NBA same-series phase lesson: for playoff rematches, use the actual series Q1 and 1H splits before broad full-game scoring. If every Q1 has cleared a low 50s threshold but only some first halves have cleared, rank Q1 over above 1H over unless a late lineup/injury change specifically damages the starting-unit scoring path. If the injury mainly changes bench/second-quarter creation, downgrade the first-half over more than the first-quarter over.
- NBA playoff injury-response lesson: if a primary/secondary creator is ruled out and the opponent's most likely adjustment is defensive physicality rather than faster offense, do not make the low-threshold Q1 under the bottom pick just because previous same-series Q1s cleared. Treat creator absences as first-quarter risks too when they affect spacing, turnover pressure, and the quality of early half-court possessions.
- NBA top-pick failure lesson: when a first-ranked early-over pick fails because a creator absence and playoff defensive adjustment crush the favorite's early offense, the fix is not just "lower confidence." The future ranking must move the corresponding under above at least one over unless confirmed lineup/rotation evidence shows the missing creation has been replaced.
- NBA coach/player comment lesson: pregame comments about specific adjustments should be treated as evidence, not colour. Wembanyama explicitly focusing on facilitating, rebounding, and forcing help, plus Spurs staff concern about non-Wembanyama minutes, pointed toward a Game 4 defensive/rotation correction more than a pure tempo spike. When comments describe tactical fixes, connect them to phase markets before final ranking.
- NBA conflicting-availability lesson: if official/live pages, local reports, and X/Twitter-indexed posts conflict on a high-usage creator's status inside the final hour, cap confidence and do not rank elevated early overs first unless the player is confirmed active and the expected role is normal. At 55.5 Q1 and 107.5 1H type thresholds, a confirmed or plausible creator absence plus an opponent defensive adjustment should put at least one under above both overs.
- NBA playoff response lesson: do not let injury absences fully suppress a home Game 5 response when the favorite has clear coach/player comments about needing to start faster after being punched first in consecutive games. If the team still has an elite primary creator plus defensive-transition guards who can score opportunistically, a Q1 over can clear even while the broader creation concern remains valid.
- PBA import clearance is binary. A replacement import being "expected" is not enough; confirm paperwork, clearance, and active status close to tip. TNT-Meralco Game 3 showed Malick Diouf was expected but not processed in time, so TNT played all-Filipino and the game landed at only 77-75 with a 36-point Q1 and 77-point half.
- In PBA import-change games, downgrade both Q1 and first-half overs if the replacement import is not confirmed active. Do not assume emotional energy alone creates an early over when offensive spacing, rim pressure, and import touches are gone.
- PBA playoff full-game totals need quarter-path modelling. Ginebra-Rain or Shine Game 3 had a 104-point first half but still finished 201 because Ginebra won Q3 by 29-12. A fast half does not automatically kill a full-game under if one team has a repeatable defensive adjustment quarter and late-game control.
- PBA Commissioner's Cup FINALS import-attrition lesson (2026 Ginebra-TNT, Season 50): a chain of import injuries does NOT automatically suppress finals scoring. TNT lost resident import Rondae Hollis-Jefferson (torn Achilles, out ~1yr), then replacement Bol Bol (7'3") also tore his Achilles, leaving 31-yo Chris McCullough (2019 Comm's Cup winner with SMB) as the third-choice import. Despite the downgraded import, Games 1 and 2 landed 202 and 195 because the LOCAL stars carried pace - Calvin Oftana dropped a conference-high 31/10 in Game 2, McCullough still posted 19/16, Brownlee 24/15, Abarrientos 21. Rule: rate the local-star scoring engine separately from the import tier. A weaker import lowers rim/spacing ceiling but does NOT bury a finals over when an elite local wing (Oftana) and a star opposing import (Brownlee) are both healthy and in rhythm.
- PBA finals full-game OVER robustness vs phase pairs: in the 2026 finals, even the "slow" game (G2: 39-pt Q1, 81-pt half) reached 195 via a second-half explosion (56-pt Q3). Full-game totals smooth quarter variance, so when BOTH prior series games clear the full-game line (195, 202 vs 192.5) but the Q1/1H pairs straddle their lines (Q1 55/39 around 45.5; 1H 101/81 around 92.5), rank the full-game over ABOVE the phase pairs and keep the phase pairs as true coinflips, NOT confident picks. Reserve the bottom slot for the genuine fade (full-game UNDER, which neither game produced), not a forced phase complement.
- PBA finals +1.5 cushion math in a 1-1 coinflip series: when the series is even and both games were single-digit (G1 Ginebra by 2 on a Brownlee buzzer-beater, G2 TNT by 7 but wire-to-wire control), the team WITH momentum getting +1.5 owns the larger covered region (wins outright OR loses by 1), so rank that cushion above the opponent's -1.5 lay. Momentum from a wire-to-wire Game 2 (led 78-59 after 3) is a legitimate tiebreaker for the side lean and the potential-winner call.
- For basketball finals or title-clinch games, separate early totals from side/winner modelling. Tartu-Kalev Game 3 cleared both early overs, but Tartu lost after a 28-15 fourth-quarter collapse. A 2-0 series lead and home clinch chance do not remove defending-champion response capacity.
- Late-season European basketball motivation can override home/away records. MoraBanc Andorra were fighting relegation while Lleida were already safe; Andorra's urgency and perimeter shooting turned a road game into a 116-88 blowout. Treat safe-team intensity as a real downgrade.
- When a user provides a live Q1 score, compute exact remaining points needed for every first-half item before applying regression logic. A high Q1 may cool in Q2, but the half over can still be nearly decided if only 30-40 more points are required.
- 2026-07-06 FIBA qualifier blowout-total lesson: New Zealand 129, Guam 75 (Q1 59, 1H 101, full 204) — every phase/total "under" lean lost and both bottom-ranked "over" legs won. "Blowout suppresses the total" only holds when the LEADING team's bench slows down or the trailing team can foul/stall; it does not hold when the trailing team is bottom-of-table/eliminated with a large season point-differential gap (Guam were 0-5, PD -110) and the favourite's reserves keep shooting efficiently (transition dunks, catch-and-shoot threes). Both teams here beat their own qualifier-window scoring averages. Rule: against a clearly overmatched, depth-less opponent, lean total/phase OVER even in a probable rout, and do not treat the bottom-of-list "over" complement as a near-certain loser.

CRICKET LESSONS:
- At Wankhede and similar high-scoring venues, a 211+ first-innings over still needs wickets in hand. RR reached only 205/8 after a 54/3 powerplay; lower-order hitting kept the score competitive but could not fully restore the high-over path.
- Do not bury the direct innings under once the live powerplay has multiple wickets down, even on a batting-friendly ground. Re-rank from the current score, wickets, balls left, death hitters available, and bowling matchups.
- For paired T20 markets, the likely middle band can make the broad under safer than the high over. A 200-211 innings band can cash under 211.5 while still feeling like a strong batting-venue score.

SOURCE AND SETTLEMENT NOTES:
- Prefer official league scorecards and trusted news recaps for final settlement, then use aggregators/social posts only as supporting context.
- For smaller leagues, official score pages may lag or index poorly. Cross-check multiple sources and record whether the phase split is official, trusted-news, or aggregator-derived.
- Do not treat an old live snapshot as final. Refresh after the event and overwrite partial/live wording once the final result is known.
BASKETBALL / GENERAL:

Use this file for basketball leagues outside the NBA or when a dedicated league file is unavailable.

CORE RULES:
- Do not guess.
- Do not use outdated lineup, injury, import, rotation, venue, rest, travel, form, or market data.
- Confirm both teams' starters/expected starters and active rotation players close to tipoff when available. If starting lineups are not released, use projected starters clearly labelled as PROJECTED and lower confidence.
- For questionable/probable/injured players, check official injury reports, official team/league accounts, and verified beat reporters on X/Twitter before game time. Classify each key player as CONFIRMED IN, CONFIRMED OUT, LIMITED/MINUTES RISK, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN.
- Adjust Q1, first-half, full-game pace/scoring, bench depth, defensive matchup, rebounding, and foul/rotation assumptions for confirmed inclusions/exclusions, late scratches, injury limitations, conditioning, and minutes restrictions.
- If the user explicitly says not to look at betting odds, lines, markets, CLV, sportsbooks, or market movement, do not open sportsbook, odds, market, or line-movement pages.
- Analyze only the user-provided thresholds/handicaps and research-based team, player, lineup, injury, import, rotation, bench, matchup, venue, rest, travel, pace, efficiency, and statistical data.
- The user-provided line or threshold may be referenced only as the item being ranked, not as evidence from the market.
- Ranked lists must run from most likely to least likely. The fourth-ranked item must be the single least-likely listed outcome; if it wins, log the normal item result plus one extra bottom-pick penalty loss and explain why it was underrated.
- When options are exact complements at the same threshold, such as Over 86.5 and Under 86.5, rank the more likely side higher and explicitly state the expected scoring band. The lower-ranked complement is not an independent outcome; it is the direct fail state.
- When options are overlapping thresholds, such as 1H Over 80.5 and 1H Under 92.5 or Q1 Over 39.5 and Q1 Under 47.5, identify the middle band where both can win. Rank the broadest middle-band covers highest, and make clear that the bottom pick may still be live if the expected band includes it.

SOURCE PRIORITY:
- Official league/team pages, official scorecards, official standings, and official social accounts.
- Official injury reports, starting lineup announcements, and verified team/beat-reporter X/Twitter updates for questionable players, late scratches, and minutes-limit news.
- Reliable statistical databases such as RealGM or league-specific stat pages.
- Reliable live-score/stat pages only for form, standings, box scores, and team/player statistics; ignore any odds sections.
- Local media and beat reports for match context, motivation, injuries, import changes, or rotation news.

NON-NBA DATA QUALITY RULES:
- If official injury/lineup/rotation data is unavailable, clearly state UNKNOWN and cap confidence.
- For smaller leagues, treat roster/import availability as high impact because one import or lead guard can heavily change scoring and margin.
- Separate standings motivation from pure form: a top seed chasing position can keep intensity high, while an eliminated team can still cover through loose pace or bench minutes.
- Treat consolation, third-place, dead-rubber, or "nothing to lose" basketball games differently from major playoff/title games. Reduced consequence can raise shot freedom, transition pace, bench usage, and early scoring, so match context can override recent low-scoring playoff history when the teams' offensive profiles support it.
- Do not assume a large favorite covers a large line just because of table position. Check recent winning margins, opponent losing margins, head-to-head margins, pace, and likely rotation management.
- For double-digit handicaps, downgrade favorite-cover confidence if the favorite has already secured playoff advantage or may manage minutes late.
- Collect quarter-by-quarter and half-by-half scoring where available, including late blowout/rotation swings, because smaller-league final margins can hide long stretches where the underdog controlled or covered comfortably.
- For paired quarter and half totals, compare whether the early scoring profile is more likely to start fast immediately or build through the second quarter. A first-half over can be stronger than a first-quarter over when Q1 pace is volatile but second-unit/defensive-drop-off scoring is likely.
- If recent H2H or same-league splits repeatedly show a lower Q1 followed by a high Q2, do not force Q1 over and first-half over together. It can be correct to rank first-half over above Q1 under when the expected path is a 41-44 Q1 and a 90+ first half.
- When revised basketball thresholds create a wide middle band, do not carry over a prior under/over ranking mechanically. A prior lean to 1H Under 86.5 can become 1H Over 80.5 at a lower threshold, while still ranking Under 92.5 first.
- When quarter/half thresholds are high exact complements, do not let recent full-game shootouts override direct same-season H2H phase splits. If the same matchup repeatedly produces Q1 and 1H totals below the user's thresholds, rank the unders first unless confirmed lineups, tempo comments, injuries, or tactical context clearly change the early scoring profile.
- In Israeli National League exact-complement Q1/1H cards, direct league H2H phase splits can outweigh poor season defensive averages. If league H2Hs sit below the thresholds but a cup meeting or late-season relegation/dead-rubber game was much faster, treat that faster data as over risk rather than the main ranking signal unless current lineups or motivation clearly match the shootout context.
- A low-looking Q1 over can still be the weakest item if multiple recent relevant Q1s landed exactly below the threshold and the opponent owns a strong early defense. Do not automatically rank a 37.5 or 38.5 Q1 over above the wider half-total middle-band cover.
- The opposite can also happen: a fast first quarter can still produce a first-half under if the second quarter cools through bench minutes, half-court execution, or defensive adjustment. Do not rank quarter and half overs as a package unless recent splits support both phases.
- In domestic top-v-bottom mismatches, the first half can be the cleaner over window if the favorite is likely to start strong before late rotation or garbage-time cooling. Use recent H2H quarter/half splits and favorite home scoring before assuming the mismatch suppresses totals.
- In WNBA early-season games, treat roster continuity, missing defenders, short benches, and opener quarter/half splits as high-impact. Early-season defensive timing can lag offensive aggression, so a first-half over can outrank a first-quarter over when both openers showed fast halves but the first quarter still has rotation/nerves volatility.
- For WNBA exact-complement Q1/1H cards, build separate phase bands from the last 2-3 games. If the Q1 data is split but first-half totals have repeated recovery paths through Q2 scoring, rank the 1H over above the Q1 over. If one team has a slow Q1 but the opponent's defense is allowing high-80s/90s scoring, do not make the 1H under the bottom unless the phase model supports a sustained half-court slowdown.
- For WNBA exact-complement cards near common early scoring bands, do not force certainty when both teams' recent Q1/1H averages sit within 1-2 points of the threshold. Current defensive quotes, foul rate, and returning creators can lean over, but if recent halves repeatedly land 80-86, keep confidence capped and explain that the under remains live.
- For WNBA expansion teams in the first few games, heavily weight offensive cohesion indicators: assists per made field goal, turnover count, three-point quality, and whether scoring came from free throws rather than clean half-court creation. A noisy home crowd and veteran names do not erase a 30-point Q1 or 67-point first half if the offense looked disjointed.
- WNBA expansion-team debut data must be widened after one game. A 27% FG debut can be real evidence of poor cohesion, but it can also regress immediately in Q1; if the next threshold is low and opponent pace supports it, do not rank the low Q1 over last merely because the opener was ugly. Prefer wide middle-band covers when both sides of an overlapping pair can cash.
- For WNBA expansion-team overlapping bands, separate the low-over and broad upper-under by phase. If the expansion team has volatile Q1 shot creation and questionable guard depth, a broad Q1 upper-under can outrank the low Q1 over even when the opponent's recent Q1s are fast. First-half middle-band covers can still be stronger because Q2 allows recovery from a cold or hot opening quarter.
- For WNBA early-season injury clusters, split offense and defense effects. Missing frontcourt scorers can drag that team's half total down, while also weakening interior defense and allowing the opponent to clear a modest phase over. Do not treat "injuries" as automatically under-only.
- For WNBA exact-complement injury-cluster cards, split Q1 and first-half effects instead of packaging all overs or all unders together. Missing frontcourt size can lift the opponent's early paint, free-throw, and rebound scoring while the injured team's own half-court offense remains fragile; this can make the first-half over stronger than the first-half under even when the Q1 sits close to the threshold.
- In best-of-three basketball playoff series, immediate series quarter/half splits can outweigh older season averages. If Games 1-2 both clear the first-half threshold but only one clears the first-quarter threshold, rank the first-half over above the Q1 over because the second quarter has shown recovery capacity.
- In playoff finals or promotion series with games on consecutive days at the same venue, treat the prior game's quarter and half splits as the strongest context signal. If Game 1 clears a Q1 or 1H threshold comfortably and the same rotations/venue are expected, do not downgrade Game 2 overs just because the stakes are high; require a concrete tactical, injury, or lineup reason.
- For 24-hour finals rematches, do a repeatability check before anchoring to Game 1 scoring. Identify whether Game 1's Q1/1H total came from hot shooting, transition leakage, foul/free-throw volume, turnovers, or poor coverage execution. If those are correction-friendly, rank broad high unders above narrow overs and cap Q1 over confidence.
- In BBL playoff Game 2 exact-complement cards, separate the full-game result from early phase splits. If Game 1 had a high final total but only a marginal Q1 over and a first-half under, and the upset was driven by hot three-point shooting, steals, opponent turnovers, or missed free throws, expect defensive and physicality adjustments before ranking Q1/1H overs.
- In playoff semifinal rematches, do not let a lopsided final score override the actual Q1/1H split. A favorite can win 90+ while the game starts at 30 Q1 / low-70s halftime; if the next thresholds are near 40.5 Q1 and 80.5 1H, direct phase suppression is the primary signal unless there is a clear home-pace or lineup change.
- For ABA semifinal exact-complement Q1/1H cards, do not downgrade overs only because of playoff stakes or neutral-site relocation. If direct H2Hs and immediate playoff games repeatedly clear the user's thresholds in Q1 and 1H, keep the over sides above the exact unders unless confirmed injuries remove primary creation or the venue/lineups clearly slow the game.
- In NBA playoff Game 1s with asymmetric Q1/1H totals, do not transfer regular-season H2H pace directly when a primary ball-handler or high-usage creator is ruled out late. Rebuild the phase band around the confirmed lineup, likely ball-handling replacement, turnover pressure, and whether the opponent is rested/healthy.
- In NBA playoff same-series rematches, weigh the most recent same-venue game but do not ignore the full series phase pattern. A first-quarter over can be more likely than a first-half over when Q1 has repeatedly started hot but Q2 has cooled through bench units, defensive adjustment, or slower half-court possessions.
- In PBA playoff rematches, heavily weight an immediate same-matchup quarter/half split from the prior game, especially when the matchup repeats within a few days and one side has a twice-to-beat advantage while the other must win. Do not overrate high-scoring blowouts against weaker or import-limited opponents if the direct matchup just showed a defensive/controlled early script.
- In NZNBL and other leagues with weaker pregame lineup transparency, use the most recent box-score starters/depth charts as PROJECTED only and cap confidence until official starters are available. Direct H2H first-half suppression can outrank recent full-game overs when the same projected cores are expected.
- For leagues that run curtain-raiser formats on the same date/fixture page, such as NZNBL Rapid League before the main NBL game, verify the competition label and tip time before using live scores. Do not treat Rapid League quarter/half scores as main-game evidence unless the user's market explicitly refers to Rapid League.
- For NZNBL high exact-complement Q1/1H totals, split season scoring from venue phase splits. A team can average 190+ combined full-game environments while its home Q1/1H starts repeatedly sit below 50.5 and 100.5; in that setup, rank the high unders first unless confirmed lineup changes or recent same-venue data clearly raise early pace.
- NZNBL settlement-availability note (2026-06-07, Southland Sharks vs Franklin Bulls): some NZNBL fixtures are not exposed with a final/quarter line in accessible sources for a long window after tip. Sofascore even labelled the 7 Jun Southland-Franklin fixture as "Rapid League," while Flashscore/Sharks-official/NZNBL carried only previews or the stale April recap. When repeated direct fetches (Flashscore match page, Sofascore team page, club site, league site) plus targeted searches all fail to expose a final, KEEP THE CARD PENDING and document the exhaustive attempt rather than forcing a settlement or fabricating a score. "Unsettleable in accessible sources" is the correct honest state; carry it forward and re-confirm main-NBL vs Rapid League before eventually grading.
- For NZNBL full-game totals, build a one-team offensive-collapse branch when one side has low shooting efficiency, weak home scoring, or recent H2H suppression. Moderate pace around 84-86 possessions can still finish far below 189 if one team scores single digits in Q1 or stays below 50 through three quarters. Do not rank a low over second solely because the opponent has a high away scoring average or poor defensive rating.
- In domestic top-seed-versus-low-seed playoff openers, separate elite-team Q1 defense from first-half scoring recovery. A heavy favorite can start controlled or defensive, then clear a high first-half number through Q2 bench depth, shooting variance, or mismatch pressure.
- Check pre-game and post-game coach/player press conference comments for tactical intent: pace, transition, defensive pressure, half-court emphasis, rebounding focus, bench/rotation usage, minute limits, and whether the team says it must start faster or tighten up defensively. Weight these comments more in rematches, playoff series, short turnarounds, and after a bad loss, but cross-check with actual lineups, injuries, and recent quarter/half splits.
- For full-game totals and spreads, check whether the competition grades overtime into the market result. If overtime counts, downgrade unders and large-favorite covers when the underdog has shown enough scoring/pace to force a late comeback or extra period.
- Before ranking a full-game under in any basketball league, compare it to first-half and quarter under logic. Smaller-league totals can flip late through foul-heavy endings, overtime, import-led scoring runs, or bench-quality gaps, so full-game unders need late-game support, not only a slow-start thesis.
- For totals, combine both teams' season PPG/PAPG with recent last-5 totals. If one team plays lower-scoring controlled games and the other plays high-scoring loose games, rank the total with medium confidence only.
- Indoor venues usually remove weather impact; note venue only if travel, court familiarity, or crowd context is material.

POST-QUERY RESULTS LOGGING:
- After each completed prediction query, update PREDICTION_RESULTS_LOG.md in the workspace root.
- If the game is live or result unavailable, mark the entry PENDING and update once known.
- Record the main lesson, especially misses caused by limited data, motivation/readiness, import availability, blowout rotation, pace mismatch, or threshold selection.
- Apply the bottom-pick penalty rule whenever the fourth-ranked item wins.


BASKETBALL / NBA:

Use this file for NBA pre-game and live prediction research.

CORE RULES:
- Do not guess.
- Do not use outdated injury, lineup, rest, rotation, venue, pace, or market data.
- Confirm both teams' official starters/expected starters and active rotation close to tipoff when available. If starters are not released, label projected starters as PROJECTED and lower confidence.
- For questionable/probable/injured players, check the official NBA injury report plus official team accounts and verified beat reporters on X/Twitter before game time. Classify key players as CONFIRMED IN, CONFIRMED OUT, LIMITED/MINUTES RISK, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN.
- Adjust Q1, first-half, full-game pace/scoring, defensive matchups, bench scoring, foul risk, and rotation compression for confirmed inclusions/exclusions, late scratches, injury limitations, conditioning, and minutes restrictions.
- If the user explicitly says not to look at betting odds, lines, markets, CLV, sportsbooks, or market movement, do not open sportsbook, odds, market, or line-movement pages.
- Analyze only the user-provided thresholds/handicaps and research-based team, player, injury, lineup, rotation, bench, matchup, venue, rest, travel, pace, efficiency, and statistical data.
- The user-provided line or threshold may be referenced only as the item being ranked, not as evidence from the market.

SOURCE PRIORITY:
- Official NBA injury report, official team reports, and official box scores.
- NBA.com/stats for pace, offensive rating, defensive rating, net rating, first-half splits, quarter splits, shooting, rebounding, turnovers, and lineup data.
- Team beat reporters and verified team accounts/X/Twitter updates for late scratches, questionable-player confirmations, minutes limits, starting lineups, and rotation changes.
- Basketball-Reference, ESPN, StatMuse, Cleaning the Glass, and other statistical sources for cross-checking when available.
- Weather is normally irrelevant for NBA totals unless travel disruption affects availability.

NBA TOTALS CHECKLIST:
- Confirm game state: regular season, playoffs, play-in, back-to-back, travel/rest, game number in series.
- Confirm venue and home/away context.
- Confirm injuries, probable starters, minutes restrictions, and late scratches.
- Separate full-game totals from first-half/quarter totals. Do not borrow the full-game read blindly for first-half markets.
- For overlapping thresholds such as Over 102.5 and Under 110.5, explicitly identify the middle band where both can win. Rank by whether the matchup is more likely to stay inside that band, break below it, or break above it, so the bottom two options represent the least likely outcomes.
- The fourth-ranked item must be the single least-likely listed outcome. If it wins, log the normal item result plus one extra bottom-pick penalty loss and explain the ranking failure in the retrospective.
- Collect quarter-by-quarter and half-by-half scoring where available, plus bench-minute stretches, foul/bonus timing, turnover bursts, and garbage-time scoring. Do not let a full-game total hide whether the first-half read was correct but the second-half/blowout path was missed.
- Before ranking a full-game under, compare it to the first-half under thesis. If the under is mainly based on early defensive game plans, starter minutes, slow starts, or playoff feel-out pace, but the game has overtime, late fouling, garbage-time, desperation-push, or Q3/Q4 pace risk, prefer the first-half under when provided.
- Full-game NBA unders require both early and late support: pace, offensive/defensive rating, Q4 scoring, bench scoring, foul rate, close-game/overtime risk, and blowout garbage-time risk should all agree.
- Compare season pace and playoff/recent pace.
- Compare offensive rating, defensive rating, effective field-goal percentage, three-point rate, free-throw rate, turnover rate, offensive rebounding, and bench scoring.
- For playoffs, lower expected pace unless both teams have shown they will run in the series or matchup.
- For Game 1 playoff totals, account for early tactical feel-out, longer starter minutes, shorter bench rotations, and defensive game-plan emphasis.
- Check pre-game and post-game coach/player press conference comments for tactical intent: pace, transition, half-court emphasis, defensive coverage changes, matchup adjustments, rotation/minute plans, and whether a team says it needs to start faster or defend harder. Weight comments more in playoff rematches and short turnarounds, but cross-check with lineups, injury status, usage, and quarter/half splits.
- Do not treat a low first-half threshold as automatically strong in playoff Game 1s. If both teams have elite rim protection, injured/limited creators, and clear defensive game-plan incentives, first-half unders can outrank modest overs even when regular-season scoring averages are high.
- For NBA same-series exact-complement Game 2 cards, make the immediate Game 1 Q1/1H split the primary signal, but run a repeatability test before burying overs. If Game 1 was low because of cold shooting or early turnovers while Q2 recovered, rank first-half overs above Q1 overs, but keep the unders first unless lineup/rotation changes clearly raise pace.
- For first-half totals, prioritize starting lineup offensive creation, early-game pace, foul risk, and whether stars are on minutes limits.
- For full-game totals, include bench quality, late-game foul/intentional-foul risk, blowout risk, and rotation compression.

INJURY / MINUTES RULES:
- A star returning from injury is not the same as a fully healthy star. Check for minutes restriction, conditioning, usage risk, and defensive mobility.
- If a high-usage creator is out or limited, downgrade overs unless the replacement usage has already produced efficient offense.
- If a primary defender or rim protector is out, upgrade opponent rim efficiency and free-throw paths.
- If a bench scorer is out, reduce non-starter scoring and increase dependence on starters.

POST-QUERY RESULTS LOGGING:
- After each completed prediction query, update PREDICTION_RESULTS_LOG.md in the workspace root.
- If the game is live or the result is unavailable, mark the entry PENDING and update it once the result is known.
- Record the main lesson, especially misses caused by injury/minutes assumptions, pace, playoff rotation compression, shooting variance, foul rate, or threshold selection.
- Apply the bottom-pick penalty rule whenever the fourth-ranked item wins.

26. ANALYSIS OUTPUT STRUCTURE
============================================================

Use this exact structure in the final analysis:

1. Event verification
2. Data cutoff
3. Sources checked
4. Toss, pitch, weather, and venue summary
5. Confirmed/probable Playing XIs
6. Availability and team news
7. Rolling Last 5/10/15/20 team stats
8. Batting and bowling phase stats
9. Venue and pitch stats
10. Head-to-head
11. Player matchup checks
12. Market-specific stats
13. Market context
14. Most likely outcome
15. Confidence level: Low / Medium / High
16. Key reasons supporting the pick
17. Key risks that could flip the pick
18. Final answer

27. FINAL DECISION RULES
============================================================



============================================================
ACCURACY UPDATE - PRE-GAME RELIABILITY AND CALIBRATION ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum implements the latest model learnings from recent prediction errors.
It is designed to improve pre-game reliability first, while still allowing live/current-state recalibration when a user supplies a live score or when the market changes close to start time.

CORE CHANGE:
- The model must be math-first, market-aware, and confidence-calibrated.
- Do not treat a lean as a strong pick.
- Do not give Medium or High confidence just because one narrative is persuasive.
- If the market is volatile, data is incomplete, or model components conflict, downgrade confidence or return PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE.
- A prediction should be evaluated by calibration, closing-line value, logic correctness, and outcome, not by win/loss alone.

PREGAME PRIORITY ORDER:
1. Verify event, venue, timing, market line, and availability.
2. Establish market baseline: opening line, current line, consensus line, price/odds, and line movement.
3. Establish sport-specific base projection using season-long and opponent-adjusted efficiency.
4. Adjust for recent form only after opponent quality, venue, and availability are considered.
5. Apply matchup-specific adjustments.
6. Apply weather, venue, travel, rest, and schedule adjustments.
7. Apply market-specific model: full-game, period, player prop, spread/line, total, team total, moneyline.
8. Apply volatility penalty and confidence cap.
9. State the best counterargument before the final pick.
10. Separate most-likely side from value at the current line.

MARKET BASELINE RULE:
- The current market line is not the prediction, but it is the baseline that must be beaten.
- Always compare the user line to the current consensus line if available.
- If the user line is worse than market consensus, downgrade confidence unless the model edge is clear.
- If the market has moved materially and no reason is known, lower confidence.
- If model fair line and market line differ by less than the sport-specific minimum edge threshold, label the edge thin/no clear value.

MINIMUM EDGE THRESHOLDS BEFORE CALLING A PICK STRONG:
- AFL/NRL full-game totals: at least 4-6 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball full-game totals: at least 3-5 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball period totals: at least 1.5-2.5 points from fair line.
- MLB totals: at least 0.4-0.7 runs from fair line, with bullpen and weather agreement.
- Cricket T20 innings totals: at least 5-8 runs from fair line after toss/Playing XI if possible.
- Soccer goals totals: at least 0.15-0.25 expected goals from fair line or price-equivalent edge.
- NHL totals: at least 0.25-0.35 goals from fair line with goalie confirmation.
- NFL totals: at least 2-3 points from fair line with quarterback/inactive/weather confirmation.

CONFIDENCE CAPS:
- If lineups/team lists/Playing XI/inactives/starting goalies are unconfirmed, maximum confidence is Low-Medium unless market is not availability-sensitive.
- If the matchup is high volatility, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If the market has moved sharply against the pick and no clear explanation supports the model, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If only one factor supports the pick, maximum confidence is Low.
- If the model edge is within the minimum threshold, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If key data is missing, state PROVISIONAL PICK or PASS.
- High confidence should be rare and requires confirmed availability, market stability, multiple aligned model components, and low volatility.

VOLATILITY CLASSIFICATION:
Before final answer, classify volatility as LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH.

High-volatility examples:
- Weak teams with bad defences and inconsistent attacks.
- Games with uncertain lineups, late outs, or unclear role allocation.
- Totals where one side can create blowout or garbage-time distortion.
- Player props dependent on a single uncertain role, minutes, usage, or matchup variable.
- MLB full-game totals with strong starters but poor or tired bullpens.
- Cricket totals where toss, dew, pitch, or wickets-in-hand can strongly alter scoring.
- Soccer cards or corners markets without confirmed referee or lineups.

If volatility is HIGH:
- Do not present the pick as reliable.
- Use conservative probability bands.
- Prefer PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE when the model edge is small.

COUNTERARGUMENT REQUIREMENT:
Before the final pick, include:
- The strongest reason the pick could lose.
- Whether that risk is already priced into the market.
- Whether the risk is strong enough to downgrade confidence or pass.

LIVE/CURRENT-STATE RECALIBRATION RULE:
Pregame accuracy is the priority, but if a user provides live/current score, current innings state, current period score, or updated market line:
- Recalculate using current state first.
- For totals, calculate required remaining pace immediately.
- Do not defend the pre-game pick if the live state has invalidated it.
- Label the old pick as STILL VALID / WEAKENED / STALE / BAD POSITION.
- Keep pre-game retrospective separate from live decision-making.

RETROSPECTIVE LEARNING RULE:
Every post-game review must classify the error type:
- Bad input data.
- Correct input but wrong weighting.
- Availability/lineup miss.
- Market line was stale or worse than consensus.
- Bullpen/bench/death-over/late-game volatility miss.
- Period-market model borrowed too much from full-game model.
- Weather/venue condition misread.
- Random variance despite correct process.

Only update the model after identifying which error type occurred.
Do not overreact to one result.
Track calibration by probability bucket and market type.


============================================================
CRICKET ACCURACY UPDATE - TOSS, PHASES, WICKETS, AND INNINGS STRUCTURE
============================================================

CRICKET-SPECIFIC MODEL CHANGES:
- Do not merge powerplay, full-innings, and match-total logic.
- A team can start fast and still finish under if wickets fall or death overs fail.
- A team can start slowly and still finish over if wickets in hand and death batting are strong.
- Toss, Playing XI, pitch, dew, and innings role must be treated as core model inputs, not minor notes.
- If toss or Playing XI is unknown, mark the prediction PROVISIONAL and cap confidence.

CRICKET LIMITED-OVERS WEIGHTING UPDATE:
- 20% pitch, venue par score, boundary size, dew, weather, and DLS risk.
- 20% confirmed Playing XI, batting order, bowling allocation, Impact/Substitute rules where relevant.
- 20% phase profile: powerplay, middle overs, death overs.
- 15% wickets and collapse risk: wickets in hand, balls per wicket, top-order stability, all-out risk.
- 10% matchup splits: pace/spin, left/right, batter-vs-bowler, death bowling matchups.
- 10% market line, movement, and consensus.
- 5% recent form adjusted for venue, opposition, and format.

CRICKET PHASE-SPECIFIC RULE:
- Powerplay lines: prioritise opening pair intent, new-ball bowlers, swing/seam, field restrictions, wicket risk.
- Middle-over lines: prioritise spin matchup, strike rotation, boundary suppression, wickets lost.
- Death-over lines: prioritise wickets in hand, death hitters, death bowling economy, yorker/slower-ball quality.
- Full innings totals: combine all phases but run separate scenario paths for early collapse, par start, and explosive start.

CRICKET CONFIDENCE CAP:
- Pre-toss: maximum Low-Medium.
- Pre-Playing XI: maximum Low-Medium.
- Rain/DLS threat: maximum Low unless market explicitly accounts for shortened match.
- Player wicket/runs props with uncertain role/order/overs: maximum Low.

FINAL DECISION RULES:
- Do not make a pick until toss and Playing XI are checked if available
- Confirm both teams' Playing XI, Impact/Substitute options, likely batting order, and bowling roles from official scorecard/team accounts or trusted live score sources. For questionable or recently injured players, check official team/league updates and verified X/Twitter/beat sources close to start; classify as CONFIRMED IN, CONFIRMED OUT, LIMITED/FITNESS RISK, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN and adjust powerplay, middle-over, death-over, and innings-total projections.
- If the user explicitly says not to look at betting odds, lines, prices, or markets, use a RESEARCH-ONLY mode: do not open odds pages, do not quote odds, do not infer value from price, and rank only by cricket research, format-specific form, toss, Playing XI, injuries/availability, pitch, weather, venue, phase scoring, bowling matchups, and match context
- If the named match and the listed innings team do not match, treat the card as invalid until clarified. Do not silently replace the team name because T20 first-innings and powerplay markets depend on batting order, matchup, and toss. If useful, provide a clearly labelled provisional read only under an explicit assumption, and do not grade/log it as a settled prediction until the team is confirmed.
- For team "1st innings" totals before toss, verify whether the team is actually batting first. If toss is unknown, make the ranking conditional on that team batting first and note that a chase changes the team-total model because target pressure, required rate, and declaration/slowdown incentives differ
- If toss is unavailable, clearly state that the pick is pre-toss/provisional
- If Playing XI is unavailable, clearly state that the pick is based on projected XI
- Do not rely on one stat only
- Prioritise same-format data over all-format data
- Prioritise recent form, but do not ignore long-term team quality
- Prioritise venue/pitch/toss/weather for cricket more heavily than in indoor sports
- For T20/T10/Hundred, prioritise powerplay, middle overs, death overs, wickets in hand, boundary rate, and dot-ball rate
- For T20 first-6-over markets, heavily weight the official pitch report, boundary dimensions, and the actual powerplay wicket-taking/economy profile of the named bowlers. If the pitch report calls the surface a high-scoring batting wicket with short boundaries, do not let poor season batting form alone push a powerplay under above a modest over threshold.
- For T20 innings and phase markets, collect phase splits where available: powerplay runs/wickets, middle overs runs/wickets, death overs runs/wickets, dot-ball rate, boundary rate, wicket timing, and which bowlers cover each phase. Do not use only the final innings score when the market is phase-specific.
- If live score arrives after the pre-match answer, immediately re-rank using current score, wickets, over, required trajectory, and wickets in hand. Live innings state overrides pre-match form assumptions.
- For overlapping thresholds such as Over 50.5 and Under 60.5, explicitly identify the middle band where both can win, then rank by whether the match context is more likely to break above the upper threshold or stay inside that band.
- For T20 first-innings totals at Wankhede or similar high-scoring venues, do not rank an under around 200-205 as the top pick when the pitch report is strongly batting-friendly and the batting XI has multiple power hitters. The pitch/venue ceiling must override poor broad form unless there is clear evidence of difficult conditions or elite bowling matchup control.
- If the live toss/broadcast pitch report says a surface is better for batting than the venue's recent average, upgrade modest innings overs but keep historical venue drag in the model when the same report mentions abrasive soil, big square boundaries, grip, or hard-length assistance. Treat that as a middle-band setup, not an automatic high-scoring game.
- If the batting captain or set top-order batter independently describes the surface as a 180-190 type pitch and the live pitch report also looks better than venue average, do not place a modest powerplay over as the bottom-ranked outcome unless there is clear new-ball swing/seam evidence or a weakened top order.
- If the official/live pitch report is strongly batting-friendly, with a venue first-innings average around 200+, short boundaries, hard/even surface, breeze/carry, and "plenty of runs expected" language, the pitch report overrides generic caution. Do not rank modest powerplay overs or innings overs as least likely unless there is clear adverse weather, elite new-ball matchup, or confirmed batting-order weakness.
- For overlapping team innings and powerplay thresholds, identify the middle band where both picks can win, but do not let that overlap hide recent phase evidence. If the batting side has consecutive recent powerplays above the upper threshold and the pitch report supports batting, rank the modest over above the broad under unless there is a clear new-ball bowling edge or adverse pitch/weather reason.
- Do not overfit one explosive batting-side result to the next match. If the new venue has black-soil/slower characteristics, very large square boundaries, early moisture/seam help, or a key finisher missing, cap the powerplay and innings ceiling unless the specific batting side has its own repeated recent phase evidence on comparable surfaces.
- Same-matchup outliers must still be venue-adjusted. A 500+ aggregate or 70+ powerplay from a flat chase does not automatically transfer to a fresh-wicket first innings if the new venue has early seam/carry, cooler weather, changed bowling personnel, or different innings pressure.
- At altitude or short-boundary venues such as Dharamshala, do not over-anchor to new-ball seam for powerplay unders near 70. Wides/extras, carry, and one opener boundary burst can clear the upper cap while the full innings still lands in the 203-222 middle band.
- At Dharamshala/HPCA or similar altitude venues, separate "good batting pitch" from "automatic extreme ceiling." Current-season 200+ venue data and true bounce support innings overs, but confirmed absences of high-impact powerplay batters or middle-over accelerators can cap the powerplay below 65 and the innings below 215. In that case, model the 195-214 / 55-64 middle bands before ranking either extreme.
- At Dharamshala/HPCA night games where the pitch is called a road but the bowling side has an elite powerplay attack, split the RCB-style innings band from the first-six band. A 202-221 innings can be the most likely final total while the powerplay still sits below 68 because cool air, new-ball swing, Rabada/Siraj-type pace, Rashid/middle-over control, and uncertain opening combinations can slow overs 1-6 without killing the full 20-over score.
- Dharamshala/HPCA correction: if the live pitch report says road/220 par with short boundaries and a fast outfield, do not rank a modest powerplay over last merely because the bowling side has a strong powerplay attack. Confirmed elite batting intent on that surface can beat Rabada/Siraj-type new-ball quality; require live swing/seam evidence or a confirmed weakened top order before making the high powerplay under the top pick.
- T20 knockout/powerplay correction: when a batting-friendly pitch and an in-form explosive opener put 67-70 powerplay runs inside the realistic range, do not make the high powerplay over the bottom pick just because early wickets or respected new-ball bowlers exist. The innings can still land inside the correct 205-224 middle band while the powerplay over wins, and an elite chasing top three can flip the match-winner lean.
- For paired T20 phase thresholds, explicitly set the likely middle band. A 60-69 powerplay or 204-223 innings can make both sides of the user's paired over/under cash; the fourth-ranked pick still has to be the least likely outcome, so do not bury a broad high under simply because the previous game cleared it narrowly.
- When a batting-first side is more stability-first than all-out aggressive and the chasing side chose to bowl for dew, separate good batting conditions from first-innings ceiling. A true-bounce surface can support 185-200 while early seam, hard lengths, slower middle overs, and a weaker middle order still cap 203+.
- At Arun Jaitley/Delhi or similar short-boundary venues, "early movement" is not enough by itself to rank a powerplay under near 70 first. If the same pitch report also says true bounce, short boundaries, clear weather, and high scoring if batters capitalize, opener ceiling and boundary rate must be weighted above generic new-ball caution.
- When an explosive young opener or left-right top-order pairing has a recent high-boundary profile, do not use a single early wicket or a respected new-ball name as the main cap. Check whether the bowling attack has actually controlled powerplays in current form; if not, keep modest powerplay overs above broad high unders.
- At Chepauk/MA Chidambaram or similar dry spin-aiding venues, split the hard-ball phase from the innings ceiling. The powerplay can be the best batting window, so modest PP overs may be live even when innings totals above 205-210 remain upper-tail because the surface slows and grips in overs 7-16.
- If the first 2-3 overs immediately validate the pitch report through 10+ RPO scoring, extras from bowlers struggling with line, or commentary forecasting 65-70+ powerplay runs, live state overrides the pre-match ranking. Mark powerplay unders as BAD POSITION and do not wait for the phase to finish.
- After an explosive start, do not overvalue wickets alone. If a side is above 150 by the 12th over on a flat ground, even losing 4-5 wickets can still leave a 220+ path through anchors, impact batters, extras, and one death-over surge.
- For live high-threshold unders such as Under 239.5, calculate the exact remaining runs required and required run rate. If the required rate is below 10 RPO with 8+ overs left on a flat pitch, the pick may be a lean but should not be described as safe.
- For ODI, prioritise first-innings average, chasing/defending record, wickets in hand, middle-over control, and death-over acceleration
- For Test cricket, prioritise pitch deterioration, weather, session trends, innings-by-innings scoring, and bowling workloads
- For team totals, prioritise batting order, opponent bowling quality, venue par score, pitch, weather, and innings state
- For match totals, prioritise venue scoring, boundary size, pitch, dew, bowling quality, and rain/DLS risk
- For player runs, prioritise batting position, balls faced, role, matchup, venue, and recent form
- For player wickets, prioritise overs bowled, bowling phase role, opponent weakness, venue assistance, and matchup
- Do not overweight H2H if squads, captains, venues, overseas players, or coaches have changed
- Check pre-match and post-match coach/captain/player press conference comments for tactical intent: powerplay aggression, target par score, pitch read, batting-order flexibility, Impact/Substitute usage, bowling matchups, death-over plan, spin/pace preference, and whether the side says it must attack harder or play more conservatively. Weight these comments more when confirmed by toss, XI, pitch report, and recent phase splits; do not use them as standalone proof.
- If the evidence is mixed, say so
- If the data is weak or unavailable, lower confidence
- If key information is missing, state that the prediction is provisional
- Never call a pick "safe" or "guaranteed"
- Mark unavailable information as UNKNOWN

============================================================
28. POST-QUERY RESULTS LOGGING
============================================================

After each completed prediction query, update PREDICTION_RESULTS_LOG.md in the workspace root.

LOGGING RULES:
- Add the query, date, sport, market type, ranked picks, final result when known, win/loss/pending status for each item, and a short retrospective.
- If a match is still live or the result is unavailable, mark the entry PENDING and update it when the result is known.
- Record the main lesson, especially when the miss came from pitch report weighting, toss/innings role, powerplay bowling, live-state changes, weather/dew, lineup/Playing XI, opener/bulk role, or threshold selection.
- Keep the log research-only when the user asked for no odds; do not add odds, prices, or market movement afterward.
- Apply the bottom-pick penalty rule whenever the fourth-ranked item wins.

============================================================
23. ANALYSIS OUTPUT STRUCTURE
============================================================

Use this exact structure in the final analysis:

1. Event verification
2. Data cutoff
3. Sources checked
4. Confirmed/probable team lists, 18th player, and late mail
5. Injuries, suspensions, judiciary, and availability
6. Rolling Last 5/10/15/20 team stats
7. Scoring, first-half, second-half, and game-phase profile
8. Attack, defence, possession, discipline, and kicking profile
9. Tactical and matchup analysis
10. Home/away, venue, weather, referee, and rest context
11. Head-to-head
12. Market-specific stats
13. Market context
14. Most likely outcome
15. Confidence level: Low / Medium / High
16. Key reasons supporting the pick
17. Key risks that could flip the pick
18. Final answer

============================================================
24. FINAL DECISION RULES
============================================================



============================================================
ACCURACY UPDATE - PRE-GAME RELIABILITY AND CALIBRATION ADDENDUM
============================================================

This addendum implements the latest model learnings from recent prediction errors.
It is designed to improve pre-game reliability first, while still allowing live/current-state recalibration when a user supplies a live score or when the market changes close to start time.

CORE CHANGE:
- The model must be math-first, market-aware, and confidence-calibrated.
- Do not treat a lean as a strong pick.
- Do not give Medium or High confidence just because one narrative is persuasive.
- If the market is volatile, data is incomplete, or model components conflict, downgrade confidence or return PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE.
- A prediction should be evaluated by calibration, closing-line value, logic correctness, and outcome, not by win/loss alone.

PREGAME PRIORITY ORDER:
1. Verify event, venue, timing, market line, and availability.
2. Establish market baseline: opening line, current line, consensus line, price/odds, and line movement.
3. Establish sport-specific base projection using season-long and opponent-adjusted efficiency.
4. Adjust for recent form only after opponent quality, venue, and availability are considered.
5. Apply matchup-specific adjustments.
6. Apply weather, venue, travel, rest, and schedule adjustments.
7. Apply market-specific model: full-game, period, player prop, spread/line, total, team total, moneyline.
8. Apply volatility penalty and confidence cap.
9. State the best counterargument before the final pick.
10. Separate most-likely side from value at the current line.

MARKET BASELINE RULE:
- The current market line is not the prediction, but it is the baseline that must be beaten.
- Always compare the user line to the current consensus line if available.
- If the user line is worse than market consensus, downgrade confidence unless the model edge is clear.
- If the market has moved materially and no reason is known, lower confidence.
- If model fair line and market line differ by less than the sport-specific minimum edge threshold, label the edge thin/no clear value.

MINIMUM EDGE THRESHOLDS BEFORE CALLING A PICK STRONG:
- AFL/NRL full-game totals: at least 4-6 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball full-game totals: at least 3-5 points from fair line.
- NBA/NBL/basketball period totals: at least 1.5-2.5 points from fair line.
- MLB totals: at least 0.4-0.7 runs from fair line, with bullpen and weather agreement.
- Cricket T20 innings totals: at least 5-8 runs from fair line after toss/Playing XI if possible.
- Soccer goals totals: at least 0.15-0.25 expected goals from fair line or price-equivalent edge.
- NHL totals: at least 0.25-0.35 goals from fair line with goalie confirmation.
- NFL totals: at least 2-3 points from fair line with quarterback/inactive/weather confirmation.

CONFIDENCE CAPS:
- If lineups/team lists/Playing XI/inactives/starting goalies are unconfirmed, maximum confidence is Low-Medium unless market is not availability-sensitive.
- If the matchup is high volatility, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If the market has moved sharply against the pick and no clear explanation supports the model, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If only one factor supports the pick, maximum confidence is Low.
- If the model edge is within the minimum threshold, maximum confidence is Low-Medium.
- If key data is missing, state PROVISIONAL PICK or PASS.
- High confidence should be rare and requires confirmed availability, market stability, multiple aligned model components, and low volatility.

VOLATILITY CLASSIFICATION:
Before final answer, classify volatility as LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH.

High-volatility examples:
- Weak teams with bad defences and inconsistent attacks.
- Games with uncertain lineups, late outs, or unclear role allocation.
- Totals where one side can create blowout or garbage-time distortion.
- Player props dependent on a single uncertain role, minutes, usage, or matchup variable.
- MLB full-game totals with strong starters but poor or tired bullpens.
- Cricket totals where toss, dew, pitch, or wickets-in-hand can strongly alter scoring.
- Soccer cards or corners markets without confirmed referee or lineups.

If volatility is HIGH:
- Do not present the pick as reliable.
- Use conservative probability bands.
- Prefer PASS / NO CLEAR EDGE when the model edge is small.

COUNTERARGUMENT REQUIREMENT:
Before the final pick, include:
- The strongest reason the pick could lose.
- Whether that risk is already priced into the market.
- Whether the risk is strong enough to downgrade confidence or pass.

LIVE/CURRENT-STATE RECALIBRATION RULE:
Pregame accuracy is the priority, but if a user provides live/current score, current innings state, current period score, or updated market line:
- Recalculate using current state first.
- For totals, calculate required remaining pace immediately.
- Do not defend the pre-game pick if the live state has invalidated it.
- Label the old pick as STILL VALID / WEAKENED / STALE / BAD POSITION.
- Keep pre-game retrospective separate from live decision-making.

RETROSPECTIVE LEARNING RULE:
Every post-game review must classify the error type:
- Bad input data.
- Correct input but wrong weighting.
- Availability/lineup miss.
- Market line was stale or worse than consensus.
- Bullpen/bench/death-over/late-game volatility miss.
- Period-market model borrowed too much from full-game model.
- Weather/venue condition misread.
- Random variance despite correct process.

Only update the model after identifying which error type occurred.
Do not overreact to one result.
Track calibration by probability bucket and market type.


============================================================
NRL ACCURACY UPDATE - TEAM LISTS, SPINE, COMPLETIONS, WEATHER, AND TOTALS
============================================================

NRL-SPECIFIC MODEL CHANGES:
- Final 17, spine combination, middle-forward rotation, edge combinations, and goal-kicker must be checked before confidence increases.
- Do not use raw points without completions, errors, penalties, set restarts, possession, field position, and line-break data.
- Wet/slippery conditions can suppress handling and goal-kicking but may also create defensive errors; classify the direction rather than assuming under.
- For totals, separate attacking quality from defensive leakage and discipline-driven repeat sets.

NRL TOTALS WEIGHTING UPDATE:
- 20% attacking profile: tries, line breaks, tackle breaks, red-zone efficiency, kick tries.
- 20% defensive profile: missed tackles, line breaks conceded, edge weakness, goal-line defence.
- 15% possession/completion/error/penalty/set-restart profile.
- 15% final team list, spine, bench/middle rotation, goal-kicker, late mail.
- 10% weather, venue surface, wind/rain/dew/slippery ball, travel/rest.
- 10% recent form adjusted for opponent quality.
- 10% market line, movement, and consensus.

NRL CONFIDENCE CAP:
- Before final team list/one-hour update: maximum Low-Medium.
- Uncertain halves/hooker/fullback role: maximum Low-Medium for team performance and totals.
- Wet-weather direction unclear: maximum Low-Medium.

FINAL DECISION RULES:
- Do not make a pick until team lists, injuries, suspensions, judiciary status, and late mail are checked if available
- Confirm both final 17s, 18th player, late mail, spine combinations, bench/middle rotation, edge roles, and injury-affected players through official NRL/club sources and verified X/Twitter reporters close to kickoff. Classify key players as CONFIRMED IN, CONFIRMED OUT, LIMITED/MINUTES RISK, PROJECTED, or UNKNOWN and adjust ruck tempo, defensive workload, attacking shape, goal-kicking, and half-specific projections.
- If final 17 is unavailable, clearly state that the pick is pre-team/provisional
- If one-hour team list is unavailable, clearly state that late changes may alter the pick
- If 18th player or HIA risk is relevant, mention it
- Do not rely on one stat only
- Prioritise same-season and same-role data over older data where possible
- Prioritise recent form, but do not ignore long-term team quality
- For totals, prioritise tries, line breaks, completion rate, errors, penalties, set restarts, weather, venue, goal-kicking, and team selection
- For line/spread, prioritise margin profile, team strength, injuries, spine availability, bench/middle rotation, venue/travel, and second-half performance
- For head-to-head, prioritise team strength, spine, middle forward edge, defence, discipline, venue, and weather context
- For anytime try scorers, prioritise starting position, attacking side, edge matchup, spine service, line breaks, and opponent edge weakness
- For run metres, prioritise position, minutes, carries, kick returns, yardage role, and game-state
- For tackles, prioritise position, minutes, defensive workload, opponent possession, and weather/ruck tempo
- For kick metres, prioritise general-play kicking role, field position, weather, and team style
- Do not overweight H2H if squads, coaches, spine combinations, venue, or tactical systems have changed
- Check pre-game and post-game coach/player press conference comments for tactical intent: attacking shape, defensive line speed, ruck tempo, kick pressure, bench/middle rotation, edge targeting, completion-rate focus, and whether the side says it wants to play more expansively or tighten up defensively. Weight comments more when they align with final 17, late mail, injuries, weather, and recent half splits; do not use them alone.
- If the evidence is mixed, say so
- If the data is weak or unavailable, lower confidence
- If key information is missing, state that the prediction is provisional
- Never call a pick "safe" or "guaranteed"
- Mark unavailable information as UNKNOWN



SOCCER / FOOTBALL:

You must obtain the following information through sound and accurate research before analysis.
If a stat is unavailable, mark it UNKNOWN or skip to the next available source.
Do not guess.
Do not use outdated lineup, injury, suspension, weather, referee, tactical, or market data.
Use the most recent available information up to the DATA CUTOFF.
Separate confirmed information from projected information.
Separate league, cup, continental, and friendly data where relevant.
Separate full-time regulation data from extra-time/penalty shootout data where relevant.

2. SOURCE PRIORITY RULES
============================================================

SOURCE PRIORITY:
- Use SPORTS DATA ACQUISITION AND LIVE SOURCE PLAYBOOK 2026-05-23.txt as a source-acquisition reference for official team sheets, late-change timing, weather-window checks, source-origin deduplication, and analysis gates.
- Official league/club source first for fixture, venue, confirmed lineups, injuries, suspensions, squad news, and result (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Official club/league X accounts for confirmed lineups and late team news (if available, if not move onto the next)
- FBref for team/player advanced stats, standard stats, shooting, passing, possession, defensive actions, goalkeeping, and miscellaneous stats (if available, if not move onto the next)
- FotMob for live scores, fixtures, tables, match stats, lineups, form, H2H, player ratings, injuries, and team news (if available, if not move onto the next)
- SofaScore for live stats, lineups, player ratings, momentum, shot maps, form, and H2H (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Understat for xG, xGA, npxG, xG shot maps, and expected points in major European leagues (if available, if not move onto the next)
- StatBunker for referee/cards, competition stats, league tables, team/player data, and cards/corners where available (if available, if not move onto the next)
- FootyStats for xG, BTTS, over/under, cards, corners, referee stats, and league trend data (if available, if not move onto the next)
- WhoScored for formations, player roles, styles, strengths/weaknesses, and tactical notes (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Transfermarkt for injuries, suspensions, squad status, player availability, transfers, and market/squad context (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Trusted news sources and beat reporters for injuries, suspensions, rotation, manager comments, personal news, and tactical changes (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Weather source for outdoor venue conditions (if available, if not move onto the next)
- Odds comparison sites only for market context, not as the main prediction source (if available, if not move onto the next)

RANKING / BOTTOM-PICK GRADING RULE:
- Ranked lists must run from most likely to least likely. The fourth-ranked item must be the single least-likely listed outcome; if it wins, log the normal item result plus one extra bottom-pick penalty loss and explain why it was underrated.
- For soccer totals, BTTS, handicap, corners, and cards, collect half-by-half data where available: first-half/second-half goals, xG, shots, corners, cards, substitutions, red cards, and goal timing. Do not let a late goal or stoppage-time sequence hide which side controlled most of the match.
