2026-06-13 COMPREHENSIVE SPORT DATA MODEL AND DEEP FEATURE UPGRADE - INCORPORATED FROM IMPORTANT UPDATES (SECTIONS 19-24)
============================================================

WHY THIS UPGRADE:
- The June 13 update expands the system from a prediction checklist into a full sports intelligence architecture. It adds complete app/data-model guidance for soccer, cricket, AFL/AFLW/state football, baseball/MLB/international baseball, plus universal pregame leakage controls and a deep-context feature layer.
- This upgrade is ACTIVE. It attaches mainly to Gates 3-7 and Gate 9: source/provenance, availability, conditions, stat coverage, projection-first logic, and extreme-slot testing.
- The condensed operational rules below are the active layer. The full source note from "C:\Users\danie\Downloads\IMPORTANT UPDATES.txt" is appended after this upgrade as an audit-preserved annex so every table, field, citation, and sport-specific learning remains inside this sports doc.

------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 19. UNIVERSAL SPORTS DATA ARCHITECTURE (attaches to Gates 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, and 7)
------------------------------------------------------------

19.1 CORE CHAIN:
Every sport analysis and app design should follow:
verified source -> timestamped raw data -> role/context adjustment -> matchup model -> probability output -> confidence score -> post-match review.

For pregame work, write the stricter chain:
pregame snapshot -> confirmed/projected status -> source confidence -> sport-specific matchup features -> projection band -> probability -> confidence -> settlement review.

19.2 FIVE DATA LAYERS:
Every sport module should separate these layers rather than blending them into a single note:
- Match metadata: competition, season, phase, round, venue, kickoff/start time, timezone, home/away/neutral, ruleset, settlement convention, stakes, rest/travel, weather window, and market/research mode.
- Match sheet and availability: starters/XI/lineups, bench, substitutions/interchange/rotation, active/inactive status, suspensions, illness, injuries, return-to-play, minutes/pitch-count/workload caps, replacement quality, chemistry, and role fit.
- Event log: sport-native raw events with time/phase, team/player IDs, location where relevant, score state, event result, source, correction state, and known-at timestamp.
- Tracking/video layer where available: player/ball positions, speed, distance, acceleration, shape/spacing, pressure, off-ball roles, and video-derived context.
- Derived features: xG/xScore/xwOBA/possession value/EPA or sport equivalent, phase splits, matchup weakness, role stability, volatility, similar-condition form, weather/venue fit, and prediction triggers.

19.3 MINIMUM DATABASE TABLES:
The minimum reusable schema is: competitions, seasons/rulesets, fixtures/matches, teams, players, venues, officials, lineups/team sheets, availability/injuries, events, tracking/video references, weather windows, travel/rest, team-match stats, player-match stats, rolling-window stats, source records, prediction snapshots, market/research context, model outputs, settlement records, and retrospective lessons.

19.4 PROVENANCE AND LEAKAGE FIELDS:
Every feature that can enter a pregame model needs:
source_name; source_url or route; source_status; published_at; fetched_at; known_at; applies_to_time; revision_number; confidence_score; is_projection; is_confirmed; leakage_risk.
If known_at is after the request cutoff, it is forbidden for pregame ranking and can be used only for live updates or settlement.

19.5 SOURCE CONFIDENCE SCORE:
Score every important input from 1-5:
5 = official, fresh, timestamped, unambiguous, directly relevant.
4 = reliable specialist or credentialed reporting, fresh, directly relevant.
3 = aggregator or model page, fresh but needs cross-check.
2 = stale, indirect, partial, or missing timestamp.
1 = snippet/rumour/unverified/social chatter.
Any rank #1 or rank #4 that depends on a score-1 or score-2 fact must be downgraded or rewritten.

19.6 STATMUSE FOR AMERICAN SPORTS: see Canonical Hard Data Rule A and Section 13.10. This former restatement is archived.

------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 20. SPORT-SPECIFIC DATA-MODEL BLUEPRINTS (translation layer for sport annexes)
------------------------------------------------------------

20.1 SOCCER / FOOTBALL:
Core principle: model match context + raw events + player/team state + tactical metrics, not only goals/assists/possession.

Required soccer layers:
- Match metadata: competition, season, round, group/knockout, aggregate/two-leg state, ruleset, VAR, extra time, substitution rules, tactical stakes, table incentive, rotation risk, derby/rivalry, coach/system, travel and schedule congestion.
- Match sheet: starting XI, bench quality, absences, availability, match sharpness, role, formation, chemistry links, tactical changes, emergency positions, youth/debutant status, and replacement coverage.
- Player stats: identity/profile, age/role/footedness/physical profile; availability/health/workload; goals/non-penalty goals/xG/xGOT; shooting type/location; xA/key passes/chance creation; passing/progression/line breaks; carries/dribbles/xT/OBV/VAEP; defensive duels/pressures/blocks/errors; goalkeeping PSxG/sweeping/distribution; tracking load and off-ball value.
- Team stats: form by L5/L10/L15/L20 and season/historical baseline, home/away, opponent tier, game state, attack/xG, shot quality, build-up, progression, set pieces, defence/xGA, shot suppression, PPDA/pressing, transitions, possession identity, squad strength, bench, depth, chemistry, and age profile.
- Context: officials/card/penalty/added-time tendency, venue/pitch size/surface/condition, altitude, weather, time of day, crowd, motivation, manager change, psychological disruption, and source disagreement.
Derived metrics to compute: xG, non-pen xG, xA, xT, possession value, PPDA, field tilt, shot quality, central-box entries, set-piece xG, counterattack xG, compactness, defensive-line height, pressure success, and role stability.
Build priority: MVP = match metadata, team sheets, event log, player/team match stats, xG/shots/chances, availability, venue/weather. Pro = xT/possession value, set-piece detail, role chemistry, referee tendencies, tracking summaries. Elite = video/tracking integration, off-ball runs, shape/spacing, automated tactical mismatch detection.

20.2 CRICKET:
Core principle: model format-specific context + ball-by-ball data + player role profiles + pitch/weather/toss conditions + matchup modelling.

Required cricket layers:
- Match metadata: format, innings length, DLS/rain rules, reserve day, Super Over/tiebreak, venue, boundary dimensions, pitch, ball type, toss, batting/bowling first, time of day, dew, officials, table/NRR incentive, and playing conditions.
- Ball-by-ball table: over/ball, innings, batter, bowler, non-striker, runs, extras, wicket, shot/control where available, line/length where available, fielding event, phase, ball age, required rate, wickets in hand, and score state.
- Format model: Tests, ODIs, T20s, domestic/franchise, and reduced-overs games must have different priors for tempo, wickets, risk, draw/no-result, batting depth, and phase value.
- Player stats: batter role/position, strike rate, average, boundary%, dot-ball%, spin/pace/handedness split, powerplay/middle/death split; bowler economy/strike rate/dot-ball%, phase role, wickets by phase, matchup style, recent workload; wicketkeeper catches/stumpings/DRS influence; fielding/catch quality and ground coverage.
- Team stats: batting phase rates, chase/set split, wickets lost by phase, bowling phase economy/wickets, death bowling, fielding/discipline, extras, and over-rate penalty risk.
- Conditions: actual pitch report, captain/toss comments, live surface behaviour, weather/dew every 5 overs in white-ball cricket or by session in Tests, ball type, boundary dimensions, altitude, wind, humidity, and interruption/DLS risk.
Derived metrics: expected runs by phase, win probability, batter-vs-bowler matchup score, dot-ball pressure, boundary pressure, death-overs ceiling, collapse risk, dew effect, pitch deterioration, DLS sensitivity, and replacement/impact-player value.
Build priority: MVP = fixture/rules, toss/XI, ball-by-ball, phase stats, venue/pitch/weather, player roles. Pro = matchup engine, dew/DLS module, boundary/ball-type data, fielding value. Elite = tracking/video, shot maps, release/trajectory, automated live surface inference.

20.3 AFL / AFLW / AUSTRALIAN RULES:
Core principle: model territory + contest + turnover + stoppage + scoring-chain quality, not possession volume alone.

Required AFL layers:
- Match metadata: AFL/AFLW/state/local variant, 2026 rule version, finals/preseason, venue/ground dimensions, weather/wind, bounce time, travel, break length, ladder incentive, rivalry, team selection timing, interchange/substitute/late-change rules.
- Event/player data: disposals, kicks/handballs, contested/uncontested possessions, clearances, centre/stoppage splits, hitouts to advantage, inside 50s, rebound 50s, intercepts, tackles/pressure, marks/contested marks/marks inside 50, scoring shots, goals/behinds, score involvements, turnovers, clangers, one-percenters, GPS/load where available.
- Team stats: scoring and attack, expected score, inside-50 quality, conversion, defence, intercept/transition, contest/stoppage, ball movement, pressure/turnover game, ruck source, time in forward half, venue/home/away/interstate split, and late-game running profile.
- Availability/role: key forward, key defender, midfield, ruck, small forward pressure, substitute/interchange usage, managed players, debutants, role changes, replacement quality, and chemistry.
Derived metrics: expected score, scoring-chain quality, inside-50 efficiency, turnover score differential, clearance source, pressure index, territory share, conversion regression, venue-size fit, weather style fit, and late-game fatigue risk.
Build priority: MVP = selection, contest/clearance, inside-50s, scoring shots/xScore, venue/weather. Pro = pressure, turnover source, role changes, interchange and 6-day-break adjustments. Elite = GPS/load, player tracking, shape/spacing, AFLW/state rule variants fully versioned.

20.4 BASEBALL / MLB / INTERNATIONAL BASEBALL:
Core principle: use a ruleset-driven baseball model with one core database and separate config layers for MLB, NPB/KBO/CPBL/ABL, and international tournaments.

Required baseball layers:
- Match metadata: league/ruleset, season phase, park, roof/weather, umpire crew, lineups, handedness, probable/confirmed starters, bullpen availability, series game number, travel/getaway day, pitch timer/ABS/shift/base rules, extra-inning rule, suspended-game handling, and settlement convention.
- Pitch-by-pitch table: inning, count, base/out state, pitcher/batter/catcher, pitch type, velocity, movement, spin, location, swing/take/contact, batted-ball event, exit velocity, launch angle, xwOBA/xBA/xSLG where available, run expectancy, win probability, and umpire call.
- Player stats: hitter traditional and advanced stats, platoon splits, chase/contact/whiff, hard-hit/barrel, ground/fly profile, baserunning/sprint, pitcher ERA/FIP/xFIP/SIERA, K-BB, pitch mix/velocity/command, times-through-order penalty, health/readiness, catcher framing/pop time/throwing, fielding OAA/DRS/errors.
- Team stats: offence, pitching, bullpen leverage/rest, defence, baserunning, bench/platoon bats, lineup quality, closer availability, and manager hook/bullpen profile.
- MLB layer: StatMuse first for statistical windows where available; Baseball Savant/Statcast for batted-ball, pitch-tracking, fielding, baserunning, catcher, and park-factor depth; MLB Stats API/Gameday for official live state.
- International layer: WBSC/tournament rules, roster source league, MLB club restrictions, league-strength translation, pitch limits and mandatory rest, bullpen depth, mercy/tiebreak rules, neutral venue uncertainty, short-sample volatility, and unfamiliar hitter-pitcher matchups.
Derived metrics: starter projection, lineup handedness advantage, bullpen fatigue score, park/weather run adjustment, umpire zone adjustment, times-through-order risk, run-expectancy swing, defensive run value, baserunning value, and tournament pitching constraint score.
Build priority: MVP = lineups, starters, bullpen rest, park/weather, team/player windows, official game state. Pro = Statcast quality, umpire zone, catcher/defence, platoon and TTO. Elite = pitch-level simulations, ABS/challenge context, tournament translation, and video/tracking integration.

20.5 AMERICAN LEAGUE ANNEXES:
NBA/WNBA, NFL/NCAA, NHL, MLB, MLS/FC, college basketball/football, PGA, and any other U.S./North American league covered by StatMuse inherit Section 19.6. StatMuse is the first statistical stop; official sources control official state. Basketball, football, hockey, and golf annexes must still collect sport-native advanced metrics from official/deeper sources when StatMuse does not carry the required split.

------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 21. PREGAME-ONLY SNAPSHOT AND LEAKAGE CONTROL (attaches to Gates 2, 3, 6, 7, and settlement review)
------------------------------------------------------------

21.1 ONLY KNOWABLE BEFORE START:
Pregame predictions may use only information available at or before the request/cutoff time. Later lineups, live scores, final box scores, injury confirmations, post-match stat corrections, and historical weather reanalysis are forbidden for pregame ranking.

21.2 REQUIRED SNAPSHOT FIELDS:
Each prediction snapshot must store: request_time_AEST; venue_local_time; fixture_start_time; source_known_at; source_published_at; fetched_at; feature_version; projected_or_confirmed; confidence_score; missing_critical_fields; and leakage_risk.

21.3 FORBIDDEN PREGAME INPUTS:
Do not use final score, final player minutes, final XI if it was not known yet, postgame injuries, postgame weather observations in place of forecast, corrected advanced stats published after the cutoff, or market moves that occurred after the request cutoff.

21.4 WEATHER DISTINCTION:
Pregame weather uses forecast/nowcast for the venue-local match window. Historical/reanalysis weather is for backtesting only. If the game is near start or live, use live/nowcast and label it as live evidence.

21.5 CONFIDENCE EFFECT:
If the prediction-driving stat, lineup, pitch/surface, weather, officials, or ruleset field is missing or only projected, cap confidence and keep dependent outcomes out of extreme slots unless the unresolved state cannot plausibly change the ranking.

------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 22. DEEP CONTEXT FEATURE LAYER (mandatory hidden-factor pass before Gate 9)
------------------------------------------------------------

22.1 SIMILAR-CONDITION FORM:
The strongest deep feature is: how has this team/player performed in the last 5/10/20 games under conditions most similar to today's match?
Similarity filters include opponent strength/style, venue/home-away/neutral, weather, altitude, rest/travel, competition phase, ruleset, role, lineup continuity, and game state. If similar-condition data is unavailable, write UNKNOWN rather than using generic form as if it matched.

22.2 ROLE AND REPLACEMENT:
Recent form is not meaningful if role changed. Track role stability score, position change flag, usage change flag, role difficulty change, and replacement role fit. Injuries must be converted into net replacement drop-off:
Absence Impact = Missing Player Role Value - Replacement Role Value.
Store missing player value, replacement value, net drop-off, position scarcity, replacement match fitness, and natural/out-of-position fit.

22.3 LINEUP CONTINUITY AND CHEMISTRY:
Track same starting unit, same defensive unit, same midfield/ruck/spine/batting order/catcher-pitcher pair, same set-piece takers, same bench rotation, and same tactical system. Chemistry changes can invalidate last-N windows.

22.4 REST, TRAVEL, SLEEP, AND BODY CLOCK:
Track days rest, short turnaround, back-to-back/3-in-4/5-in-7, travel distance, time-zone change, body-clock kickoff, red-eye travel, day game after night game, sleep disruption, altitude, heat, and late-game fatigue exposure.

22.5 CROWD AND HOME-GROUND INTENSITY:
Track attendance/crowd density, hostility, derby/rivalry, finals atmosphere, travel support, neutral-site effects, crowd absence, and likely official/referee influence. Treat crowd as context unless supported by sport/venue history.

22.6 OFFICIALS PRESSURE PROFILE:
Beyond assignment names, track card/foul/free-kick/penalty/strike-zone/six-again/power-play tendencies, home-team call tendency, late-game strictness, review/VAR/DRS/bunker profile, and volatility. Officials are environment modifiers, not side anchors, unless a market is directly official-sensitive.

22.7 WEATHER INTERACTION AND WEATHER STYLE FIT:
Do not just record weather. Map weather to team/player style:
- rain/wet surface: ball security, marking, kicking, fielding, spin grip, pressure sides.
- wind: AFL goalkicking, baseball HR carry, soccer crosses, cricket swing.
- heat/humidity/WBGT: fatigue, pressing/running, substitution/interchange, injury risk.
- dew: cricket chase and death bowling control.
- altitude/air pressure: endurance and ball carry.
Create Weather Style Fit Score.

22.8 VENUE FAMILIARITY AND VENUE STYLE FIT:
Track venue dimensions, surface, roof, altitude, sightlines, crowd, travel, ground/park/pitch quirks, historical scoring/cards/corners/runs, and team/player familiarity. Create:
Venue Style Fit Score = team style strength x venue condition suitability.

22.9 MATCHUP-SPECIFIC WEAK POINTS:
Output primary mismatch, secondary mismatch, mismatch confidence, and affected players/units. Examples: weak left back vs elite winger; batter weak vs left-arm orthodox; AFL key defender mismatch; baseball lineup poor vs sliders/sweepers; starter weak third time through order.

22.10 TACTICAL SURPRISE PROBABILITY:
Estimate whether a team is likely to change structure because of new coach, losing streak, opponent-specific setup, finals/knockout, returning star, coach comments, training reports, or role trials. This prevents blindly projecting the last 5 games forward.

22.11 TRAINING, SOFT-TISSUE, AND PLAYED-INJURED FLAGS:
When reliable reports exist, structure training availability: full, modified, no contact, left early, reserves, different role, absent from captain's run. Track return-from-injury flag, days since return, games/minutes/overs/pitches since return, injury type, recurrence category, minutes cap, managed role, reduced training load, early substitution/removal, and output drop since injury.

22.12 MOTIVATION AND INCENTIVE PRECISION:
Replace vague "must win" language with exact incentive: must win, draw enough, dead rubber, margin/percentage/goal difference/NRR needed, rivalry/milestone, finals/resting risk, relegation/survival/title pressure, draft/tanking context where applicable. Link incentive to likely tempo, risk-taking, rotation, and late-game aggression.

22.13 MARKET, PUBLIC, AND NEWS CONTEXT:
Market movement is optional and is an information-detection tool, not the model. Track opening/current line, direction, timing, disagreement with model, and volatility only when market data is allowed. Track public overreaction triggers: huge last-game win, ugly loss, star hype, rivalry narrative, coach pressure, viral clip/injury scare. Store narrative strength, confirmed evidence, and likely overreaction.

22.14 STAT STABILITY, VOLATILITY, AND FLOOR/CEILING:
Every stat has a stability profile. Stable-sooner examples include strikeout rate, xG/xScore trend, inside-50 differential, dot-ball rate, pressure rate, and pitch velocity. Noisier examples include batting average, raw goals/accuracy, goalkicking conversion, wickets, limited-chance goals, and ERA.
Track median output, standard deviation, coefficient of variation, floor, ceiling, blowout-adjusted average, and consistency index. Average form alone is not enough.

22.15 GARBAGE TIME AND SCORE EFFECTS:
Discount stats from dead periods: late soccer shots while 3-0 down, impossible cricket chases/dead rubber overs, AFL junk-time disposals/goals, baseball position-player pitching or low-leverage bullpen innings, basketball/NFL/NHL blowout bench minutes. Store competitive_minutes_only, high_leverage_only, blowout_adjusted_stat, and garbage_time_percentage.

22.16 PRESSURE AND CLUTCH:
Track pressure situations without overrating them: soccer tied/down-one chances, cricket death overs/high required-rate chases, AFL fourth quarter/margin under 12, baseball high-leverage PA/RISP, basketball clutch/close-score possessions, NHL late/one-goal state. Treat as descriptive unless sample is large.

22.17 OPPONENT FATIGUE EXPLOITATION:
Do not only ask whether your team is fatigued. Ask whether your style can exploit the opponent's fatigue: fast wingers vs tired fullbacks, AFL pressure vs short-break opponent, death hitters vs tired death bowlers, patient lineup vs tired bullpen, NBA pace/drive pressure vs tired legs.

22.18 BENCH, SUBSTITUTION, AND COACH PROFILE:
Track starting strength, bench strength, late-game strength, replacement flexibility, substitution/interchange/bullpen/line-change timing, conservative/aggressive game-state behaviour, rotation tendency, tactical flexibility, set-piece emphasis, ruck/midfield mix, batting order flexibility, and bullpen hook.

22.19 PSYCHOLOGICAL DISRUPTION:
Use only confirmed reliable sources for coach sacking/resignation, captain absence, contract dispute, travel chaos, illness outbreak, public controversy, bereavement/family emergency, or leadership disruption. These are context and confidence modifiers, not standalone anchors.

22.20 DATA-SOURCE DISAGREEMENT:
If sources conflict, store it. Required fields: source A claim, source B claim, conflict level, confidence effect, prediction impact, and what source would resolve it. Source disagreement reduces confidence until resolved.

22.21 WHAT WOULD CHANGE THE PREDICTION:
Every serious pregame card must include trigger conditions: star late out, weather worsens, roof closes/opens, toss decision, starting pitcher scratched, goalie changed, ruck late out, official/referee changed, lineup role surprise, surface report contradicts expectation, market/news shock, or live phase pace breaks projection. This makes the output practical and auditable.

22.22 MASTER DEEP-LAYER CHECKLIST:
Very high importance: timing/leakage, similar-condition form, replacement quality, role stability, data-source confidence, modelling volatility/regression.
High importance: venue-style fit, weather-style fit, travel/body clock, venue familiarity, availability/bench, stat stability.
Medium-high importance: crowd, officials, lineup continuity, chemistry, tactical surprise, matchup-specific weak points.
Medium importance: coach profile, market movement where allowed, public overreaction, motivation/incentive precision.
Low-medium but nonzero: psychological disruption, training clues, soft-tissue recurrence, played-injured flags when reliable.

22.23 FINAL DEEP-FEATURE FORMULA:
Normal pregame stats
+ rolling 5/10/15/20 trends
+ season/current-year/historical averages
+ opponent adjustment
+ similar-condition form
+ lineup/replacement/role adjustment
+ venue/weather/style fit
+ rest/travel/body-clock adjustment
+ source confidence and disagreement
+ leakage protection
= high-quality pregame prediction feature.

------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 23. REQUIRED CARD OUTPUT ADDITIONS (attaches to the results-log template)
------------------------------------------------------------

Every serious card must add or preserve these lines:
- SOURCE CONFIDENCE / DISAGREEMENT: decisive sources, confidence scores, conflicts, and confidence cap.
- SIMILAR-CONDITION FORM: best matching L5/L10/L20 sample or UNKNOWN, with filters used.
- ROLE / REPLACEMENT / BENCH: role changes, net replacement drop-off, bench/late-game strength, and unresolved availability scenarios.
- STYLE FIT: weather style fit, venue style fit, and opponent-fatigue exploitation path.
- VOLATILITY / STAT STABILITY: stable vs noisy stats, floor/ceiling, garbage-time adjustment, and regression flags.
- MOTIVATION / NEWS / MARKET CONTEXT: exact incentive plus market/public/news signal if allowed and sourced.
- PREDICTION TRIGGERS: what would change the ranking before start or live.

If any of these lines is not relevant, write NOT RELEVANT. If a relevant line cannot be sourced, write UNKNOWN and cap confidence when it matters.

------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 24. IMPLEMENTATION PRIORITIES AND DASHBOARDS
------------------------------------------------------------

24.1 MVP BUILD:
Build official fixture/ruleset state, source/provenance, lineups/availability, core event logs, team/player rolling windows, venue/weather, officials, basic derived stats, projection output, and settlement review. For American sports, wire StatMuse as the first statistical lookup.

24.2 PRO BUILD:
Add role/replacement modelling, similar-condition samples, style-weather/venue fit, matchup engine, phase-specific projections, bench/late-game strength, official/referee tendencies, market/news context where allowed, and calibration dashboards.

24.3 ELITE BUILD:
Add tracking/video/computer-vision, possession-value models, off-ball and shape metrics, live recalibration, simulation/forecast ensembles, source-disagreement automation, leakage audits, and sport-specific advanced data integrations.

24.4 CORE DASHBOARDS:
Use these screens across sports: match overview, source/provenance panel, lineups/availability, team form and deltas, player role profiles, venue/weather/officials, matchup board, deep-context signals, projection/probability output, and retrospective learning/calibration.

PRECEDENCE: Sections 19-24 sit directly under Sections 13-18 and extend the Universal Core. They do not relax Gates 0-10, ruleset lock, source freshness, StatMuse-first enforcement for American sports, or extreme-slot calibration. Where older sport-specific notes conflict, use the stricter/current rule and state the conflict in the card.

2026-06-13 SOCCER WORLD CUP CO-HOST OPENER SUPPORT NOTE:
- Canada 1-1 Bosnia-Herzegovina validated ranking full Over 1.5 and 1H Over 0.5 above their unders while keeping the winner lean low-confidence. Bosnia scored from a first-half/set-piece route and Canada equalised late through bench pressure. This reinforces the existing soccer group-opener rule: separate goal-market likelihood from side/winner confidence. A co-host/home-pressure team can produce enough chances and late pressure for the over while still failing to convert territorial dominance into a win.
- Scotland 0-1 Morocco, added 2026-06-20: when one side has the clearer technical profile but the match context still carries draw/winner volatility, express the edge through non-loss and opponent team-total ceilings before exact score or exact winner markets. Morocco/draw and Scotland team total Under 2.5 both won, while the low-likelihood Scotland win and Scotland clean-sheet fades correctly lost after an early Morocco goal.

2026-06-14 CRICKET NON-COMPLEMENTARY TOTAL-BAND SUPPORT NOTE:
- Akcel United Brussels vs JB Bruges exposed a different cricket trap from the usual same-line over/under pair. When the listed full-innings options are not complements, such as Over 174.5 and Under 74.5, rank them from the projected band and market settlement rules, not as a forced 50/50 pair. In a normal 20-over first innings, Under 74.5 is a collapse-only or settlement-quirk tail if the batting side has credible T20 depth; it can be a structural bottom even while Over 174.5 is only a modest edge. Still rerate immediately if overs are reduced, because a shortened innings changes the whole distribution.
- Women's T20 powerplay/full-innings split, added 2026-06-14 from Australia Women-South Africa Women: a modest powerplay over can win while a high full-innings over loses. Australia were 52/2 after six but finished 172/8, so first-6 Over 48.5 and full Under 177.5 both won. Treat powerplay intent/top-order counterattack and full-innings wicket/death-over control as separate axes; do not stack all overs or all unders together merely because the first six overs are fast.
- Women's T20 elite-batting ceiling correction, added 2026-06-14 from England Women-Sri Lanka Women: do not bury a full-innings over when an elite batting side is confirmed batting first, the powerplay starts hot, and the top order is intact. England reached 51/0 after six and 219/1 after 20, so the first-6 over and innings over both won while the top-ranked innings under failed. Surface/weather caution and opponent bowling form must not override live batting dominance once the first-six and wickets-in-hand evidence points to a 190+ ceiling.
- Women's T20 high-line/ceiling-player correction, added 2026-06-20 from New Zealand Women 140/6 vs Ireland Women at Southampton: when the innings line is 170+ at a venue already producing 150-162 scores for that same batting side, and a major ceiling player is out, the full-innings under can be the clean anchor even against a weaker opponent. New Zealand's 31-run powerplay and 10/3 start confirmed the early-wicket path. Keep powerplay over/under adjacent unless same-day XI or surface evidence creates a true structural phase edge.


============================================================

2026-06-23 GENERAL RULE UPDATE - STARTER/OPENING-PHASE QUALITY DOES NOT CONTROL THE FULL-GAME TAIL:
- Across sports, do not project a full-game under solely from strong starters or a slow opening-phase expectation. Explicitly test the later-phase failure path: relief units, bench quality, late fouling, overtime/extra innings, fatigue and score-state aggression. The lower the full-game threshold, the more heavily that tail must be weighted.
- When a full-game total is unusually low, write two separate projections before ranking: regulation/standard-duration scoring and the overtime/extra-inning tail. A close-game script may support the under early while increasing the chance that additional periods destroy it.

2026-06-23 BASEBALL / NPB RULE UPDATE - LOW TOTALS, BULLPENS AND EXTRA INNINGS:
- Rakuten's 8-7, 12-inning win over Seibu on June 22 invalidated the Lions ML and Under 5.5 anchors despite Kaima Taira and Takahisa Hayakawa entering with elite run-prevention profiles. The score was already 6-6 through nine, and the 11th/12th innings added three more runs.
- For NPB totals of 5.5 or lower, starter ERA/HQS evidence is necessary but not sufficient. Collect both bullpens' latest workload and leverage availability, then model the tied-after-nine probability under NPB's extra-inning/tie rules. Do not rank the under above a side/cushion unless both the starter phase and late-relief phase support it.
- A dome removes weather variance; it does not remove bullpen, home-run, defensive-error or extra-inning variance.

2026-06-23 BASKETBALL / ISRAEL WINNER LEAGUE PREGAME APPLICATION - GALIL ELYON VS NETANYA:
- In a lower-house season finale, separate motivation from lineup quality. A safe home team and a relegated visitor may play with freedom, but confirmed game-roster omissions still control the scoring ceiling. If a relegated team's leading scorer is absent from the official game roster, downgrade its side and full-game over before applying a generic dead-rubber pace boost.
- Direct matchup scoring remains useful when it is current and lineup-compatible: Galil won the three prior 2025-26 league meetings with totals of 163, 153 and 170. Use those games alongside current L5 phase splits; do not use H2H alone.
- For exact-complement basketball cards, rank the projected number first. Here the pregame projection was roughly 162-165 points and 80-83 first-half points, making Under 173.5 the structural anchor and 1H Under 87.5 a smaller edge. Keep the opposing overs explicitly labelled as forced complements rather than independent positive picks.

2026-06-23 GENERAL RULE UPDATE - DUPLICATED USER OPTIONS:
- If the same market and threshold are listed twice, treat them as one distinct outcome. Do not double-count the evidence, manufacture an unstated complement, or imply that two identical entries have independent probabilities.
- If the user also requires bottom-two slots, the duplicated entries may occupy both displayed bottom slots only when that shared outcome is genuinely the least likely. State explicitly that they are the same pick and that the card contains fewer distinct outcomes than numbered entries.

2026-06-23 BASKETBALL / ACB FINALS GAME 3 APPLICATION - BARCELONA VS VALENCIA:
- For a same-series Game 3, immediate Q1 and halftime evidence outranks season scoring averages when the threshold is phase-specific. Barcelona-Valencia produced 38 and 37 first-quarter points in Finals Games 1-2, and their May 31 Palau meeting produced 42. Three straight direct Q1 results below 43.5 make Q1 Over 43.5 a true fade even though both teams own elite full-game scoring ceilings.
- Do not infer a fast first quarter from a high full-game total. Game 1 reached 202 in regulation and 225 after overtime despite a 38-point Q1; late regulation shot-making and overtime were separate scoring mechanisms.
- Run the repeatability test after a blowout: Valencia's Game 2 102-75 win included 52.4% three-point shooting versus Barcelona's 19.1%, so some shooting regression is likely. However, Valencia's strong opening and Barcelona's slow start repeated across both Finals games and the latest Palau H2H. Regress the shooting percentage without erasing the repeatable early control pattern.
- Player return does not equal normal availability. Josep Puerto's four-minute Game 2 return after nearly six weeks out should be treated as a limited-depth addition, while Xabi Lopez-Arostegui and Nate Reuvers remain confirmed absences.

2026-06-23 GENERAL RULE UPDATE - TIMEZONE VERIFICATION FOR THINLY COVERED EVENTS:
- Never classify a minor or new-competition fixture as live from a local-time conversion alone. Confirm the published UTC kickoff against the official broadcaster/event page. Atletico Grau-Pirata FC was listed at 20:15 UTC, which is 06:15 AEST on June 23; an initial one-hour conversion error would have incorrectly triggered live-score modelling.
- When aggregators disagree, the official scheduled broadcast state controls whether the card is pregame or live. Record both the source time and converted user-zone time.

2026-06-23 BASKETBALL / ACB FINALS SETTLEMENT - BARCELONA 80, VALENCIA 88:
- Immediate same-series Q1 evidence repeated again: the first quarter total was 32 after the prior direct sequence of 38, 37 and 42. Keep direct phase evidence above season scoring averages for Q1 markets until a lineup or tactical change explains why the pattern should break.
- A low total does not require poor overall shooting. Barcelona made 50% from the field, but Valencia's 42-26 rebounding edge, controlled lead and both teams' modest three-point rates kept the game to 168. Model possession control, offensive-rebound ownership and clock state separately from raw field-goal percentage.
- The Valencia winner call was supported by the same matchup mechanisms that drove the under: guard creation led by Jean Montero and a decisive rebounding advantage. Side and under can correlate when the stronger matchup team controls second chances and game state without raising pace.

2026-06-23 SOCCER / PERU COPA DE LA LIGA APPLICATION - ATLETICO GRAU VS PIRATA FC:
- In a new regional cup with thin injury and lineup coverage, cap confidence and use competition-level adjustment explicitly. Pirata's strong Liga 2 scoring form is relevant, but it must be discounted when stepping up against a Liga 1 defence; Grau's poor Liga 1 attack remains relevant even against lower-tier opposition.
- Hot, dry afternoon conditions can suppress sustained pressing and favour a lower total, but temperature forecasts that disagree materially must be logged as a range rather than a precise input. Weather is a modifier, not the anchor.
- When the first-tier favourite has scored one or fewer in most recent matches and the lower-tier opponent arrives in strong form, prefer a narrow winner projection and total under over a confident handicap or blowout script.

2026-06-23 GENERAL RULE CORRECTION - THINLY COVERED CUP TOTALS:
- Atletico Grau 3-2 Pirata FC exposed the danger of using division gap and weather to stack 1H/full unders when lineups are unconfirmed. Grau scored in the 8th and 22nd minutes; Pirata then scored twice after 82 minutes.
- In new or regional cups, widen both tails for rotation, unfamiliar defensive partnerships, uneven motivation and late consolation scoring. If the lower-tier side has scored repeatedly in current form, discount its scoring rate for opponent strength but do not erase it.
- Without confirmed defensive lineups or a competition-specific low-tempo sample, no card should place both 1H Under 0.5 and full Under 2.5 in the top two solely from the favourite's league-season under profile.

2026-06-23 BASEBALL / MLB COORS FIELD APPLICATION - BOSTON AT COLORADO:
- Separate winner probability from run-line cushion probability. A road team can be the likely winner while the home +1.5 remains the safest listed side because Coors inflates comeback and one-run-game paths, especially against a contact-heavy rookie starter.
- At Coors totals of 11.5 or higher, do not make the over automatic from heat and altitude. Write the projected score first and require multiple independent 12+ mechanisms: vulnerable starters, taxed bullpens, full-strength lineups, meaningful wind out and weak defence. A light 3 mph breeze out is a modifier, not a primary over trigger.
- Starter surface stats must be paired with role/recovery context. Ryan Feltner's 5.05 ERA overstates his four-start post-IL improvement, while his recent workload and walks still create risk. Jake Bennett's 4.79 ERA understates Coors volatility because his low strikeout total leaves more balls in play.
- Mandatory defence check application: Boston's .987 fielding/35 errors versus Colorado's .983/47 errors strengthens Boston's winner path and Colorado multi-run-inning risk. Defensive quality should influence side and total independently.

2026-06-23 STATMUSE DIRECTIVE - CONSOLIDATED:
- Generic requirements moved to Canonical Hard Data Rules A-B and Section 13.10. The process correction below remains active because it adds a specific failure remedy.
- 2026-06-28 KC-CHW PROCESS CORRECTION: never answer an American-sports card from official API + ESPN only and then add StatMuse/rolling windows after the user asks. The pre-answer gate must include StatMuse status plus L5/L10/L15/L20 versus season and historical baseline for both teams, relevant home/away or venue split, H2H recency with latest completed date validated, and decision-relevant players/starters where the market depends on them. If a broad query silently omits the newest completed game or returns spring/preseason rows, reject it, rerun with regular-season and date filters, and mark the first pull invalid in the log. Any card sent before this gate is complete must be labelled PROCESS VIOLATION and corrected with an audit addendum.

2026-06-23 GENERAL RULE APPLICATION - STATMUSE OPPONENT-PHASE MISPARSE:
- A StatMuse query for "opponent first-quarter points" or "opponent first-half points" can silently return the requested team's own scoring. Reject the result whenever the answer sentence repeats the team's own phase average or otherwise fails to name opponent points allowed.
- After rejection, calculate opponent Q1/1H scoring from official or reliable dated gamebooks. Record the newest included game date and keep the valid StatMuse own-team L5/L10/L15/L20 data rather than discarding the entire source.

2026-06-23 CROSS-SPORT ROLLING DIRECTIVE - CONSOLIDATED:
- Generic rolling-window requirements moved to Canonical Hard Data Rule B and Section 13.10. The press-signal and baseline-comparison details below remain active.
- 2026-06-27 USER DIRECTIVE - EVERY-SPORT PRESS-CONFERENCE / TEAM-REP PLAYSTYLE GATE: before ranking any serious card, search for current pre-match coach, captain, player, selector, manager, or official team-representative comments from official club/league sites, press-conference videos/transcripts, accredited media, and official social/video mirrors. Extract whether the comments imply a more attacking, defensive, conservative, aggressive, high-tempo, low-tempo, selection-experiment, youth-development, rest-management, chase/defend, press/transition, stoppage/territory, set-piece, bullpen/rotation, or phase-specific approach compared with previous games. Classify each comment as TACTICAL SIGNAL / AVAILABILITY SIGNAL / MOTIVATION SIGNAL / PR-NOISE / UNKNOWN, name the speaker and source, and state whether it supports or conflicts with the rolling-stat form anchor. If no current comments are found, write PRESS SIGNAL: UNKNOWN and cap confidence on totals, spreads, and game-script picks that depend on style. Post-match coach/player comments must be checked in retrospectives to explain whether the pregame style read was right, wrong, hidden, or contradicted by execution. Do not let L5/L10 trends carry a #1 total or spread unless either the broader L15/L20/season baselines agree or a current press/role/selection/tactical mechanism explains the short-window change.
- Never present L5/L10/L15/L20 in isolation. Compare each decision-driving rolling statistic with:
  (a) current season or current competition average;
  (b) current calendar-year average when materially different from the competition season;
  (c) historical team baseline at the same level, venue or role when available;
  (d) player career average and relevant current-role/season average when evaluating players.
- Print or log the comparison as: STAT | L5 | L10 | L15 | L20 | SEASON | YEAR | CAREER/HISTORICAL | d5 | d10 | FORM VERDICT.
- Calculate d5 and d10 against the most relevant baseline. State whether the evidence indicates acceleration, decline, stable form, regression risk, role change, opponent/schedule distortion or insufficient sample.
- Use participation-aware denominators. Player per-game trends must be checked against minutes, starts, innings, plate appearances, overs, snaps, possessions or other sport-relevant opportunity. Do not label a player "in form" from per-game output if the change is mainly caused by playing-time growth unless that role growth is itself expected to continue.
- Match the baseline to the market. Examples: quarter/half scoring against season quarter/half scoring; pitcher recent ERA/WHIP/K-BB against season and career starter/relief baselines; football goals/xG against season and historical level; cricket powerplay/death rates against season/year/career phase rates.
- If season, year or career/historical comparison is unavailable, mark it `MISSING` rather than silently substituting a different scope. Team-level season/year data should normally be obtainable and its absence must reduce confidence.
- The final ranking must identify which window is the FORM ANCHOR and why it deserves more weight than the other windows and baselines. Short windows cannot override the season/career baseline without a concrete mechanism such as availability, role, tactical, venue, schedule or skill change.
- Retrospectives must compare the realised result with both the short-window signal and the longer baseline, then identify whether the miss came from overreacting to recent form, underreacting to a real change, or using the wrong baseline.

2026-06-23 GENERAL SETTLEMENT RULE - HALF-POINT RESULT FRAGILITY:
- A pick that wins by only 0.5 at the supplied threshold is a correct settlement but weak calibration evidence. Log it as a win and separately tag the projection as fragile/near-line; do not convert it into a broad rule without supporting repeat evidence.
- When both a side and total win by 0.5 in the same game, review the realised phase path before claiming the model was strongly right. Galil 89-84 produced a five-point cover at -4.5 and a 173-point under at 173.5, but its 90-point first half contradicted the slow-start premise.

2026-06-23 SOCCER / WORLD CUP PHASE-FULL REPEAT - ARGENTINA 2, AUSTRIA 0:
- Argentina-Austria confirms that 1H Over 0.5 and full Under 2.5 can be the strongest paired outcomes when the favourite owns both early scoring routes and match-control/clean-sheet quality.
- Do not automatically stack full Over 2.5 behind an early-goal lean. If the opponent creates little on target and the favourite can protect possession/territory, the second goal may arrive late without producing a third.

2026-06-23 BASEBALL / MLB ROOKIE-SAMPLE AND LATE-BULLPEN CORRECTION - COLORADO 3, BOSTON 2:
- Jake Bennett entered with only 13 strikeouts in 20.2 MLB innings, then struck out nine over six scoreless innings at Coors. Four-start MLB samples are too small to hard-cap a young pitcher's strikeout ceiling. Compare minor-league/career pitch traits, swinging-strike indicators and opponent matchup before treating low MLB K rate as stable.
- Separate starter projection from exact-winner projection. Boston's starter and defence controlled the game for eight innings, but Aroldis Chapman allowed three runs without recording an out in the ninth. A strong starter/under read can coexist with a wrong favourite ML when the underdog +1.5 protects one-inning bullpen volatility.
- Coors does not require a high-scoring game: strong starter execution can suppress eight innings, while altitude/contact can still amplify one decisive late cluster. Model inning-cluster volatility separately from total-run expectation.

2026-06-23 BASKETBALL / WNBA ROLE-CONCENTRATION CORRECTION - DALLAS 112, SEATTLE 110 OT:
- A low first half does not validate a full-game under. Dallas-Seattle had 82 at halftime but reached 192 in regulation and 222 after overtime. Recalculate the full-game projection from halftime rotations, shooting quality, foul environment and available high-usage scorers; do not carry the pregame under forward mechanically.
- When both teams lose major defenders, net the absences symmetrically. Seattle missing Magbegor raised Dallas paint efficiency; Dallas missing Alanna Smith and Alysha Clark raised Seattle frontcourt, free-throw and three-point efficiency. Counting Seattle's defensive loss but Dallas's offensive losses created a one-sided availability verdict.
- Team L10/L15 averages can be stale after role concentration. Compare them with available-player L5/L10 deltas: Hiedeman and Malonga were already above longer baselines before producing 21 and 37, while Dallas retained Bueckers/Fudd/Shepard creation. If healthy players are absorbing vacated usage, lift the full-game ceiling even when team-level longer windows are low.
- Direct H2H must pass an availability/role compatibility test. The prior 79-56 Dallas win was not a sufficient anchor once the rematch removed key defenders and redistributed minutes and usage.
- Overtime was not the cause of the over; regulation total was already 192. Retrospectives must separate regulation model failure from OT tail amplification.

2026-06-23 BASKETBALL / WNBA PREGAME APPLICATION - DALLAS AT SEATTLE:
- When a direct current-season H2H is dramatically below every threshold (26 Q1, 61 halftime, 135 final), use it as a matchup anchor but stress-test current availability before treating it as deterministic.
- Missing rim protection can raise the opponent's scoring ceiling without automatically lifting the full game over. Seattle losing Ezi Magbegor materially improves Dallas interior efficiency, but Seattle's own depleted frontcourt/secondary creation and 73.6 L10 scoring average still support the high full-game under.
- On exact-complement phase cards, use separate phase projections. Dallas-Seattle's Q1 evidence was near 41.5 and therefore only a modest under, while the 170.5 full-game under had greater separation from the L10/L15 Seattle total environment. Do not assign equal confidence to Q1, half and full unders merely because they point in the same direction.
- Available-player rolling production must be netted by role: Dallas retained Bueckers, Fudd, Ogunbowale and Shepard despite four outs; Seattle's three outs removed its best rim protector plus wing/guard depth. Count the quality and function of the missing players, not the raw number of absences.

2026-06-24 CRICKET / WT20I SETTLEMENT SWEEP - IRELAND 130/5 VS SRI LANKA, AUSTRALIA 199/7 VS PAKISTAN:
- Ireland-Sri Lanka reinforced the wicket-gated under rule: Ireland were 30/3 after six and 130/5 after 20. When a batting side with limited lower-order acceleration loses three top-order wickets inside the powerplay, short square boundaries and one senior batter's recovery innings are not enough to treat 40+ powerplay or 134+ innings paths as likely.
- Australia-Pakistan exposed the opposite elite-ceiling problem. Australia lost Mooney first ball but still reached 64/1 in the powerplay and 199/7 because Perry/Voll rebuilt at boundary pace, the middle/death order kept hitting, and Pakistan leaked 21 extras. Do not assume one early wicket kills an Australia/India/England women innings over; require sustained dot pressure, two or more early wickets, or a confirmed difficult surface.
- India U19 Women-Sri Lanka U19 Women, added 2026-06-24: U19 women's innings totals need a stricter rotation/core-batter check than senior WT20I cards. India followed 170/6 in the opener with only 134/6 and a 38-run powerplay after Nidhi Mahto moved to the bench and the scoring profile slowed. - At 190s WT20I lines for Australia women, do not rank both powerplay under and full-innings under above their complements when toss intent, batting-first venue profile, recent 200+ ceiling, tournament powerplay rate and deep middle/death batting all point to a plausible 195-205 path. 2026-06-24 SOCCER / THIN LOWER-LEAGUE TOTALS - BUENOS AIRES CITY 1, CONTROL ORIENTADO 2:
- Tiny-sample home under profiles are fragile when confirmed lineups, injury news and chance quality are unavailable. Buenos Aires City-Control Orientado finished 1-2 after a 0-1 halftime despite the home side's prior low-scoring trend.

2026-06-24 SOCCER / WORLD CUP FAVOURITE REBOUND TAIL - PORTUGAL 5, UZBEKISTAN 0:
- A favourite's poor opener xG or shot count should be separated into chance-creation failure versus finishing/selection failure. Portugal's opener was sterile, but confirmed attacking quality plus urgency against a debutant defence created a rebound tail that produced 3-0 by halftime and 5-0 full time.
- When full Under 2.5 and Over 2.5 are near coinflip in a World Cup group mismatch, do not put the under above the over unless the under mechanism is clearly stronger than "favourite controls game after scoring." If the same favourite also has a strong first-half goal route, a multi-goal first-half can invalidate the full-under thesis very quickly.

2026-06-24 USER DIRECTIVE - ODDS/LINES ALLOWED AS SUPPORTING CONTEXT:
- The standing default is now: betting lines, odds and market movement may be checked and referenced as supporting context unless the user explicitly says not to use them for that query.
- Odds/lines are not the primary model. They must not distract from the sports-doc research sequence: game state, ruleset, lineups, injuries/availability, player and unit quality, form, venue, weather, pitch/surface, phase splits, model output, stress tests, settlement rules and prior retrospectives.
- Use market context mainly to sanity-check probabilities, identify consensus disagreement, detect stale assumptions, and decide when to re-research. Never copy a pick from odds alone, never let price replace evidence, and do not provide staking/P&L/CLV advice unless the user explicitly asks for execution tracking.

2026-06-28 PENDING SETTLEMENT CLEANUP - WT20I CHASE CAP AND MLB RAIN/LOW-RUN LESSONS:
- England Women chased New Zealand's 164 target at 164/1 in 17.2 overs, with a 57/1 powerplay. The Under 164.5 innings leg won because the chase stopped below 164.5, but the PP Under 52.5 lost. Chase-cap logic controls final innings-total markets, not the first-six market; if an elite batting side can attack a good surface, keep PP over/under complements close even when the full chase under is structurally strong.
- For chase totals above the target, explicitly write the overshoot condition: the over can only win via a boundary/six carrying the final score above the target or unusual house grading. If the chase ends exactly on or below target, the under wins regardless of how fast the batting side scored.
- Cincinnati-Pittsburgh finished 9-7 after a rain-delayed start, four homers, 21 hits, seven walks and a three-run Reds ninth. A rain delay, overcast sky or light wind-in note is not a standalone MLB under anchor. After a delay, re-check actual first-pitch weather, starter readiness, command, HR/traffic risk, bullpen exposure and lineup power before ranking an under above a live over tail.
- Kansas City-Chicago finished 2-1. A blowout H2H or hot recent scoring sample should not erase the MLB one-run distribution when the fresh-game starters can restore run suppression. In low-run games both +1.5 sides can win; exact-winner confidence and total confidence must be modelled separately.
- Tampa Bay-Arizona finished 4-2. A hot favourite top order can support favourite +1.5/ML without requiring a full-game over when rolling totals, dome/no-weather context and contained opener/bulk risk point under. Do not force an opener/bulk uncertainty into an over unless the opponent is also likely to contribute 3-4 runs.
- Belgium-New Zealand finished 5-1 after Belgium led only 1-0 at halftime. In must-win final group matches, a favourite with elite attackers, goal-difference incentive and an opponent forced to chase can carry a 5+ goal tail even if the first half is controlled. Team-total unders and favourite/draw structures can still be right while a broad Under 4.5 is under-ranked; do not bury the blowout/consolation path without a strong defensive-control mechanism.
- 2026-07-06 MLB cleanup: Pittsburgh-Washington finished Pirates 11, Nationals 5 after the model ranked Pirates +1.5 first but buried Over 9.5 fourth. The side cushion was right, yet the total order repeated the MLB bottom-slot leak: both teams' recent total ladders were already near or above the line, the weather was warm, Chandler carried walk risk, and both bullpens had traffic/fatigue paths. If both recent total ladders and both pitching staffs create ordinary 10+ run paths, do not rank a modest over last from a narrow 5-4 projection alone; keep it adjacent to or above the under unless a concrete suppression source exists.
- 2026-07-06 MLB cleanup: Houston-Tampa Bay finished Astros 2, Rays 0 after the model ranked Rays +1.5 first and Under 9.0 third. The under was the cleanest expression: roof closed, no wind, Lambert competence, and Tampa's staff all pointed to run suppression, while Englert's uncertain role did not become bullpen exposure because he worked 5.2 strong innings. Pitcher role uncertainty is an over input only when it creates sourced workload limits, command risk, or weak-bullpen exposure; in a roof/no-wind setting with two credible suppression paths, a whole-number under can outrank broad runline cushions.

2026-06-30 SOCCER WORLD CUP KNOCKOUT / CORNERS CLEANUP - BRAZIL 2 JAPAN 1, CORNERS 8:
- Brazil-Japan confirmed the value of broad knockout safety legs: Japan TT Under 2.5, Brazil TT Over 0.5 and Under 4.5 all won in a 2-1. A favourite can advance and score without turning the match into a blowout.

2026-07-13 AFL LIVE BLOWOUT TOTAL CORRECTION - BRISBANE 149 ESSENDON 59:
- At halftime Brisbane led 64-12 with a 37-19 inside-50 advantage. The final total reached 208 after a 132-point second half and an 83-point fourth quarter. Under 180.5 lost while Over 160.5 won.
- A 50+ halftime lead with extreme territory dominance does more than keep an over tail alive: it widens the remaining-score distribution enough that a high under probability requires a sourced slowdown mechanism. Do not top-rank the under from arithmetic cushion alone.
- Model the favourite-continuation and trailing-side-rebound paths jointly, not as mutually exclusive scenarios. Brisbane kept attacking for percentage while Essendon rose from 15 points through three quarters to 44 in Q4; both written under-failure mechanisms occurred in the same game.

If those cannot support the confidence, cap the under and elevate/pass the over rather than merely mentioning its tail.

2026-07-13 MLB DOUBLEHEADER / REPLACEMENT-STARTER EARLY-BRIDGE CORRECTION - PITTSBURGH 14 MILWAUKEE 5:

2026-07-13 WNBA LIVE Q1-TO-1H PROPAGATION CORRECTION - WASHINGTON 84 SEATTLE 79:
- At the final research cutoff Seattle led 10-4 with 7:26 left in Q1. Q1 finished 42, halftime reached 85 and the final reached 163. Q1 Over 41 and 1H Over 83.5 won; 1H Under lost by 1.5 and full Under lost by 0.5.

Seattle entered with four of its last five first halves Over 83.5; that evidence should have moved 1H Over above Under once the opening scoring arrived, even though Washington's own recent first halves were low.

Here Q3 rose to 47 and Q4 fell to 31; the full Under missed by only 0.5, so treat it as a narrow projection miss, not proof that every fast Q1 requires a full-game Over.
